    39	pub struct VmState<'vm> {
    40	    pub store: &'vm mut PrunedStore,
    41	    pub stack: &'vm mut Stack,
    42	    pub code: &'vm CodeMap,
    43	    done_reason: Option<DoneReason>,
    44	}
    45	
    46	impl<'vm> VmState<'vm> {
    47	    pub fn new(store: &'vm mut PrunedStore, stack: &'vm mut Stack, code: &'vm CodeMap) -> Self {
    48	        Self {
    49	            store,
    50	            stack,
    51	            code,
    52	            done_reason: None,
    53	        }
    54	    }
    55	
    56	    pub fn done_with(&mut self, reason: impl FnOnce() -> DoneReason) {
    57	        #[cold]
    58	        #[inline(never)]
    59	        fn err(prev: &DoneReason, reason: impl FnOnce() -> DoneReason) -> ! {
    60	            panic!(
    61	                "\
    62	                tried to done with reason while reason already exists:\n\
    63	                \t- new reason: {:?},\n\
    64	                \t- old reason: {:?},\
    65	                ",
    66	                reason(),
    67	                prev,
    68	            )
    69	        }
    70	
    71	        if let Some(prev) = &self.done_reason {
    72	            err(prev, reason)
    73	        }
    74	        self.done_reason = Some(reason());
    75	    }
    76	
    77	    pub fn take_done_reason(&mut self) -> DoneReason {
    78	        let Some(reason) = self.done_reason.take() else {
    79	            panic!("missing break reason")
    80	        };
    81	        reason
    82	    }
    83	
    84	    pub fn execution_outcome(&mut self) -> Result<Sp, ExecutionOutcome> {
    85	        self.take_done_reason().into_execution_outcome()
    86	    }
    87	}
    88	
    89	/// The reason why a Wasmi execution has halted.
    90	///
    91	/// # Note
    92	///
    93	/// This type lives in the [`VmState`] type and in case of a halt needs to be
    94	/// updated manually which is a bit costly which is why the most common reason
    95	/// which is a raised [`TrapCode`] is not included in this `enum` and was put
    96	/// into the return type of execution handlers directly, instead.
    97	#[derive(Debug)]
    98	pub enum DoneReason {
    99	    /// The execution finished successfully with a result found at the [`Sp`].
   100	    Return(Sp),
   101	    /// A resumable error indicating an error returned by a called host function.
   102	    Host(ResumableHostTrapError),
   103	    /// A resumable error indicating that the execution ran out of fuel.
   104	    OutOfFuel(ResumableOutOfFuelError),
   105	    /// A non-resumable error.
   106	    Error(Error),
   107	}
   108	
   109	impl DoneReason {
   110	    /// The execution halted due to a generic [`Error`].
   111	    #[cold]
   112	    #[inline]
   113	    pub fn error(error: Error) -> Self {
   114	        Self::Error(error)
   115	    }
   116	
   117	    /// The executed halted because a called host function yielded an error.
   118	    ///
   119	    /// # Note
   120	    ///
   121	    /// This needs special treatment due to resumable function calls.
   122	    #[cold]
   123	    #[inline]
   124	    pub fn host_error(error: Error, func: Func, results: SlotSpan) -> Self {
   125	        Self::Host(ResumableHostTrapError::new(error, func, results))
   126	    }
   127	
   128	    /// The executed halted because the execution ran out of fuel.
   129	    ///
   130	    /// # Note
   131	    ///
   132	    /// This needs special treatment due to resumable function calls.
   133	    #[cold]
   134	    #[inline]
   135	    pub fn out_of_fuel(required_fuel: u64) -> Self {
   136	        Self::OutOfFuel(ResumableOutOfFuelError::new(required_fuel))
   137	    }
   138	
   139	    /// Converts `self` into an [`ExecutionOutcome`].
   140	    #[inline]
   141	    pub fn into_execution_outcome(self) -> Result<Sp, ExecutionOutcome> {
   142	        let outcome = match self {
   143	            DoneReason::Return(sp) => return Ok(sp),
   144	            DoneReason::Host(error) => error.into(),
   145	            DoneReason::OutOfFuel(error) => error.into(),
   146	            DoneReason::Error(error) => error.into(),
   147	        };
   148	        Err(outcome)
   149	    }
   150	}
   151	
   152	/// A thin-wrapper around a non-owned [`InstanceEntity`].
   153	#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
   154	#[repr(transparent)]
   155	pub struct Inst {
   156	    /// The underlying reference to the [`InstanceEntity`].
   157	    value: NonNull<InstanceEntity>,
   158	    /// Indicates to the compiler that this type is similar in behavior as
   159	    /// a non-owning, non-lifetime restricted `*const InstanceEntity` type.
   160	    marker: PhantomData<*const InstanceEntity>,
   161	}
   162	
   163	impl From<&'_ InstanceEntity> for Inst {
   164	    fn from(entity: &'_ InstanceEntity) -> Self {
   165	        Self {
   166	            value: entity.into(),
   167	            marker: PhantomData,
   168	        }
   169	    }
   170	}
   171	
   172	impl PartialEq for Inst {
   173	    fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
   174	        self.value == other.value
   175	    }
   176	}
   177	impl Eq for Inst {}
   178	
   179	impl Inst {
   180	    /// Returns a shared reference to the referenced [`InstanceEntity`].
   181	    ///
   182	    /// # Safety
   183	    ///
   184	    /// The caller must ensure that:
   185	    ///
   186	    /// - The [`Inst`] was constructed from a valid, properly aligned
   187	    ///   `InstanceEntity` pointer.
   188	    /// - The referenced [`InstanceEntity`] remains alive and is not
   189	    ///   mutably accessed for the entire duration of the returned
   190	    ///   reference.
   191	    pub unsafe fn as_ref(&self) -> &InstanceEntity {
   192	        unsafe { self.value.as_ref() }
   193	    }
   194	}
   195	
   196	/// # Safety
   197	///
   198	/// It is safe to send `Inst` to another thread because:
   199	/// - The `InstanceEntity` behind the pointer is itself `Send`.
   200	/// - `Inst` only allows shared (`&`) access to the `InstanceEntity` through its API.
   201	/// - There is no interior mutability that could cause data races.
   202	unsafe impl Send for Inst {}
   203	
   204	/// # Safety
   205	///
   206	/// It is safe to share `&Inst` across threads because:
   207	/// - All access to the `InstanceEntity` through `Inst` is immutable.
   208	/// - `InstanceEntity` is `Sync`.
   209	/// - The pointer will not be mutated, preventing data races.
   210	unsafe impl Sync for Inst {}
   211	
   212	mod inst_tests {
   213	    // Note: the `Send` and `Sync` impl for `Inst` is only valid if
   214	    //       `InstanceEntity` is `Send` and `Sync`.
   215	    //
   216	    // Below are compile-time tests, thus they are not just run with
   217	    // `cargo test` but with any compilation of the `wasmi` crate.
   218	    // Compilation would fail if `InstanceEntity` no longer implements
   219	    // `Send` or `Sync`.
   220	    use super::*;
   221	
   222	    const _: fn() = || {
   223	        fn assert_send<T: Send>() {}
   224	        fn assert_sync<T: Sync>() {}
   225	
   226	        assert_send::<InstanceEntity>();
   227	        assert_sync::<InstanceEntity>();
   228	        assert_send::<Inst>();
   229	        assert_sync::<Inst>();
   230	    };
   231	}

   233	/// The data pointer to the default Wasm linear memory at index 0.
   234	#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
   235	#[repr(transparent)]
   236	pub struct Mem0Ptr(*mut u8);
   237	
   238	impl From<*mut u8> for Mem0Ptr {
   239	    fn from(value: *mut u8) -> Self {
   240	        Self(value)
   241	    }
   242	}
   243	
   244	/// The length in bytes of the default Wasm linear memory at index 0.
   245	#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
   246	#[repr(transparent)]
   247	pub struct Mem0Len(usize);
   248	
   249	impl From<usize> for Mem0Len {
   250	    fn from(value: usize) -> Self {
   251	        Self(value)
   252	    }
   253	}
   254	
   255	/// Construct the default linear memory slice of bytes from its raw parts.
   256	pub fn mem0_bytes<'a>(mem0: Mem0Ptr, mem0_len: Mem0Len) -> &'a mut [u8] {
   257	    unsafe { slice::from_raw_parts_mut(mem0.0, mem0_len.0) }
   258	}

   260	/// The instruction pointer.
   261	///
   262	/// This always points to the currently executed instruction (or operator).
   263	///
   264	/// # Note
   265	///
   266	/// The pointer points to a `u8` since [`Op`](crate::ir::Op)s in Wasmi are
   267	/// encoded and need to be decoded prior to execution.
   268	#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
   269	#[repr(transparent)]
   270	pub struct Ip {
   271	    value: *const u8,
   272	}
   273	
   274	impl<'a> From<&'a [u8]> for Ip {
   275	    fn from(ops: &'a [u8]) -> Self {
   276	        Self {
   277	            value: ops.as_ptr(),
   278	        }
   279	    }
   280	}
   281	
   282	impl Ip {
   283	    /// Decodes a value of type `T` from the instruction stream at the [`Ip`].
   284	    ///
   285	    /// # Returns
   286	    ///
   287	    /// - This returns the advanced [`Ip`] together with the decoded value of type `T`.
   288	    /// - The returned [`Ip`] points to the first byte immediately following the decoded value.
   289	    ///
   290	    /// # Safety
   291	    ///
   292	    /// The caller must guarantee that [`Ip`] points to the start of a valid
   293	    /// encoding of `T` and that the underlying instruction sequence remains
   294	    /// readable for the full duration of the decode, including any bytes consumed
   295	    /// by `T`.
   296	    ///
   297	    /// The behavior of this operation is undefined if:
   298	    ///
   299	    /// - The instruction sequence does not contain a valid encoding of `T` at [`Ip`].
   300	    /// - Decoding `T` would read past the end of the instruction sequence.
   301	    /// - The underlying memory is invalid, or no longer alive while decoding.
   302	    #[inline]
   303	    pub unsafe fn decode<T: ir::Decode>(self) -> (Ip, T) {
   304	        struct IpDecoder(Ip);
   305	        impl ir::Decoder for IpDecoder {
   306	            #[inline(always)]
   307	            fn read_bytes(&mut self, buffer: &mut [u8]) -> Result<(), ir::DecodeError> {
   308	                let src = self.0.value;
   309	                let dst = buffer.as_mut_ptr();
   310	                let len = buffer.len();
   311	                unsafe { ptr::copy_nonoverlapping(src, dst, len) };
   312	                self.0 = unsafe { self.0.add(len) };
   313	                Ok(())
   314	            }
   315	        }
   316	
   317	        let mut ip = IpDecoder(self);
   318	        let decoded = match <T as ir::Decode>::decode(&mut ip) {
   319	            Ok(decoded) => decoded,
   320	            Err(error) => unsafe {
   321	                crate::engine::utils::unreachable_unchecked!(
   322	                    "failed to decode `OpCode` or op-handler: {error}"
   323	                )
   324	            },
   325	        };
   326	        (ip.0, decoded)
   327	    }
   328	
   329	    /// Advances [`Ip`] past a value of type `T` without decoding it.
   330	    ///
   331	    /// # Note
   332	    ///
   333	    /// This is equivalent to calling [`Self::decode`] and discarding the decoded value,
   334	    /// and may be used when the value is not needed.
   335	    ///
   336	    /// # Safety
   337	    ///
   338	    /// The caller must ensure that offsetting [`Ip`] by `delta` bytes does
   339	    /// not move it outside the valid bounds of the instruction sequence
   340	    /// and that any subsequent use of the returned [`Ip`] only reads from valid,
   341	    /// alive memory.
   342	    #[inline]
   343	    pub unsafe fn skip<T: ir::Decode>(self) -> Ip {
   344	        let (ip, _) = unsafe { self.decode::<T>() };
   345	        ip
   346	    }
   347	
   348	    /// Returns a new [`Ip`] offset by `delta` bytes from this one.
   349	    ///
   350	    /// # Note
   351	    ///
   352	    /// - This method performs no bounds checking.
   353	    /// - A positive `delta` moves the pointer forward, a negative `delta` moves it backward.
   354	    ///
   355	    /// # Safety
   356	    ///
   357	    /// The caller must ensure that offsetting [`Ip`] by `delta` bytes does
   358	    /// not move it outside the valid bounds of the instruction sequence
   359	    /// and that any subsequent use of the returned [`Ip`] only reads from valid,
   360	    /// alive memory.
   361	    #[inline]
   362	    pub unsafe fn offset(self, delta: isize) -> Self {
   363	        let value = unsafe { self.value.byte_offset(delta) };
   364	        Self { value }
   365	    }

   386	/// # Safety
   387	///
   388	/// [`Ip`] (instruction pointer) is a new-type thin wrapper to `*const u8`.
   389	///
   390	/// Moving the pointer to another thread does not by itself create aliasing or
   391	/// data races. All methods that dereference or advance the pointer are marked as
   392	/// `unsafe` and require the caller to guarantee that the underlying instruction
   393	/// sequence remains valid for the duration of use, including across threads.
   394	///
   395	/// # Note
   396	///
   397	/// [`Ip`] is not [`Sync`] because concurrent access to the same [`Ip`] value
   398	/// could lead to unsynchronized mutation of the instructions.
   399	unsafe impl Send for Ip {}

   407	    };

   410	        // Note: this module contains type defs to assert that `Ip` is not `Sync`.

   412	        trait AmbiguousIfSync<A> {
   413	            fn some_item() {}
   414	        }
   415	        impl<T: ?Sized> AmbiguousIfSync<()> for T {}
   416	        // Specialized impl that only exists for `Sync` types.
   417	        struct Invalid;
   418	        impl<T: ?Sized + Sync> AmbiguousIfSync<Invalid> for T {}
   419	
   420	        // This becomes ambiguous *iff* `Ip: Sync`.
   421	        let _ = <Ip as AmbiguousIfSync<_>>::some_item;
   422	    };

   659	/// The value stack.
   660	///
   661	/// The Wasmi value stack is organized in 64-bit cells
   662	/// where each is associated to a single function frame.
   663	///
   664	/// Cells can be read from and written to via [`Slot`]s.
   665	///
   666	/// # Note
   667	///
   668	/// - A [`ValueStack`] has a maximum height which it cannot exceed.
   669	/// - A [`ValueStack`] can only grow (via [`ValueStack::grow_if_needed`]) and never shrink.
   670	#[derive(Debug)]
   671	pub struct ValueStack {
   672	    /// The cells of the value stack.
   673	    cells: Vec<Cell>,
   674	    /// The maximum height of the value stack.
   675	    max_height: usize,
   676	}
   677	
   678	impl ValueStack {
   679	    /// Create a new [`ValueStack`] with the minimum and maximum height limits.
   680	    fn new(min_height: usize, max_height: usize) -> Self {
   681	        debug_assert!(min_height <= max_height);
   682	        // We need to convert from `size_of<Cell>`` to `size_of<u8>`:
   683	        let sizeof_cell = mem::size_of::<Cell>();
   684	        let min_height = min_height / sizeof_cell;
   685	        let max_height = max_height / sizeof_cell;
   686	        let cells = Vec::with_capacity(min_height);
   687	        Self { cells, max_height }
   688	    }
   689	
   690	    /// Create an empty [`ValueStack`] which uses no heap allocations.
   691	    fn empty() -> Self {
   692	        Self {
   693	            cells: Vec::new(),
   694	            max_height: 0,
   695	        }
   696	    }
   697	
   698	    /// Reset `self` for reuse.
   699	    fn reset(&mut self) {
   700	        self.cells.clear();
   701	    }
   702	
   703	    /// Returns the number of heap allocated bytes of `self`.
   704	    ///
   705	    /// # Note
   706	    ///
   707	    /// This is mostly used to separate instances with and without heap allocations for caching.
   708	    fn bytes_allocated(&self) -> usize {
   709	        let bytes_per_frame = mem::size_of::<Cell>();
   710	        self.cells.capacity() * bytes_per_frame
   711	    }
   712	
   713	    /// Returns an [`Sp`] pointing to the cell at the `start` index.
   714	    fn sp(&mut self, start: SpOffset) -> Sp {
   715	        let offset = start.into_inner();
   716	        debug_assert!(
   717	            // Note: it is fine to use <= here because for zero sized frames
   718	            //       we sometimes end up with `start == cells.len()` which isn't
   719	            //       bad since in those cases `Sp` is never used.
   720	            offset <= self.cells.len(),
   721	            "start = {}, cells.len() = {}",
   722	            offset,
   723	            self.cells.len()
   724	        );
   725	        let value = unsafe { self.cells.as_mut_ptr().add(offset) };
   726	        Sp::new(value)
   727	    }
   728	
   729	    /// Returns an [`Sp`] pointing to the cell at the `start` index if `self` is non-empty.
   730	    ///
   731	    /// Otherwise returns a dangling [`Sp`] that must not be dereferenced.
   732	    fn sp_or_dangling(&mut self, start: SpOffset) -> Sp {
   733	        match self.cells.is_empty() {
   734	            true => {
   735	                debug_assert_eq!(start.into_inner(), 0);
   736	                Sp::dangling()
   737	            }
   738	            false => self.sp(start),
   739	        }
   740	    }
   741	
   742	    /// Grows the number of cells to `new_len` if the current number is less than `new_len`.
   743	    ///
   744	    /// Does nothing if the number of cells is already at least `new_len`.
   745	    ///
   746	    /// # Errors
   747	    ///
   748	    /// - Returns [`TrapCode::OutOfSystemMemory`] if the machine ran out of memory.
   749	    /// - Returns [`TrapCode::StackOverflow`] if this exceeds the stack's predefined limits.
   750	    fn grow_if_needed(&mut self, new_len: SpOffset) -> Result<(), TrapCode> {
   751	        let new_len = new_len.into_inner();
   752	        if new_len > self.max_height {
   753	            return Err(TrapCode::StackOverflow);
   754	        }
   755	        let capacity = self.cells.capacity();
   756	        let len = self.cells.len();
   757	        if new_len > capacity {
   758	            debug_assert!(
   759	                self.cells.len() <= self.cells.capacity(),
   760	                "capacity must always be larger or equal to the actual number of the cells"

   678	impl ValueStack {
   679	    /// Create a new [`ValueStack`] with the minimum and maximum height limits.
   680	    fn new(min_height: usize, max_height: usize) -> Self {
   681	        debug_assert!(min_height <= max_height);
   682	        // We need to convert from `size_of<Cell>`` to `size_of<u8>`:
   683	        let sizeof_cell = mem::size_of::<Cell>();
   684	        let min_height = min_height / sizeof_cell;
   685	        let max_height = max_height / sizeof_cell;
   686	        let cells = Vec::with_capacity(min_height);
   687	        Self { cells, max_height }
   688	    }
   689	
   690	    /// Create an empty [`ValueStack`] which uses no heap allocations.
   691	    fn empty() -> Self {
   692	        Self {
   693	            cells: Vec::new(),
   694	            max_height: 0,
   695	        }
   696	    }
   697	
   698	    /// Reset `self` for reuse.
   699	    fn reset(&mut self) {
   700	        self.cells.clear();
   701	    }
   702	
   703	    /// Returns the number of heap allocated bytes of `self`.
   704	    ///
   705	    /// # Note
   706	    ///
   707	    /// This is mostly used to separate instances with and without heap allocations for caching.
   708	    fn bytes_allocated(&self) -> usize {
   709	        let bytes_per_frame = mem::size_of::<Cell>();
   710	        self.cells.capacity() * bytes_per_frame
   711	    }
   712	
   713	    /// Returns an [`Sp`] pointing to the cell at the `start` index.
   714	    fn sp(&mut self, start: SpOffset) -> Sp {
   715	        let offset = start.into_inner();
   716	        debug_assert!(
   717	            // Note: it is fine to use <= here because for zero sized frames
   718	            //       we sometimes end up with `start == cells.len()` which isn't
   719	            //       bad since in those cases `Sp` is never used.
   720	            offset <= self.cells.len(),
   721	            "start = {}, cells.len() = {}",
   722	            offset,
   723	            self.cells.len()
   724	        );
   725	        let value = unsafe { self.cells.as_mut_ptr().add(offset) };
   726	        Sp::new(value)
   727	    }
   728	
   729	    /// Returns an [`Sp`] pointing to the cell at the `start` index if `self` is non-empty.
   730	    ///
   731	    /// Otherwise returns a dangling [`Sp`] that must not be dereferenced.
   732	    fn sp_or_dangling(&mut self, start: SpOffset) -> Sp {
   733	        match self.cells.is_empty() {
   734	            true => {
   735	                debug_assert_eq!(start.into_inner(), 0);
   736	                Sp::dangling()
   737	            }
   738	            false => self.sp(start),
   739	        }
   740	    }
   741	
   742	    /// Grows the number of cells to `new_len` if the 
[OUTPUT_TAIL]
     let callee_size = params_len.max(results_len);
   800	        if callee_size == 0 {
   801	            let sp = match caller {
   802	                Some(_) if caller_start != callee_start => self.sp(caller_start),
   803	                _ => Sp::dangling(),
   804	            };
   805	            let inout = InOutParams::new(&mut [], 0, 0).unwrap();
   806	            let control = match caller {
   807	                Some((ip, _, instance)) => ReturnCallHost::Continue((ip, sp, instance)),
   808	                None => ReturnCallHost::Break(sp),
   809	            };
   810	            return Ok((control, inout));
   811	        }
   812	        let params_start = callee_start.add(params_offset)?;
   813	        let params_end = params_start.add(params_len)?;
   814	        self.cells_copy_within(params_start..params_end, callee_start);
   815	        let callee_end = callee_start.add(callee_size)?;
   816	        self.grow_if_needed(callee_end)?;
   817	        let caller_sp = self.sp(caller_start);
   818	        let Some(cells) = self.cells_from_to(callee_start, callee_end) else {
   819	            unsafe { unreachable_unchecked!("must fit slice after `grow_if_needed` operation") }
   820	        };
   821	        let Ok(inout) = InOutParams::new(cells, params_len, results_len) else {
   822	            panic!("todo")
   823	        };
   824	        let control = match caller {
   825	            Some((ip, _, instance)) => ReturnCallHost::Continue((ip, caller_sp, instance)),
   826	            None => ReturnCallHost::Break(caller_sp),
   827	        };
   828	        Ok((control, inout))
   829	    }
   830	
   831	    /// Prepares `self` for a host function call.
   832	    fn prepare_host_frame<'a>(
   833	        &'a mut self,
   834	        caller_start: SpOffset,
   835	        callee_params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   836	        results_len: u16,
   837	    ) -> Result<(Sp, InOutParams<'a>), TrapCode> {
   838	        let params_offset = usize::from(u16::from(callee_params.span().head()));
   839	        let params_len = usize::from(callee_params.len());
   840	        let results_len = usize::from(results_len);
   841	        let callee_size = params_len.max(results_len);
   842	        let callee_start = caller_start.add(params_offset)?;
   843	        let callee_end = callee_start.add(callee_size)?;
   844	        self.grow_if_needed(callee_end)?;
   845	        let sp = self.sp(caller_start);
   846	        let Some(cells) = self.cells_from_to(callee_start, callee_end) else {
   847	            unsafe { unreachable_unchecked!("must fit slice after `grow_if_needed` operation") }
   848	        };
   849	        let Ok(inout) = InOutParams::new(cells, params_len, results_len) else {
   850	            panic!("todo")
   851	        };
   852	        Ok((sp, inout))
   853	    }
   854	
   855	    /// Adjusts `self` for a normal function call.
   856	    #[inline(always)]
   857	    fn push(&mut self, start: SpOffset, len_slots: usize, len_params: u16) -> Result<Sp, TrapCode> {
   858	        let len_params = usize::from(len_params);
   859	        debug_assert!(len_params <= len_slots);
   860	        if len_slots == 0 {
   861	            return Ok(Sp::dangling());
   862	        }
   863	        let end = start.add(len_slots)?;
   864	        self.grow_if_needed(end)?;
   865	        let start_locals = start.into_inner().wrapping_add(len_params);
   866	        self.cells[start_locals..end.into_inner()].fill_with(Cell::default);
   867	        let sp = self.sp(start);
   868	        Ok(sp)
   869	    }
   870	
   871	    /// Adjusts `self` for a function tail call.
   872	    #[inline(always)]
   873	    fn replace(
   874	        &mut self,
   875	        callee_start: SpOffset,
   876	        callee_size: usize,
   877	        callee_params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   878	    ) -> Result<Sp, TrapCode> {
   879	        let params_len = usize::from(callee_params.len());
   880	        let params_start = usize::from(u16::from(callee_params.span().head()));
   881	        let params_end = params_start.wrapping_add(params_len);
   882	        if callee_size == 0 {
   883	            return Ok(Sp::dangling());
   884	        }
   885	        let callee_end = callee_start.add(callee_size)?;
   886	        self.grow_if_needed(callee_end)?;
   887	        let Some(callee_cells) = self.cells_from(callee_start) else {
   888	            unsafe { unreachable_unchecked!("ValueStack::replace: out of bounds callee cells") }
   889	        };
   890	        callee_cells.copy_within(params_start..params_end, 0);
   891	        callee_cells[params_len..callee_size].fill_with(Cell::default);
   892	        let sp = self.sp(callee_start);
   893	        Ok(sp)
   894	    }
   895	
   896	    /// Returns cells as slice: `cells[start..]`
   897	    fn cells_from(&mut self, start: SpOffset) -> Option<&mut [Cell]> {
   898	        let start = start.into_inner();
   899	        self.cells.get_mut(start..)
   900	    }

END STEP

/// A single 64-bit cell of the value stack.
///
/// This stores values on the value stack without type or store association.
/// For values of type [`V128`] two consecutive 64-bit [`Cell`]s are used.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Copy, Clone)]
#[repr(transparent)]
pub struct Cell(u64);

macro_rules! iN_to_u64 {
    ($ty:ty, $unsigned:ty) => {
        |value: $ty| -> u64 { u64::from(value as $unsigned) }
    };
}

macro_rules! impl_from_for_cell {
    ( $($ty:ty = $eval:expr),* $(,)? ) => {
        $(
            impl From<$ty> for Cell {
                #[allow(clippy::cast_lossless)]
                fn from(value: $ty) -> Self {
                    Self($eval(value) as u64)
                }
            }
        )*
    };
}
impl_from_for_cell! {
    bool = u64::from,
    u8 = u64::from,
    u16 = u64::from,
    u32 = u64::from,
    u64 = identity,
    i8 = iN_to_u64!(i8, u8),
    i16 = iN_to_u64!(i16, u16),
    i32 = iN_to_u64!(i32, u32),
    i64 = iN_to_u64!(i64, u64),
    f32 = |v| u64::from(f32::to_bits(v)),
    F32 = |v| u64::from(F32::to_bits(v)),
    f64 = f64::to_bits,
    F64 = F64::to_bits,
    RawRef = u32::from,
}

macro_rules! impl_into_for_cell {
    ( $($ty:ty = $eval:expr),* $(,)? ) => {
        $(
            impl From<Cell> for $ty {
                fn from(cell: Cell) -> Self {
                    $eval(cell.0)
                }
            }
        )*
    };
}
impl_into_for_cell! {
    bool = |v| !matches!(v, 0),
    u8 = |v| v as _,
    u16 = |v| v as _,
    u32 = |v| v as _,
    u64 = identity,
    i8 = |v| v as i64 as _,
    i16 = |v| v as i64 as _,
    i32 = |v| v as i64 as _,
    i64 = |v| v as _,
    f32 = |v| f32::from_bits(v as _),
    F32 = |v| F32::from_bits(v as _),
    f64 = f64::from_bits,
    F64 = F64::from_bits,
    RawRef = |v| RawRef::from(v as u32),
}

/// Errors raised in the encode and decode APIs of [`Cell`].
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
pub enum CellError {
    /// Raised when there are not enough [`Cell`]s for the given amount of values.
    NotEnoughCells,
    /// Raised when there are not enough values for the given amount of [`Cell`]s.
    NotEnoughValues,
    /// Raised when a lowered value originates from a different [`Store`](crate::Store).
    StoreOwnerMismatch,
}

impl fmt::Display for CellError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        let s = match self {
            Self::NotEnoughCells => "not enough cells",
            Self::NotEnoughValues => "not enough values",
            Self::StoreOwnerMismatch => "value originates from different store",
        };
        f.write_str(s)
    }
}

/// Trait implemented by types that can be encoded onto a slice of [`Cell`]s.
pub trait StoreToCells {
    /// Encodes `self` to `cells`.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the number of [`Cell`]s that `value` requires exceeds `cells.len()`.
    fn store_to_cells(self, cells: &mut impl CellsWriter) -> Result<(), CellError>;
}

/// Trait implemented by types that can be stored onto a slice of [`Cell`]s.
///
/// # Note
///
/// In contrast to [`StoreToCells`] this also disassociates the stored values
/// from their [`Store`](crate::Store).
pub trait LowerToCells {
    /// Encodes `self` to `cells`.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the number of [`Cell`]s that `value` requires exceeds `cells.len()`.
    fn lower_to_cells(
        self,
        store: impl AsStoreId,
        cells: &mut impl CellsWriter,
    ) -> Result<(), CellError>;
}

macro_rules! impl_lower_and_lift_for_refs {
    ( $($refty:ty),* $(,)? ) => {
        $(
            impl LowerToCells for $refty {
                fn lower_to_cells(
                    self,
                    store: impl AsStoreId,
                    cells: &mut impl CellsWriter,
                ) -> Result<(), CellError> {
                    let Some(value) = store.unwrap(self.as_raw()) else {
                        return Err(CellError::StoreOwnerMismatch);
                    };
                    value.raw().get().store_to_cells(cells)
                }
            }

            impl LowerToCells for Nullable<$refty> {
                fn lower_to_cells(
                    self,
                    store: impl AsStoreId,
                    cells: &mut impl CellsWriter,
                ) -> Result<(), CellError> {
                    match self {
                        Self::Null => 0_u32.lower_to_cells(store, cells),
                        Self::Val(value) => value.lower_to_cells(store, cells),
                    }
                }
            }

            impl LiftFromCellsByValue for Nullable<$refty> {
                fn lift_from_cells_by_value(
                    store: impl AsStoreId,
                    cells: &mut impl CellsReader,
           
[OUTPUT_TAIL]
omCells for &'_ mut T
where
    T: LiftFromCellsByValue,
{
    type Value = ();

    fn lift_from_cells(
        self,
        store: impl AsStoreId,
        cells: &mut impl CellsReader,
    ) -> Result<Self::Value, CellError> {
        *self = <T as LiftFromCellsByValue>::lift_from_cells_by_value(store, cells)?;
        Ok(())
    }
}

macro_rules! impl_load_from_cells_for_tuples {
    (
        $arity:literal $( $camel:ident )*
    ) => {
        impl<$($camel),*> LoadFromCellsByValue for ($($camel,)*)
        where
            $( $camel: LoadFromCellsByValue, )*
        {
            #[inline]
            fn load_from_cells_by_value(_cells: &mut impl CellsReader) -> Result<Self, CellError> {
                Ok( ($( <$camel as LoadFromCellsByValue>::load_from_cells_by_value(_cells)?, )*) )
            }
        }

        impl<$($camel),*> LiftFromCellsByValue for ($($camel,)*)
        where
            $( $camel: LiftFromCellsByValue, )*
        {
            #[inline]
            fn lift_from_cells_by_value(
                _store: impl AsStoreId,
                _cells: &mut impl CellsReader,
            ) -> Result<Self, CellError> {
                Ok( ($( <$camel as LiftFromCellsByValue>::lift_from_cells_by_value(_store, _cells)?, )*) )
            }
        }
    };
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::Store;

    #[test]
    fn tuple_works() {
        let mut cells = [Cell::default(); 7];
        assert!(matches!(
            store_and_load_tuple(&mut cells[..5]),
            Err(CellError::NotEnoughCells)
        ));
        assert!(store_and_load_tuple(&mut cells[..6]).unwrap());
        assert!(matches!(
            store_and_load_tuple(&mut cells[..7]),
            Err(CellError::NotEnoughValues)
        ));
    }

    fn store_and_load_tuple(cells: &mut [Cell]) -> Result<bool, CellError> {
        let values = (1_i32, 2_i64, 3_f32, 4_f64, V128::from(5_u128));
        values.store_to_cells(&mut &mut cells[..])?;
        let cells = &mut &cells[..];
        let expected = <_ as LoadFromCellsByValue>::load_from_cells_by_value(cells)?;
        if !cells.is_empty() {
            return Err(CellError::NotEnoughValues);
        }
        Ok(values == expected)
    }

    #[test]
    fn val_slice_works() {
        let mut cells = [Cell::default(); 7];
        assert!(matches!(
            store_and_load_val_slice(&mut cells[..5]),
            Err(CellError::NotEnoughCells)
        ));
        assert!(matches!(
            store_and_load_val_slice(&mut cells[..6]),
            Ok(true)
        ));
        assert!(matches!(
            store_and_load_val_slice(&mut cells[..7]),
            Err(CellError::NotEnoughValues)
        ));
    }

    fn store_and_load_val_slice(cells: &mut [Cell]) -> Result<bool, CellError> {
        let store = <Store<()>>::default();
        let values = [
            Val::I32(1_i32),
            Val::I64(2_i64),
            Val::F32(3_f32.into()),
            Val::F64(4_f64.into()),
            Val::V128(V128::from(5_u128)),
        ];
        let mut expected = values.clone();
        values.lower_to_cells(&store, &mut &mut cells[..])?;
        let cells = &mut &cells[..];
        expected.lift_from_cells(&store, cells)?;
        if !cells.is_empty() {
            return Err(CellError::NotEnoughValues);
        }
        let is_eq = is_val_slice_eq(&values[..], &expected[..]);
        Ok(is_eq)
    }

    /// Panics if `lhs` and `rhs` have mismatching [`Val`] items.
    fn is_val_slice_eq(lhs: &[Val], rhs: &[Val]) -> bool {
        for (value, expected) in lhs.iter().zip(rhs.iter()) {
            let is_eq = match (value, expected) {
                (Val::I32(lhs), Val::I32(rhs)) => lhs == rhs,
                (Val::I64(lhs), Val::I64(rhs)) => lhs == rhs,
                (Val::F32(lhs), Val::F32(rhs)) => lhs == rhs,
                (Val::F64(lhs), Val::F64(rhs)) => lhs == rhs,
                (Val::V128(lhs), Val::V128(rhs)) => lhs == rhs,
                _ => false,
            };
            if !is_eq {
                return false;
            }
        }
        true
    }

    #[test]
    fn v128_works() {
        let mut cells = [Cell::default(); 3];
        assert!(matches!(
            store_and_load_v128(&mut cells[..1]),
            Err(CellError::NotEnoughCells)
        ));
        assert!(store_and_load_v128(&mut cells[..2]).unwrap());
        assert!(matches!(
            store_and_load_v128(&mut cells[..3]),
            Err(CellError::NotEnoughValues)
        ));
    }

    fn store_and_load_v128(cells: &mut [Cell]) -> Result<bool, CellError> {
        let value = V128::from(42_u128);
        value.store_to_cells(&mut &mut cells[..])?;
        let cells = &mut &cells[..];
        let loaded = <V128 as LoadFromCellsByValue>::load_from_cells_by_value(cells)?;
        if !cells.is_empty() {
            return Err(CellError::NotEnoughValues);
        }
        Ok(value == loaded)
    }
}

//! Data structure storing information about compiled functions.
//!
//! # Note
//!
//! This is the data structure specialized to handle compiled
//! register machine based bytecode functions.

use super::{FuncTranslationDriver, FuncTranslator, TranslationError, ValidatingFuncTranslator};
use crate::{
    Config,
    Error,
    TrapCode,
    collections::arena::{Arena, ArenaKey},
    core::{Fuel, FuelCostsProvider},
    engine::{ResumableOutOfFuelError, utils::unreachable_unchecked},

};

/// A reference to a compiled function stored in the [`CodeMap`] of an [`Engine`](crate::Engine).
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub struct EngineFunc(u32);

impl From<EngineFunc> for InternalFunc {
    fn from(value: EngineFunc) -> Self {
        InternalFunc::from(value.0)
    }
}

impl From<InternalFunc> for EngineFunc {
    fn from(index: InternalFunc) -> Self {
        Self(u32::from(index))
    }
}

impl EngineFunc {
    /// Creates a new [`EngineFunc`] from the given `u32` index.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// This is a test-only API and not meant for code outside of tests.
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub fn from_u32(index: u32) -> Self {
        Self(index)
    }
}

impl ArenaKey for EngineFunc {
    fn into_usize(self) -> usize {
        self.0.into_usize()
    }

    fn from_usize(value: usize) -> Option<Self> {
        <_ as ArenaKey>::from_usize(value).map(Self)
    }
}

/// Datastructure to efficiently store information about compiled functions.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct CodeMap {
    funcs: Mutex<Arena<EngineFunc, FuncEntity>>,
    features: WasmFeatures,
}

/// A range of [`EngineFunc`]s with contiguous indices.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct EngineFuncSpan {
    start: EngineFunc,
    end: EngineFunc,
}

impl Default for EngineFuncSpan {
    #[inline]
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::empty()
    }
}

impl EngineFuncSpan {
    /// Creates a new [`EngineFuncSpan`] for `start..end`.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If `start` index is not less than or equal to `end` index.
    pub fn new(start: EngineFunc, end: EngineFunc) -> Self {
        assert!(start <= end);
        Self { start, end }
    }

    /// Creates an empty [`EngineFuncSpan`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn empty() -> Self {
        Self {
            start: EngineFunc(0),
            end: EngineFunc(0),
        }
    }

    /// Returns `true` if `self` is empty.
    #[inline]
    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
        debug_assert!(self.start <= self.end);
        self.start == self.end
    }

    /// Returns the number of [`EngineFunc`] in `self`.
    pub fn len(&self) -> u32 {
        debug_assert!(self.start <= self.end);
        let start = self.start.0;
        let end = self.end.0;
        end - start
    }

    /// Returns the n-th [`EngineFunc`] in `self`, if any.
    ///
    /// Returns `None` if `n` is out of bounds.
    pub fn get(&self, n: u32) -> Option<EngineFunc> {
        debug_assert!(self.start <= self.end);
        if n >= self.len() {
            return None;
        }
        Some(EngineFunc(self.start.0 + n))
    }

    /// Returns the `u32` index of the [`EngineFunc`] in `self` if any.
    ///
    /// Returns `None` if `func` is not contained in `self`.
    pub fn position(&self, func: EngineFunc) -> Option<u32> {
        debug_assert!(self.start <= self.end);
        if func < self.start || func >= self.end {
            return None;
        }
        Some(func.0 - self.start.0)
    }

    /// Returns the n-th [`EngineFunc`] in `self`, if any.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If `n` is out of bounds.
    #[track_caller]
    pub fn get_or_panic(&self, n: u32) -> EngineFunc {
        debug_assert!(self.start <= self.end);
        self.get(n)
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("out of bounds `EngineFunc` index: {n}"))
    }

    /// Returns an iterator over the [`EngineFunc`]s in `self`.
    #[inline]
    pub fn iter(&self) -> EngineFuncSpanIter {
        debug_assert!(self.start <= self.end);
        EngineFuncSpanIter { span: *self }
    }
}

#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct EngineFuncSpanIter {
    span: EngineFuncSpan,
}

impl Iterator for EngineFuncSpanIter {
    type Item = EngineFunc;

    #[inline]
    fn next(&mut self) -> Option<Self::Item> {
        if self.span.is_empty() {
            return None;
        }
        let func = self.span.start;
        self.span.start = EngineFunc(self.span.start.0 + 1);
        Some(func)
    }

    #[inline]
    fn size_hint(&self) -> (usize, Option<usize>) {
        let remaining = self.span.len() as usize;
        (remaining, Some(remaining))
    }
}

impl DoubleEndedIterator
[OUTPUT_TAIL]
= FuncTranslator::new(func_idx, module, allocs)?;
                let allocs = FuncTranslationDriver::new(0, wasm_bytes, translator)?.translate(
                    |compiled_func| {
                        result.write(compiled_func);
                    },
                )?;
                engine.recycle_translation_allocs(allocs);
            }
        };
        Ok(unsafe { result.assume_init() })
    }
}

impl fmt::Debug for UncompiledFuncEntity {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter) -> fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("UncompiledFuncEntity")
            .field("func_idx", &self.func_index)
            .field("bytes", &self.bytes)
            .field("module", &self.module)
            .field("validate", &self.validation.is_some())
            .finish()
    }
}

/// A boxed byte slice that can store some bytes inline.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub enum SmallByteSlice {
    /// The byte slice fits in the inline buffer.
    Small {
        /// The length of the byte slice.
        len: u8,
        /// The bytes stored inline.
        ///
        /// Bytes beyond `len` are zeroed.
        bytes: [u8; Self::MAX_INLINE_SIZE],
    },
    /// The byte slice is too big and allocated on the heap.
    Big(Box<[u8]>),
}

impl Default for SmallByteSlice {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::Small {
            len: 0,
            bytes: [0x00; Self::MAX_INLINE_SIZE],
        }
    }
}

impl SmallByteSlice {
    /// The maximum amount of bytes that can be stored inline.
    ///
    /// This value was chosen because it allows for the maximum
    /// amount of bytes to be stored inline with minimal `size_of`.
    const MAX_INLINE_SIZE: usize = 22;

    /// Returns the underlying slice of bytes.
    #[inline]
    pub fn as_slice(&self) -> &[u8] {
        match self {
            SmallByteSlice::Small { len, bytes } => &bytes[..usize::from(*len)],
            SmallByteSlice::Big(bytes) => &bytes[..],
        }
    }
}

impl<I> ops::Index<I> for SmallByteSlice
where
    I: slice::SliceIndex<[u8]>,
{
    type Output = <I as slice::SliceIndex<[u8]>>::Output;

    #[inline]
    fn index(&self, index: I) -> &Self::Output {
        self.as_slice().index(index)
    }
}

impl<'a> From<&'a [u8]> for SmallByteSlice {
    fn from(bytes: &'a [u8]) -> Self {
        if bytes.len() <= Self::MAX_INLINE_SIZE {
            let len = bytes.len() as u8;
            let mut buffer = [0x00_u8; Self::MAX_INLINE_SIZE];
            buffer[..usize::from(len)].copy_from_slice(bytes);
            return Self::Small { len, bytes: buffer };
        }
        Self::Big(bytes.into())
    }
}

/// Meta information about a [`EngineFunc`].
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct CompiledFuncEntity {
    /// The sequence of [`Op`] of the [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
    ops: Pin<Box<[u8]>>,
    /// The number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`] in total.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// This includes stack slots to store the function local constant values,
    /// function parameters, function locals and dynamically used stack slots.
    len_stack_slots: u16,
}

impl CompiledFuncEntity {
    /// Create a new initialized [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// - If `ops` is empty.
    /// - If `ops` contains more than `i32::MAX` encoded bytes.
    pub fn new(len_stack_slots: u16, ops: &[u8]) -> Self {
        let ops: Pin<Box<[u8]>> = Pin::new(ops.into());
        assert!(
            !ops.is_empty(),
            "compiled functions must have at least one instruction"
        );
        assert!(
            // Generally, Wasmi has no issues with more than `i32::MAX` instructions.
            // However, Wasmi's branch instructions can jump across at most `i32::MAX`
            // forwards or `i32::MIN` instructions backwards and thus having more than
            // `i32::MAX` instructions might introduce problems.
            ops.len() <= i32::MAX as usize,
            "compiled function has too many instructions: {}",
            ops.len(),
        );
        Self {
            ops,
            len_stack_slots,
        }
    }
}

/// A shared reference to the data of a [`EngineFunc`].
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
pub struct CompiledFuncRef<'a> {
    /// The sequence of encoded [`Op`]s of the [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
    ops: Pin<&'a [u8]>,
    /// The number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`] in total.
    len_stack_slots: u16,
}

impl<'a> From<&'a CompiledFuncEntity> for CompiledFuncRef<'a> {
    #[inline]
    fn from(func: &'a CompiledFuncEntity) -> Self {
        Self {
            ops: func.ops.as_ref(),
            len_stack_slots: func.len_stack_slots,
        }
    }
}

impl<'a> CompiledFuncRef<'a> {
    /// Returns the sequence of encoded [`Op`]s of the [`EngineFunc`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn ops(&self) -> &'a [u8] {
        self.ops.get_ref()
    }

    /// Returns the number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn len_stack_slots(&self) -> u16 {
        self.len_stack_slots
    }
}

   119	    /// Returns the [`EngineFunc`] for the given [`FuncIdx`].
   120	    ///
   121	    /// Returns `None` if [`FuncIdx`] refers to an imported function.
   122	    pub fn get_engine_func(&self, func_idx: FuncIdx) -> Option<EngineFunc> {
   123	        let index = func_idx.into_u32();
   124	        let len_imported = self.inner.imports.len_funcs() as u32;
   125	        let index = index.checked_sub(len_imported)?;
   126	        // Note: It is a bug if this index access is out of bounds
   127	        //       therefore we panic here instead of using `get`.
   128	        Some(self.inner.engine_funcs.get_or_panic(index))
   129	    }
   130	
   131	    /// Returns the [`FuncIdx`] for the given [`EngineFunc`].
   132	    #[expect(unused)]
   133	    pub fn get_func_index(&self, func: EngineFunc) -> Option<FuncIdx> {
   134	        let position = self.inner.engine_funcs.position(func)?;
   135	        let len_imports = self.inner.imports.len_funcs as u32;
   136	        Some(FuncIdx::from(position + len_imports))
   137	    }
   138	
   139	    /// Returns the global variable type and optional initial value.
   140	    pub fn get_global(&self, global_idx: GlobalIdx) -> (&GlobalType, Option<&ConstExpr>) {
   141	        let index = global_idx.into_u32() as usize;
   142	        let len_imports = self.inner.imports.len_globals();
   143	        let global_type = self.get_type_of_global(global_idx);
   144	        if index < len_imports {
   145	            // The index refers to an imported global without init value.
   146	            (global_type, None)
   147	        } else {
   148	            // The index refers to an internal global with init value.
   149	            let init_expr = &self.inner.globals_init[index - len_imports];
   150	            (global_type, Some(init_expr))
   151	        }
   152	    }
   153	}

   119	define_handle! {
   120	    /// An instantiated WebAssembly [`Module`].
   121	    ///
   122	    /// This type represents an instantiation of a [`Module`].
   123	    /// It primarily allows to access its [`exports`](Instance::exports)
   124	    /// to call functions, get or set globals, read or write memory, etc.
   125	    ///
   126	    /// When interacting with any Wasm code you will want to create an
   127	    /// [`Instance`] in order to execute anything.
   128	    ///
   129	    /// Instances are owned by a [`Store`](crate::Store).
   130	    /// Create new instances using [`Linker::instantiate_and_start`](crate::Linker::instantiate_and_start).
   131	    struct Instance(u32, Stored) => InstanceEntity;
   132	}
   133	
   134	impl Instance {
   135	    /// Creates a new [`Instance`] from the pre-compiled [`Module`] and the list of `imports`.
   136	    ///
   137	    /// Uses the official [Wasm instantiation procedure] in order to resolve and type-check
   138	    /// the provided `imports` and match them with the required imports of the [`Module`].
   139	    ///
   140	    /// # Note
   141	    ///
   142	    /// - This function intentionally is rather low-level for [`Instance`] creation.
   143	    ///   Please use the [`Linker`](crate::Linker) type for a more high-level API for Wasm
   144	    ///   module instantiation with name-based resolution.
   145	    /// - Wasm module instantiation implies running the Wasm `start` function which is _not_
   146	    ///   to be confused with WASI's `_start` function.
   147	    ///
   148	    /// # Usage
   149	    ///
   150	    /// The `imports` are intended to correspond 1:1 with the required imports as returned by [`Module::imports`].
   151	    /// For each import type returned by [`Module::imports`], create an [`Extern`] which corresponds to that type.
   152	    /// Collect the [`Extern`] values created this way into a list and pass them to this function.
   153	    ///
   154	    /// # Errors
   155	    ///
   156	    /// - If the number of provided imports does not match the number of imports required by the [`Module`].
   157	    /// - If the type of any provided [`Extern`] does not match the corresponding required [`ExternType`].
   158	    /// - If the `start` function, that is run at the end of the Wasm module instantiation, traps.
   159	    /// - If Wasm module or instance related resource limits are exceeded.
   160	    ///
   161	    /// # Panics
   162	    ///
   163	    /// If any [`Extern`] does not originate from the provided `store`.
   164	    ///
   165	    /// [Wasm instantiation procedure]: https://webassembly.github.io/spec/core/exec/modules.html#exec-instantiation
   166	    pub fn new(
   167	        mut store: impl AsContextMut,
   168	        module: &Module,
   169	        imports: &[Extern],
   170	    ) -> Result<Instance, Error> {
   171	        let instance = Module::instantiate(module, &mut store, imports.iter().cloned())?;
   172	        Ok(instance)
   173	    }

   235	        Params: WasmParams,

   237	    {
   238	        self.get_export(&store, name)
   239	            .and_then(Extern::into_func)
   240	            .ok_or_else(|| Error::from(FuncError::ExportedFuncNotFound))?
   241	            .typed::<Params, Results>(store)
   242	    }
   243	
   244	    /// Looks up an exported [`Global`] value by `name`.
   245	    ///
   246	    /// Returns `None` if there was no export named `name`,
   247	    /// or if there was but it wasn’t a global variable.
   248	    ///
   249	    /// # Panics
   250	    ///
   251	    /// If `store` does not own this [`Instance`].
   252	    pub fn get_global(&self, store: impl AsContext, name: &str) -> Option<Global> {
   253	        self.get_export(store, name)?.into_global()
   254	    }
   255	
   256	    /// Looks up an exported [`Table`] value by `name`.
   257	    ///
   258	    /// Returns `None` if there was no export named `name`,
   259	    /// or if there was but it wasn’t a table.
   260	    ///

cat crates/wasmi/src/instance/builder.rs

returncode=0
use super::InstanceEntity;
use crate::{
    ElementSegment,
    Extern,
    ExternType,
    Func,
    Global,
    Memory,
    Module,
    Table,
    collections::Map,
    engine::DedupFuncType,
    memory::DataSegment,
    module::FuncIdx,
};
use alloc::{boxed::Box, sync::Arc, vec::Vec};

/// A module instance entity builder.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct InstanceEntityBuilder {
    func_types: Arc<[DedupFuncType]>,
    tables: Vec<Table>,
    funcs: Vec<Func>,
    memories: Vec<Memory>,
    globals: Vec<Global>,
    start_fn: Option<FuncIdx>,
    exports: Map<Box<str>, Extern>,
    data_segments: Vec<DataSegment>,
    elem_segments: Vec<ElementSegment>,
}

impl InstanceEntityBuilder {
    /// Creates a new [`InstanceEntityBuilder`] optimized for the [`Module`].
    pub fn new(module: &Module) -> Self {
        fn vec_with_capacity_exact<T>(capacity: usize) -> Vec<T> {
            let mut v = Vec::new();
            v.reserve_exact(capacity);
            v
        }
        let mut len_funcs = module.len_funcs();
        let mut len_globals = module.len_globals();
        let mut len_tables = module.len_tables();
        let mut len_memories = module.len_memories();
        for import in module.imports() {
            match import.ty() {
                ExternType::Func(_) => {
                    len_funcs += 1;
                }
                ExternType::Table(_) => {
                    len_tables += 1;
                }
                ExternType::Memory(_) => {
                    len_memories += 1;
                }
                ExternType::Global(_) => {
                    len_globals += 1;
                }
            }
        }
        Self {
            func_types: module.func_types_cloned(),
            tables: vec_with_capacity_exact(len_tables),
            funcs: vec_with_capacity_exact(len_funcs),
            memories: vec_with_capacity_exact(len_memories),
            globals: vec_with_capacity_exact(len_globals),
            start_fn: None,
            exports: Map::default(),
            data_segments: Vec::new(),
            elem_segments: Vec::new(),
        }
    }

    /// Sets the start function of the built instance.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If the start function has already been set.
    pub fn set_start(&mut self, start_fn: FuncIdx) {
        match &mut self.start_fn {
            Some(index) => panic!("already set start function {index:?}"),
            None => {
                self.start_fn = Some(start_fn);
            }
        }
    }

    /// Returns the start function index if any.
    pub fn get_start(&self) -> Option<FuncIdx> {
        self.start_fn
    }

    /// Returns the [`Memory`] at the `index`.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If there is no [`Memory`] at the given `index.
    pub fn get_memory(&self, index: u32) -> Memory {
        self.memories
            .get(index as usize)
            .copied()
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing `Memory` at index: {index}"))
    }

    /// Returns the [`Table`] at the `index`.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If there is no [`Table`] at the given `index.
    pub fn get_table(&self, index: u32) -> Table {
        self.tables
            .get(index as usize)
            .copied()
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing `Table` at index: {index}"))
    }

    /// Returns the [`Global`] at the `index`.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If there is no [`Global`] at the given `index.
    pub fn get_global(&self, index: u32) -> Global {
        self.globals
            .get(index as usize)
            .copied()
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing `Global` at index: {index}"))
    }

    /// Returns the function at the `index`.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If there is no function at the given `index.
    pub fn get_func(&self, index: u32) -> Func {
        self.funcs
            .get(index as usize)
            .copied()
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing `Func` at index: {index}"))
    }

    /// Pushes a new [`Memory`] to the [`InstanceEntity`] under construction.
    pub fn push_memory(&mut self, memory: Memory) {
        self.memories.push(memory);
    }

    /// Pushes a new [`Table`] to the [`InstanceEntity`] under construction.
    pub fn push_table(&mut self, table: Table) {
        self.tables.push(table);
    }

    /// Pushes a new [`Global`] to the [`InstanceEntity`] under construction.
    pub fn push_global(&mut self, global: Global) {
        self.globals.push(global);
    }

    /// Pushes a new [`Func`] to the [`InstanceEntity`] under construction.
    pub fn push_func(&mut self, func: Func) {
        self.funcs.push(func);
    }

    /// Pushes a new [`Extern`] under the given `name` to the [`InstanceEntity`] under construction.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If the name has already been used by an already pushed [`Extern`].
    pub fn push_export(&mut self, name: &str, new_value: Extern) {
        if let Some(old_value) = self.exports.get(name) {
            panic!(
                "tried to register {new_value:?} for name {name} \
                but name is already used by {old_value:?}",
            )
        }
        self.exports.insert(name.into(), new_value);
    }

    /// Pushes the [`DataSegment`] to the [`InstanceEntity`] under construction.
    pub fn push_data_segment(&mut self, segment: DataSegment) {
        self.data_segments.push(segment);
    }

    /// Pushes the [`ElementSegment`] to the [`InstanceEntity`] under construction.
    pub fn push_element_segment(&mut self, segment: ElementSegment) {
        self.elem_segments.push(segment);
    }

    /// Finishes constructing the [`InstanceEntity`].
    pub fn finish(self) -> InstanceEntity {
        InstanceEntity {
            initialized: true,
            func_types: self.func_types,
            tables: self.tables.into(),
            funcs: self.funcs.into(),
            memories: self.memories.into(),
            globals: self.globals.into(),
            exports: self.exports,
            data_segments: self.data_segments.into(),
            elem_segments: self.elem_segments.into(),
        }
    }
}

END STEP

   162	/// A host function instance.
   163	pub struct HostFuncTrampolineEntity<T> {
   164	    /// The type of the associated host function.
   165	    ty: FuncType,
   166	    /// The trampoline of the associated host function.
   167	    trampoline: TrampolineEntity<T>,
   168	}
   169	
   170	impl<T> Clone for HostFuncTrampolineEntity<T> {
   171	    fn clone(&self) -> Self {
   172	        Self {
   173	            ty: self.ty.clone(),
   174	            trampoline: self.trampoline.clone(),
   175	        }
   176	    }
   177	}
   178	
   179	impl<T> Debug for HostFuncTrampolineEntity<T> {
   180	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
   181	        Debug::fmt(&self.ty, f)
   182	    }
   183	}
   184	
   185	impl<T> HostFuncTrampolineEntity<T> {
   186	    /// Creates a new host function trampoline from the given dynamically typed closure.
   187	    pub fn new(
   188	        ty: FuncType,
   189	        func: impl Fn(Caller<'_, T>, &[Val], &mut [Val]) -> Result<(), Error> + Send + Sync + 'static,
   190	    ) -> Self {
   191	        // Preprocess parameters and results buffers so that we can reuse those
   192	        // computations within the closure implementation. We put both parameters
   193	        // and results into a single buffer which we can split to minimize the
   194	        // amount of allocations per trampoline invocation.
   195	        let params_iter = ty.params().iter().copied().map(Val::default_for_ty);
   196	        let results_iter = ty.results().iter().copied().map(Val::default_for_ty);
   197	        let len_params = ty.params().len();
   198	        let params_results: Box<[Val]> = params_iter.chain(results_iter).collect();
   199	        let trampoline = <TrampolineEntity<T>>::new(move |caller, inout| {
   200	            // We are required to clone the buffer because we are operating within a `Fn`.
   201	            // This way the trampoline closure only has to own a single slice buffer.
   202	            // Note: An alternative solution is to use interior mutability but that solution
   203	            //       comes with its own downsides.
   204	            let mut params_results = params_results.clone();
   205	            let (params, results) = params_results.split_at_mut(len_params);
   206	            let store_id = caller.as_context().store.inner.id();
   207	            inout
   208	                .decode_params_into(store_id, &mut params[..])
   209	                .unwrap_or_else(|error| {
   210	                    panic!("failed to decode host function parameters: {error}")
   211	                });
   212	            func(caller, params, results)?;
   213	            let results = inout
   214	                .encode_results(store_id, &results[..])
   215	                .unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("failed to encode host function results: {error}"));
   216	            Ok(results)
   217	        });
   218	        Self { ty, trampoline }
   219	    }
   220	
   221	    /// Creates a new host function trampoline from the given statically typed closure.
   222	    pub fn wrap<Params, Results>(func: impl IntoFunc<T, Params, Results>) -> Self {
   223	        let (ty, trampoline) = func.into_func();
   224	        Self { ty, trampoline }
   225	    }
   226	
   227	    /// Returns the [`FuncType`] of the host function.
   228	    pub fn func_type(&self) -> &FuncType {
   229	        &self.ty
   230	    }
   231	
   232	    /// Returns the trampoline of the host function.
   233	    pub fn trampoline(&self) -> &TrampolineEntity<T> {
   234	        &self.trampoline
   235	    }
   236	}
   237	
   238	type TrampolineFn<T> = dyn for<'a> Fn(Caller<T>, InOutParams<'a>) -> Result<InOutResults<'a>, Error>
   239	    + Send
   240	    + Sync
   241	    + 'static;
   242	
   243	pub struct TrampolineEntity<T> {
   244	    closure: Arc<TrampolineFn<T>>,
   245	}
   246	
   247	impl<T> Debug for TrampolineEntity<T> {
   248	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
   249	        f.debug_struct("TrampolineEntity").finish()
   250	    }
   251	}
   252	
   253	impl<T> TrampolineEntity<T> {
   254	    /// Creates a new [`TrampolineEntity`] from the given host function.
   255	    pub fn new<F>(trampoline: F) -> Self
   256	    where
   257	        F: for<'a> Fn(Caller<T>, InOutParams<'a>) -> Result<InOutResults<'a>, Error>
   258	            + Send
   259	            + Sync
   260	            + 'static,
   261	    {
   262	        Self {
   263	            closure: Arc::new(trampoline),
   264	        }
   265	    }
   266	
   267	    /// Calls the host function trampoline with the given inputs.
   268	    ///
   269	    /// The result is written back into the `outputs` buffer.
   270	    pub fn call<'a>(
   271	        &self,
   272	        mut ctx: impl AsContextMut<Data = T>,
   273	        instance: Option<Inst>,
   274	        params: InOutParams<'a>,
   275	    ) -> Result<InOutResults<'a>, Error> {
   276	        let caller = <Caller<T>>::new(&mut ctx, instance);
   277	        (self.closure)(caller, params)
   278	    }
   279	}
   280	
   281	impl<T> Clone for TrampolineEntity<T> {
   282	    fn clone(&self) -> Self {
   283	        Self {
   284	            closure: self.closure.clone(),
   285	        }
   286	    }
   287	}

    33	/// The inner store that owns all data not associated to the host state.
    34	#[derive(Debug)]
    35	pub struct StoreInner {
    36	    /// The unique store index.
    37	    ///

    39	    id: StoreId,
    40	    /// Stored Wasm or host functions.
    41	    funcs: StoreArena<Func>,

    85	        }

   144	    /// Unwraps the given [`Stored<T>`] reference and returns the `T`.
   145	    ///
   146	    /// # Errors
   147	    ///
   148	    /// If the [`Stored<T>`] does not originate from `self`.
   149	    pub(super) fn unwrap_stored<'a, T>(
   150	        &self,
   151	        stored: &'a Stored<T>,
   152	    ) -> Result<&'a T, InternalStoreError> {
   153	        match self.id.unwrap(stored) {
   154	            Some(value) => Ok(value),
   155	            None => Err(InternalStoreError::store_mismatch()),
   156	        }
   157	    }
   158	}
   159	
   160	impl StoreInner {
   161	    /// Allocates a new [`CoreGlobal`] and returns a [`Global`] reference to it.
   162	    pub fn alloc_global(&mut self, value: CoreGlobal) -> Global {
   163	        let key = match self.globals.alloc(value) {
   164	            Ok(key) => key,
   165	            Err(err) => handle_arena_err(err, "alloc global"),
   166	        };
   167	        Global::from_raw(self.id.wrap(key))
   168	    }
   169	
   170	    /// Allocates a new [`CoreTable`] and returns a [`Table`] reference to it.
   171	    pub fn alloc_table(&mut self, value: CoreTable) -> Table {
   172	        let key = match self.tables.alloc(value) {
   173	            Ok(key) => key,

   288	    pub fn get<'a>(
   289	        &'a self,
   290	        fuel: Option<&mut Fuel>,
   291	        func: EngineFunc,
   292	    ) -> Result<CompiledFuncRef<'a>, Error> {

   300	    ///
   301	    /// # Errors
   302	    ///
   303	    /// - If translation or Wasm validation of `func` failed.
   304	    /// - If `ctx` ran out of fuel in case fuel consumption is enabled.

   311	    ) -> Result<CompiledFuncRef<'a>, Error> {

   313	            Some(entity) => self.compile(fuel, func, entity),
   314	            None => self.wait_for_compilation(func),
   315	        }
   316	    }

   436	
   437	/// An internal function entity.
   438	///
   439	/// Either an already compiled or still uncompiled function entity.
   440	#[derive(Debug)]
   441	enum FuncEntity {
   442	    /// The function entity has not yet been initialized.
   443	    Uninit,
   444	    /// An internal function that has not yet been compiled.
   445	    Uncompiled(UncompiledFuncEntity),
   446	    /// The function entity is currently compiling.
   447	    Compiling,
   448	    /// The function entity failed to compile lazily.
   449	    FailedToCompile,
   450	    /// An internal function that has already been compiled.
   451	    Compiled(CompiledFuncEntity),
   452	}
   453	
   454	impl Default for FuncEntity {
   455	    #[inline]
   456	    fn default() -> Self {
   457	        Self::Uninit
   458	    }
   459	}
   460	
   461	impl FuncEntity {
   462	    /// Initializes the [`FuncEntity`] with a [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
   463	    ///
   464	    /// # Panics
   465	    ///
   466	    /// If `func` has already been initialized.
   467	    #[inline]
   468	    pub fn init_compiled(&mut self, entity: CompiledFuncEntity) {
   469	        assert!(matches!(self, Self::Uninit));
   470	        *self = Self::Compiled(entity);
   471	    }
   472	
   473	    /// Initializes the [`FuncEntity`] to an uncompiled state.
   474	    ///
   475	    /// # Panics
   476	    ///
   477	    /// If `func` has already been initialized.
   478	    #[inline]
   479	    pub fn init_uncompiled(&mut self, entity: UncompiledFuncEntity) {
   480	        assert!(matches!(self, Self::Uninit));
   481	        *self = Self::Uncompiled(entity);
   482	    }
   483	
   484	    /// Returns the [`CompiledFuncEntity`] if possible.
   485	    ///
   486	    /// Returns `None` if the [`FuncEntity`] has not yet been compiled.
   487	    #[inline]
   488	    pub fn get_compiled(&self) -> Option<CompiledFuncRef<'_>> {
   489	        match self {
   490	            FuncEntity::Compiled(func) => Some(func.into()),
   491	            _ => None,
   492	        }
   493	    }
   494	
   495	    /// Returns the [`UncompiledFuncEntity`] if possible.
   496	    ///
   497	    /// # Errors
   498	    ///
   499	    /// Returns a proper error if the [`FuncEntity`] is not uncompiled.
   500	    #[inline]
   501	    pub fn get_uncompiled(&mut self) -> Option<UncompiledFuncEntity> {
   502	        match self {
   503	            Self::Uncompiled(_) => {}
   504	            _ => return None,
   505	        };
   506	        match mem::replace(self, Self::Compiling) {
   507	            Self::Uncompiled(func) => Some(func),
   508	            _ => {
   509	                // Safety: we just asserted that `self` must be an uncompiled function
   510	                //         since otherwise we would have returned `None` above.
   511	                //         Since this is a performance critical path we need to leave out this check.
   512	                unsafe {
   513	                    unreachable_unchecked!("expected uncompiled function but found: {self:?}")
   514	                }
   515	            }
   516	        }
   517	    }
   518	
   519	    /// Sets the state back to [`UncompiledFuncEntity`] if possible.
   520	    ///
   521	    /// # Panics
   522	    ///
   523	    /// If the current state was not [`FuncEntity::Compiling`].
   524	    #[inline]
   525	    pub fn set_uncompiled(&mut self, uncompiled: UncompiledFuncEntity) {
   526	        match mem::replace(self, FuncEntity::Uncompiled(uncompiled)) {
   527	            Self::Compiling => {}
   528	            unexpected => {
   529	                // Safety: we just asserted that `self` must be an uncompiled function
   530	                //         since otherwise we would have returned `None` above.
   531	                //         Since this is a performance critical path we need to leave out this check.
   532	                unsafe {
   533	                    unreachable_unchecked!(
   534	                        "can only set `Compiling` back to `UncompiledFuncEntity` but found: {unexpected:?}"
   535	                    )
   536	                }
   537	            }
   538	        }
   539	    }
   540	
   541	    /// Sets the [`FuncEntity`] as [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
   542	    ///
   543	    /// Returns a [`CompiledFuncRef`] to the [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
   544	    ///
   545	    /// # Panics
   546	    ///
   547	    /// If `func` has already been initialized.
   548	    #[inline]
   549	    pub fn set_compiled(&mut self, entity: CompiledFuncEntity) -> CompiledFuncRef<'_> {
   550	        assert!(matches!(self, Self::Compiling));
   551	        *self = Self::Compiled(entity);
   552	        let Self::Compiled(entity) = self else {
   553	            panic!("just initialized `self` as compiled")
   554	        };
   555	        CompiledFuncRef::from(&*entity)
   556	    }
   557	
   558	    /// Signals a failed compilation for the [`FuncEntity`].
   559	    ///
   560	    /// # Panics

   259	pub fn exec_copy_span(sp: Sp, dst: SlotSpan, src: SlotSpan, len: u16) {
   260	    let op = match dst.head() <= src.head() {
   261	        true => exec_copy_span_asc,
   262	        false => exec_copy_span_des,
   263	    };
   264	    op(sp, dst, src, len)
   265	}
   266	
   267	pub fn exec_copy_span_asc(sp: Sp, dst: SlotSpan, src: SlotSpan, len: u16) {
   268	    debug_assert!(dst.head() <= src.head());
   269	    let dst = dst.iter(len);
   270	    let src = src.iter(len);
   271	    for (dst, src) in dst.into_iter().zip(src) {
   272	        let value: u64 = get_value(src, sp);
   273	        set_value(sp, dst, value);
   274	    }
   275	}
   276	
   277	pub fn exec_copy_span_des(sp: Sp, dst: SlotSpan, src: SlotSpan, len: u16) {
   278	    debug_assert!(dst.head() >= src.head());
   279	    let dst = dst.iter(len);
   280	    let src = src.iter(len);
   281	    for (dst, src) in dst.into_iter().zip(src).rev() {
   282	        let value: u64 = get_value(src, sp);
   283	        set_value(sp, dst, value);
   284	    }
   285	}

   287	pub fn extract_mem0(store: &mut PrunedStore, instance: Inst) -> (Mem0Ptr, Mem0Len) {
   288	    let instance = unsafe { instance.as_ref() };
   289	    let Some(memory) = instance.get_memory(0) else {
   290	        return (Mem0Ptr::from([].as_mut_ptr()), Mem0Len::from(0));
   291	    };
   292	    let mem0 = resolve_memory_mut(store, &memory).data_mut();
   293	    let mem0_ptr = mem0.as_mut_ptr();
   294	    let mem0_len = mem0.len();
   295	    (Mem0Ptr::from(mem0_ptr), Mem0Len::from(mem0_len))
   296	}

   310	}

   336	macro_rules! impl_fetch_from_instance {
   337	    (
   338	        $( fn $fn:ident($param:ident: $ty:ty) -> $ret:ty = $getter:expr );* $(;)?
   339	    ) => {
   340	        $(
   341	            pub fn $fn(instance: Inst, $param: $ty) -> $ret {
   342	                let instance = unsafe { instance.as_ref() };
   343	                let index = ::core::primitive::u32::from($param);
   344	                let Some($param) = $getter(instance, index) else {
   345	                    unsafe {
   346	                        $crate::engine::utils::unreachable_unchecked!(
   347	                            ::core::concat!("missing ", ::core::stringify!($param), " at: {:?}"),
   348	                            index,
   349	                        )
   350	                    }
   351	                };
   352	                $param
   353	            }
   354	        )*
   355	    };
   356	}

   404	pub fn resolve_indirect_func(
   405	    index: Slot,
   406	    table: index::Table,
   407	    func_type: index::FuncType,
   408	    state: &mut VmState<'_>,
   409	    sp: Sp,
   410	    instance: Inst,
   411	) -> Result<Func, TrapCode> {
   412	    let index = get_value(index, sp);
   413	    let table = fetch_table(instance, table);
   414	    let table = resolve_table(state.store, &table);
   415	    let rawref = table.get(index).ok_or(TrapCode::TableOutOfBounds)?;
   416	    debug_assert!(matches!(rawref.ty(), RefType::Func));
   417	    let funcref = <Nullable<Func>>::from_raw_parts(rawref.raw(), &*state.store);
   418	    let func = funcref.val().ok_or(TrapCode::IndirectCallToNull)?;
   419	    let actual_fnty = resolve_func(state.store, func).ty_dedup();
   420	    let expected_fnty = fetch_func_type(instance, func_type);
   421	    if expected_fnty.ne(actual_fnty) {
   422	        return Err(TrapCode::BadSignature);
   423	    }
   424	    Ok(*func)
   425	}

   427	pub fn set_global<V>(global: index::Global, value: V, state: &mut VmState, instance: Inst)
   428	where
   429	    RawVal: WriteAs<V>,
   430	{
   431	    let global = fetch_global(instance, global);
   432	    let global = resolve_global_mut(state.store, &global);
   433	    let mut value_ptr = global.get_raw_ptr();
   434	    let global_ref = unsafe { value_ptr.as_mut() };
   435	    global_ref.write_as(value);
   436	}

   438	pub fn update_instance(
   439	    store: &mut PrunedStore,
   440	    instance: Inst,
   441	    new_instance: Inst,
   442	    mem0: Mem0Ptr,
   443	    mem0_len: Mem0Len,
   444	) -> (Inst, Mem0Ptr, Mem0Len) {
   445	    if new_instance == instance {
   446	        return (instance, mem0, mem0_len);
   447	    }
   448	    let (mem0, mem0_len) = extract_mem0(store, new_instance);
   449	    (new_instance, mem0, mem0_len)
   450	}

   452	pub fn call_wasm(
   453	    state: &mut VmState,
   454	    caller_ip: Ip,
   455	    params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   456	    func: EngineFunc,
   457	    instance: Option<Inst>,
   458	) -> Control<(Ip, Sp), Break> {
   459	    let (callee_ip, size) = compile_or_get_func!(state, func);
   460	    let callee_sp = state
   461	        .stack
   462	        .push_frame(Some(caller_ip), callee_ip, params, size, instance)
   463	        .into_control()?;
   464	    Control::Continue((callee_ip, callee_sp))
   465	}
   466	
   467	pub fn return_call_wasm(
   468	    state: &mut VmState,
   469	    params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   470	    func: EngineFunc,
   471	    instance: Option<Inst>,
   472	) -> Control<(Ip, Sp), Break> {
   473	    let (callee_ip, size) = compile_or_get_func!(state, func);
   474	    let callee_sp = state
   475	        .stack
   476	        .replace_frame(callee_ip, params, size, instance)
   477	        .into_control()?;
   478	    Control::Continue((callee_ip, callee_sp))
   479	}
   480	
   481	pub fn call_host(
   482	    state: &mut VmState,
   483	    func: Func,
   484	    caller_ip: Option<Ip>,
   485	    host_func: HostFuncEntity,
   486	    params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   487	    instance: Option<Inst>,
   488	    call_hooks: CallHooks,
   489	) -> Control<Sp, Break> {
   490	    debug_assert_eq!(params.len(), host_func.len_param_cells());
   491	    let trampoline = *host_func.trampoline();
   492	    let (sp, inout) = state
   493	        .stack
   494	        .prepare_host_frame(caller_ip, params, host_func.len_result_cells())
   495	        .into_control()?;
   496	    match state
   497	        .store
   498	        .call_host_func(trampoline, instance, inout, call_hooks)
   499	    {
   500	        Ok(()) => {}
   501	        Err(StoreError::External(error)) => {
   502	            done!(state, DoneReason::host_error(error, func, params.span()))
   503	        }
   504	        Err(StoreError::Internal(error)) => unsafe {
   505	            unreachable_unchecked!(
   506	                "internal interpreter error while executing host function: {error}"
   507	            )
   508	        },
   509	    }
   510	    Control::Continue(sp)
   511	}

   520	    debug_assert_eq!(params.len(), host_func.len_param_cells());
