   575	/// An internal uncompiled function entity.
   576	pub struct UncompiledFuncEntity {
   577	    /// The index of the function within the Wasm module.
   578	    func_index: FuncIdx,
   579	    /// The Wasm binary bytes.
   580	    bytes: SmallByteSlice,
   581	    /// The Wasm module of the Wasm function.
   582	    ///
   583	    /// This is required for Wasm module related information in order
   584	    /// to compile the Wasm function body.
   585	    module: ModuleHeader,
   586	    /// Optional Wasm validation information.
   587	    ///
   588	    /// This is `Some` if the [`UncompiledFuncEntity`] is to be validated upon compilation.
   589	    validation: Option<(TypeIndex, ValidatorResources)>,
   590	}
   591	
   592	impl UncompiledFuncEntity {
   593	    /// Creates a new [`UncompiledFuncEntity`].
   594	    pub fn new(
   595	        func_index: FuncIdx,
   596	        bytes: &[u8],
   597	        module: ModuleHeader,
   598	        func_to_validate: impl Into<Option<FuncToValidate<ValidatorResources>>>,
   599	    ) -> Self {
   600	        let validation = func_to_validate.into().map(|func_to_validate| {
   601	            assert_eq!(
   602	                func_to_validate.index,
   603	                func_index.into_u32(),
   604	                "Wasmi function index ({}) does not match with Wasm validation function index ({})",
   605	                func_to_validate.index,
   606	                func_index.into_u32(),
   607	            );
   608	            (TypeIndex(func_to_validate.ty), func_to_validate.resources)
   609	        });
   610	        let bytes = bytes.into();
   611	        Self {
   612	            func_index,
   613	            bytes,
   614	            module,
   615	            validation,
   616	        }
   617	    }
   618	
   619	    /// Compile the [`UncompiledFuncEntity`].
   620	    ///
   621	    /// # Panics
   622	    ///
   623	    /// - If the `func` unexpectedly has already been compiled.
   624	    /// - If the `engine` unexpectedly no longer exists due to weak referencing.
   625	    ///
   626	    /// # Errors
   627	    ///
   628	    /// - If function translation failed.
   629	    /// - If `ctx` ran out of fuel in case fuel consumption is enabled.
   630	    fn compile(
   631	        &mut self,
   632	        fuel: Option<&mut Fuel>,
   633	        features: &WasmFeatures,
   634	    ) -> Result<CompiledFuncEntity, Error> {
   635	        /// The amount of fuel required to compile a function body per byte.
   636	        ///
   637	        /// This does _not_ include validation.
   638	        ///
   639	        /// # Note
   640	        ///
   641	        /// This fuel amount was chosen after extensive worst-case translation benchmarking.
   642	        const COMPILE_FUEL_PER_BYTE: u64 = 7;
   643	        /// The amount of fuel required to validate a function body per byte.
   644	        ///
   645	        /// This does _not_ include compilation.
   646	        ///
   647	        /// # Note
   648	        ///
   649	        /// This fuel amount was chosen after extensive worst-case translation benchmarking.
   650	        const VALIDATE_FUEL_PER_BYTE: u64 = 2;
   651	        /// The amount of fuel required to validate and compile a function body per byte.
   652	        const VALIDATE_AND_COMPILE_FUEL_PER_BYTE: u64 =
   653	            VALIDATE_FUEL_PER_BYTE + COMPILE_FUEL_PER_BYTE;
   654	
   655	        let func_idx = self.func_index;
   656	        let wasm_bytes = self.bytes.as_slice();
   657	        let needs_validation = self.validation.is_some();
   658	        let compilation_fuel = |_costs: &FuelCostsProvider| {
   659	            let len_bytes = wasm_bytes.len() as u64;
   660	            let fuel_per_byte = match needs_validation {
   661	                false => COMPILE_FUEL_PER_BYTE,
   662	                true => VALIDATE_AND_COMPILE_FUEL_PER_BYTE,
   663	            };
   664	            len_bytes.saturating_mul(fuel_per_byte)
   665	        };
   666	        if let Some(fuel) = fuel {
   667	            match fuel.consume_fuel(compilation_fuel) {
   668	                Err(FuelError::OutOfFuel { required_fuel }) => {
   669	                    return Err(Error::from(ResumableOutOfFuelError::new(required_fuel)));
   670	                }
   671	                Ok(_) | Err(FuelError::FuelMeteringDisabled) => {}
   672	            }
   673	        }
   674	        let module = self.module.clone();
   675	        let Some(engine) = module.engine().upgrade() else {
   676	            panic!(
   677	                "cannot compile function lazily since engine does no longer exist: {:?}",
   678	                module.engine()
   679	            )
   680	        };
   681	        let mut result = MaybeUninit::uninit();
   682	        match self.validation.take() {
   683	            Some((type_index, resources)) => {
   684	                let allocs = engine.get_allocs();
   685	                let translator = FuncTranslator::new(func_idx, module, allocs.0)?;
   686	                let func_to_validate = FuncToValidate {
   687	                    resources,
   688	                    index: func_idx.into_u32(),
   689	                    ty: type_index.0,
   690	                    features: *features,
   691	                };
   692	                let validator = func_to_validate.into_validator(allocs.1);
   693	                let translator = ValidatingFuncTranslator::new(validator, translator)?;
   694	                let allocs = FuncTranslationDriver::new(0, wasm_bytes, translator)?.translate(
   695	                    |compiled_func| {
   696	                        result.write(compiled_func);
   697	                    },
   698	                )?;
   699	                engine.recycle_allocs(allocs.translation, allocs.validation);
   700	            }
   701	            None => {
   702	                let allocs = engine.get_translation_allocs();
   703	                let translator = FuncTranslator::new(func_idx, module, allocs)?;
   704	                let allocs = FuncTranslationDriver::new(0, wasm_bytes, translator)?.translate(
   705	                    |compiled_func| {
   706	                        result.write(compiled_func);
   707	                    },
   708	                )?;
   709	                engine.recycle_translation_allocs(allocs);
   710	            }
   711	        };
   712	        Ok(unsafe { result.assume_init() })
   713	    }
   714	}
   715	
   716	impl fmt::Debug for UncompiledFuncEntity {
   717	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter) -> fmt::Result {
   718	        f.debug_struct("UncompiledFuncEntity")
   719	            .field("func_idx", &self.func_index)
   720	            .field("bytes", &self.bytes)
   721	            .field("module", &self.module)
   722	            .field("validate", &self.validation.is_some())
   723	            .finish()
   724	    }
   725	}

   727	/// A boxed byte slice that can store some bytes inline.
   728	#[derive(Debug)]
   729	pub enum SmallByteSlice {
   730	    /// The byte slice fits in the inline buffer.
   731	    Small {
   732	        /// The length of the byte slice.
   733	        len: u8,
   734	        /// The bytes stored inline.
   735	        ///
   736	        /// Bytes beyond `len` are zeroed.
   737	        bytes: [u8; Self::MAX_INLINE_SIZE],
   738	    },
   739	    /// The byte slice is too big and allocated on the heap.
   740	    Big(Box<[u8]>),
   741	}
   742	
   743	impl Default for SmallByteSlice {
   744	    fn default() -> Self {
   745	        Self::Small {
   746	            len: 0,
   747	            bytes: [0x00; Self::MAX_INLINE_SIZE],
   748	        }
   749	    }
   750	}
   751	
   752	impl SmallByteSlice {
   753	    /// The maximum amount of bytes that can be stored inline.
   754	    ///
   755	    /// This value was chosen because it allows for the maximum
   756	    /// amount of bytes to be stored inline with minimal `size_of`.
   757	    const MAX_INLINE_SIZE: usize = 22;
   758	
   759	    /// Returns the underlying slice of bytes.
   760	    #[inline]
   761	    pub fn as_slice(&self) -> &[u8] {
   762	        match self {
   763	            SmallByteSlice::Small { len, bytes } => &bytes[..usize::from(*len)],
   764	            SmallByteSlice::Big(bytes) => &bytes[..],
   765	        }
   766	    }
   767	}
   768	
   769	impl<I> ops::Index<I> for SmallByteSlice
   770	where
   771	    I: slice::SliceIndex<[u8]>,
   772	{
   773	    type Output = <I as slice::SliceIndex<[u8]>>::Output;
   774	
   775	    #[inline]
   776	    fn index(&self, index: I) -> &Self::Output {
   777	        self.as_slice().index(index)
   778	    }
   779	}
   780	
   781	impl<'a> From<&'a [u8]> for SmallByteSlice {
   782	    fn from(bytes: &'a [u8]) -> Self {
   783	        if bytes.len() <= Self::MAX_INLINE_SIZE {
   784	            let len = bytes.len() as u8;
   785	            let mut buffer = [0x00_u8; Self::MAX_INLINE_SIZE];
   786	            buffer[..usize::from(len)].copy_from_slice(bytes);
   787	            return Self::Small { len, bytes: buffer };
   788	        }
   789	        Self::Big(bytes.into())
   790	    }
   791	}

   795	pub struct CompiledFuncEntity {

    21	/// Types that can be used as indices for arenas.
    22	pub trait ArenaKey: Copy {
    23	    /// Converts the [`ArenaKey`] into the underlying `usize` value.
    24	    ///
    25	    /// # Note
    26	    ///
    27	    /// - No checks need to be applied here since it is assumed that `ArenaKey`
    28	    ///   items are only valid if they can be converted into a valid `usize`.
    29	    /// - The `from_usize` constructor is supposed to make sure that this
    30	    ///   invariant is conserved.
    31	    fn into_usize(self) -> usize;
    32	
    33	    /// Converts the `usize` value into the associated [`ArenaKey`].
    34	    ///
    35	    /// Returns `None` if `Self` cannot represent `value`.
    36	    fn from_usize(value: usize) -> Option<Self>;
    37	}
    38	
    39	impl ArenaKey for usize {
    40	    #[inline]
    41	    fn into_usize(self) -> usize {
    42	        self
    43	    }
    44	
    45	    #[inline]
    46	    fn from_usize(value: usize) -> Option<Self> {
    47	        Some(value)
    48	    }
    49	}
    50	
    51	impl ArenaKey for u32 {
    52	    #[inline]
    53	    fn into_usize(self) -> usize {
    54	        // Note: there is no need to perform checks for the cast as those
    55	        //       have already been applied and implied in `from_usize`.
    56	        self as usize
    57	    }
    58	
    59	    #[inline]
    60	    fn from_usize(value: usize) -> Option<Self> {
    61	        u32::try_from(value).ok()
    62	    }
    63	}
    64	
    65	impl ArenaKey for NonZero<usize> {
    66	    #[inline]
    67	    fn into_usize(self) -> usize {
    68	        // Note: there is no need to perform checks for the cast as those
    69	        //       have already been applied and implied in `from_usize`.
    70	        self.get().wrapping_sub(1)
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    #[inline]
    74	    fn from_usize(value: usize) -> Option<Self> {
    75	        Self::new(value.wrapping_add(1))
    76	    }
    77	}
    78	
    79	impl ArenaKey for NonZero<u32> {
    80	    #[inline]
    81	    fn into_usize(self) -> usize {
    82	        // Note: there is no need to perform checks for the cast as those
    83	        //       have already been applied and implied in `from_usize`.
    84	        self.get().wrapping_sub(1) as usize
    85	    }
    86	
    87	    #[inline]
    88	    fn from_usize(value: usize) -> Option<Self> {
    89	        let value = u32::try_from(value).ok()?;
    90	        Self::new(value.wrapping_add(1))
    91	    }
    92	}

    94	/// An arena allocator with a given key and entity type.
    95	///
    96	/// For performance reasons the arena cannot deallocate single entities.
    97	#[derive(Debug)]
    98	pub struct Arena<Key, T> {
    99	    /// The items stored in the arena.
   100	    items: Vec<T>,
   101	    /// Marker for the compiler to associate the `Key` type.
   102	    marker: PhantomData<Key>,
   103	}
   104	
   105	/// [`Arena`] does not store `Key` therefore it is `Send` without its bound.
   106	unsafe impl<Key, T> Send for Arena<Key, T> where T: Send {}
   107	
   108	/// [`Arena`] does not store `Key` therefore it is `Sync` without its bound.
   109	unsafe impl<Key, T> Sync for Arena<Key, T> where T: Sync {}
   110	
   111	impl<Key, T> Default for Arena<Key, T> {
   112	    fn default() -> Self {
   113	        Self::new()
   114	    }
   115	}

   118	where
   119	    T: PartialEq,
   120	{
   121	    fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
   122	        self.items.eq(&other.items)
   123	    }
   124	}

    81	/// Type concerned with translating from Wasm bytecode to Wasmi bytecode.
    82	#[derive(Debug)]
    83	pub struct FuncTranslator {
    84	    /// The reference to the Wasm module function under construction.
    85	    func: FuncIdx,
    86	    /// The engine for which the function is compiled.
    87	    ///
    88	    /// # Note
    89	    ///
    90	    /// Technically this is not needed since the information is redundant given via
    91	    /// the `module` field. However, this acts like a faster access since `module`
    92	    /// only holds a weak reference to the engine.
    93	    engine: Engine,
    94	    /// The immutable Wasmi module resources.
    95	    module: ModuleHeader,
    96	    /// This represents the reachability of the currently translated code.
    97	    ///
    98	    /// - `true`: The currently translated code is reachable.
    99	    /// - `false`: The currently translated code is unreachable and can be skipped.
   100	    ///
   101	    /// # Note
   102	    ///
   103	    /// Visiting the Wasm `Else` or `End` control flow operator resets
   104	    /// reachability to `true` again.
   105	    reachable: bool,
   106	    /// Wasm value and control stack.
   107	    stack: Stack,
   108	    /// Types of local variables and function parameters.
   109	    locals: LocalsRegistry,
   110	    /// Wasm layout to map stack slots to Wasmi registers.
   111	    layout: StackLayout,
   112	    /// Constructs and encodes function instructions.
   113	    instrs: OpEncoder,
   114	    /// Temporary buffer for operands.
   115	    operands: Vec<Operand>,
   116	    /// Temporary buffer for immediate values.
   117	    immediates: Vec<TypedRawVal>,
   118	}
   119	
   120	/// Heap allocated data structured used by the [`FuncTranslator`].
   121	#[derive(Debug, Default)]
   122	pub struct FuncTranslatorAllocations {
   123	    /// Wasm value and control stack.
   124	    stack: StackAllocations,
   125	    /// Types of local variables and function parameters.
   126	    locals: LocalsRegistry,
   127	    /// Wasm layout to map stack slots to Wasmi registers.
   128	    layout: StackLayout,
   129	    /// Constructs and encodes function instructions.
   130	    instrs: OpEncoderAllocations,
   131	    /// Temporary buffer for operands.
   132	    operands: Vec<Operand>,
   133	    /// Temporary buffer for immediate values.
   134	    immediates: Vec<TypedRawVal>,
   135	}
   136	
   137	impl Reset for FuncTranslatorAllocations {
   138	    fn reset(&mut self) {
   139	        self.stack.reset();
   140	        self.locals.reset();
   141	        self.layout.reset();
   142	        self.instrs.reset();
   143	        self.operands.clear();
   144	        self.immediates.clear();
   145	    }
   146	}
   147	
   148	impl WasmTranslator<'_> for FuncTranslator {
   149	    type Allocations = FuncTranslatorAllocations;
   150	
   151	    fn setup(&mut self, _bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<bool, Error> {
   152	        Ok(false)
   153	    }
   154	
   155	    fn features(&self) -> WasmFeatures {
   156	        self.engine.config().wasm_features()
   157	    }
   158	
   159	    fn translate_locals(
   160	        &mut self,
   161	        amount: u32,
   162	        value_type: wasmparser::ValType,
   163	    ) -> Result<(), Error> {
   164	        let ty = WasmiValueType::from(value_type).into_inner();
   165	        self.register_locals(amount, ty)?;
   166	        Ok(())
   167	    }
   168	
   169	    fn finish_translate_locals(&mut self) -> Result<(), Error> {
   170	        // Note: must initialize function body `block` after registering all
   171	        //       function parameters and locals so that the function `block`
   172	        //       has proper knowledge of its position within the operands stack.
   173	        self.init_func_body_block()?;
   174	        Ok(())
   175	    }
   176	
   177	    fn update_pos(&mut self, _pos: usize) {}
   178	
   179	    fn finish(
   180	        mut self,
   181	        finalize: impl FnOnce(CompiledFuncEntity),
   182	    ) -> Result<Self::Allocations, Error> {
   183	        // Note: `update_branch_offsets` might change `frame_size` so we need to compute it prior.
   184	        //
   185	        // Context:
   186	        // This only happens if the function has so many instructions that some conditional branch
   187	        // operators need to be encoded as their fallbacks which requires to allocate more function
   188	        // local constant values, thus increasing the size of the function frame.
   189	        self.instrs.update_branch_offsets()?;
   190	        let Some(frame_size) = self.frame_size() else {
   191	            return Err(Error::from(TranslationError::AllocatedTooManySlots));
   192	        };
   193	        finalize(CompiledFuncEntity::new(
   194	            frame_size,
   195	            self.instrs.encoded_ops(),
   196	        ));
   197	        Ok(self.into_allocations())
   198	    }
   199	}
   200	
   201	impl ReusableAllocations for FuncTranslator {
   202	    type Allocations = FuncTranslatorAllocations;
   203	
   204	    fn into_allocations(self) -> Self::Allocations {
   205	        Self::Allocations {
   206	            stack: self.stack.into_allocations(),
   207	            locals: self.locals,
   208	            layout: self.layout,
   209	            instrs: self.instrs.into_allocations(),
   210	            operands: self.operands,
   211	            immediates: self.immediates,
   212	        }
   213	    }
   214	}
   215	
   216	impl FuncTranslator {
   217	    /// Creates a new [`FuncTranslator`].
   218	    pub fn new(
   219	        func: FuncIdx,
   220	        module: ModuleHeader,
   221	        alloc: FuncTranslatorAllocations,
   222	    ) -> Result<Self, Error> {
   223	        let Some(engine) = module.engine().upgrade() else {
   224	            panic!(
   225	                "cannot compile function since engine does no longer exist: {:?}",
   226	                module.engine()
   227	            )
   228	        };
   229	        let FuncTranslatorAllocations {
   230	            stack,

END STEP

    76	/// A WebAssembly (Wasm) function translator.
    77	pub trait WasmTranslator<'parser>:
    78	    VisitOperator<'parser, Output = Result<(), Error>> + VisitSimdOperator<'parser>
    79	{
    80	    /// The reusable allocations required by the [`WasmTranslator`].
    81	    ///
    82	    /// # Note
    83	    ///
    84	    /// Those allocations can be cached on the caller side for reusability
    85	    /// in order to avoid frequent memory allocations and deallocations.
    86	    type Allocations: Default;
    87	
    88	    /// Sets up the translation process for the Wasm `bytes` and Wasm `module` header.
    89	    ///
    90	    /// - Returns `true` if the [`WasmTranslator`] is done with the translation process.
    91	    /// - Returns `false` if the [`WasmTranslator`] demands the translation driver to
    92	    ///   proceed with the process of parsing the Wasm module and feeding parse pieces
    93	    ///   to the [`WasmTranslator`].
    94	    ///
    95	    /// # Note
    96	    ///
    97	    /// - This method requires `bytes` to be the slice of bytes that make up the entire
    98	    ///   Wasm function body (including local variables).
    99	    /// - Also `module` must be a reference to the Wasm module header that is going to be
   100	    ///   used for translation of the Wasm function body.
   101	    fn setup(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<bool, Error>;
   102	
   103	    /// Returns a reference to the [`WasmFeatures`] used by the [`WasmTranslator`].
   104	    fn features(&self) -> WasmFeatures;
   105	
   106	    /// Translates the given local variables for the translated function.
   107	    fn translate_locals(
   108	        &mut self,
   109	        amount: u32,
   110	        value_type: wasmparser::ValType,
   111	    ) -> Result<(), Error>;
   112	
   113	    /// Informs the [`WasmTranslator`] that the Wasm function header translation is finished.
   114	    ///
   115	    /// # Note
   116	    ///
   117	    /// - After this function call no more locals and parameters may be registered
   118	    ///   to the [`WasmTranslator`] via [`WasmTranslator::translate_locals`].
   119	    /// - After this function call the [`WasmTranslator`] expects its [`VisitOperator`]
   120	    ///   trait methods to be called for translating the Wasm operators of the
   121	    ///   translated function.
   122	    ///
   123	    /// # Dev. Note
   124	    ///
   125	    /// This got introduced to properly calculate the fuel costs for all local variables
   126	    /// and function parameters.
   127	    fn finish_translate_locals(&mut self) -> Result<(), Error>;
   128	
   129	    /// Updates the [`WasmTranslator`] about the current byte position within translated Wasm binary.
   130	    ///
   131	    /// # Note
   132	    ///
   133	    /// This information is mainly required for properly locating translation errors.
   134	    fn update_pos(&mut self, pos: usize);
   135	
   136	    /// Finishes constructing the Wasm function translation.
   137	    ///
   138	    /// # Note
   139	    ///
   140	    /// - Initialized the [`EngineFunc`] in the [`Engine`].
   141	    /// - Returns the allocations used for translation.
   142	    fn finish(self, finalize: impl FnOnce(CompiledFuncEntity)) -> Result<Self::Allocations, Error>;
   143	}
   144	
   145	impl<T> ValidatingFuncTranslator<T> {
   146	    /// Creates a new [`ValidatingFuncTranslator`].
   147	    pub fn new(validator: FuncValidator, translator: T) -> Result<Self, Error> {
   148	        Ok(Self {
   149	            pos: 0,
   150	            validator,
   151	            translator,
   152	        })
   153	    }
   154	
   155	    /// Returns the current position within the Wasm binary while parsing operators.
   156	    fn current_pos(&self) -> usize {
   157	        self.pos
   158	    }
   159	
   160	    /// Translates into Wasmi bytecode if the current code path is reachable.
   161	    fn validate_then_translate<Validate, Translate>(
   162	        &mut self,
   163	        validate: Validate,
   164	        translate: Translate,
   165	    ) -> Result<(), Error>
   166	    where
   167	        Validate: FnOnce(&mut FuncValidator) -> Result<(), BinaryReaderError>,
   168	        Translate: FnOnce(&mut T) -> Result<(), Error>,
   169	    {
   170	        validate(&mut self.validator)?;
   171	        translate(&mut self.translator)?;
   172	        Ok(())
   173	    }
   174	}
   175	
   176	impl<'parser, T> WasmTranslator<'parser> for ValidatingFuncTranslator<T>
   177	where
   178	    T: WasmTranslator<'parser>,
   179	{
   180	    type Allocations = ReusableAllocations<T::Allocations>;
   181	
   182	    fn setup(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<bool, Error> {
   183	        self.translator.setup(bytes)?;
   184	        // Note: Wasm validation always need to be driven, therefore returning `Ok(false)`
   185	        //       even if the underlying Wasm translator does not need a translation driver.
   186	        Ok(false)
   187	    }
   188	
   189	    fn features(&self) -> WasmFeatures {
   190	        self.translator.features()
   191	    }
   192	
   193	    fn translate_locals(
   194	        &mut self,
   195	        amount: u32,
   196	        value_type: wasmparser::ValType,
   197	    ) -> Result<(), Error> {
   198	        self.validator
   199	            .define_locals(self.current_pos(), amount, value_type)?;
   200	        self.translator.translate_locals(amount, value_type)?;
   201	        Ok(())
   202	    }
   203	
   204	    fn finish_translate_locals(&mut self) -> Result<(), Error> {
   205	        self.translator.finish_translate_locals()?;
   206	        Ok(())
   207	    }
   208	
   209	    fn update_pos(&mut self, pos: usize) {
   210	        self.pos = pos;
   211	    }
   212	
   213	    fn finish(
   214	        mut self,
   215	        finalize: impl FnOnce(CompiledFuncEntity),
   216	    ) -> Result<Self::Allocations, Error> {
   217	        let pos = self.current_pos();
   218	        self.validator.finish(pos)?;
   219	        let translation = self.translator.finish(finalize)?;
   220	        let validation = self.validator.into_allocations();
   221	        let allocations = ReusableAllocations {
   222	            translation,
   223	            validation,
   224	        };
   225	        Ok(allocations)
   226	    }
   227	}

returncode=0
use super::Reset;
use crate::{Error, ValType, engine::TranslationError};
use alloc::vec::Vec;
use core::{cmp, iter};

/// A local variable index.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct LocalIdx(u32);

impl From<u32> for LocalIdx {
    fn from(index: u32) -> Self {
        Self(index)
    }
}

impl From<LocalIdx> for u32 {
    fn from(index: LocalIdx) -> Self {
        index.0
    }
}

/// Stores definitions of locals.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone)]
pub struct LocalsRegistry {
    /// The types of the first defined local variables.
    tys_first: Vec<ValType>,
    /// The types of the remaining defined local variables.
    tys_remaining: Vec<LocalGroup>,
    /// The number of registered locals.
    len_locals: usize,
}

impl Reset for LocalsRegistry {
    fn reset(&mut self) {
        self.tys_first.clear();
        self.tys_remaining.clear();
        self.len_locals = 0;
    }
}

impl LocalsRegistry {
    /// Returns the number of registered local variables in `self`.
    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
        self.len_locals
    }

    /// The maximum number of local variables per function.
    const LOCAL_VARIABLES_MAX: usize = 30_000;

    /// The maximum number of local variables in the fast `tys_first` vector.
    const FIRST_TYS_MAX: usize = 100;

    /// Slots `amount` of locals of type `ty` for `self`.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If too many locals are registered.
    pub fn register(&mut self, amount: usize, ty: ValType) -> Result<(), Error> {
        if amount == 0 {
            return Ok(());
        }
        if self.len().saturating_add(amount) > Self::LOCAL_VARIABLES_MAX {
            return Err(Error::from(TranslationError::TooManyFunctionParams));
        }
        let vacant_first = Self::FIRST_TYS_MAX.saturating_sub(self.tys_first.len());
        let push_to_first = cmp::min(vacant_first, amount);
        self.tys_first.extend(iter::repeat_n(ty, push_to_first));
        let remaining_amount = amount - push_to_first;
        let remaining_index = (self.len() + amount - 1) as u32;
        if remaining_amount > 0 {
            self.tys_remaining
                .push(LocalGroup::new(remaining_index, ty));
        }
        self.len_locals += amount;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Converts `index` into a `usize` value.
    fn local_idx_to_index(index: LocalIdx) -> usize {
        let index = u32::from(index);
        let Ok(index) = usize::try_from(index) else {
            panic!("out of bounds `LocalIdx`: {index}")
        };
        index
    }

    /// Returns the type of the local variable at `index` if any.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If `index` is out of bounds and does not refer to a local in `self`.
    pub fn ty(&self, index: LocalIdx) -> ValType {
        let index_sz = Self::local_idx_to_index(index);
        match self.tys_first.get(index_sz) {
            Some(ty) => *ty,
            None => self
                .ty_slow(index)
                .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("out of bounds local index: {index:?}")),
        }
    }

    /// Returns the type of the local variable at `index` if any.
    ///
    /// This is the slow-path for local variables that have been stored in the `remaining` buffer.
    #[cold]
    fn ty_slow(&self, index: LocalIdx) -> Option<ValType> {
        if self.tys_remaining.is_empty() {
            return None;
        }
        match self
            .tys_remaining
            .binary_search_by_key(&index.0, LocalGroup::max_index)
        {
            Err(i) if i == self.tys_remaining.len() => None,
            Ok(i) | Err(i) => Some(self.tys_remaining[i].ty()),
        }
    }
}

/// A local group of one or more locals sharing a common type.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
struct LocalGroup {
    /// The local index of the first local in the group.
    max_index: u32,
    /// The shared type of the locals in the local group.
    ty: ValType,
}

impl LocalGroup {
    /// Creates a new [`LocalGroup`].
    fn new(max_index: u32, ty: ValType) -> Self {
        Self { max_index, ty }
    }

    /// Returns the maximum index of the local variables in the [`LocalGroup`].
    fn max_index(&self) -> u32 {
        self.max_index
    }

    /// Returns the [`ValType`] of the [`LocalGroup`].
    fn ty(&self) -> ValType {
        self.ty
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn ty_works() {
        let mut locals = LocalsRegistry::default();
        for locals_per_type in [1, 2, 10, 100] {
            locals.reset();
            let tys = [ValType::I32, ValType::I64, ValType::F32, ValType::F64];
            for ty in tys {
                locals.register(locals_per_type, ty).unwrap();
            }
            assert_eq!(locals.len(), locals_per_type * tys.len());
            for i in 0..locals.len() {
                assert_eq!(locals.ty(LocalIdx(i as u32)), tys[i / locals_per_type]);
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn locals_followed_by_groups() {
        let mut locals = LocalsRegistry::default();
        let len_single = [1, 10, 100];
        let len_groups = [1, 10, 100];
        let locals_per_group = [10, 100];
        for len_single in len_single {
            for len_groups in len_groups {
                for locals_per_group in locals_per_group {
                    locals.reset();
                    let len_locals = len_single + (len_groups * locals_per_group);
                    for _ in 0..len_single {
                        locals.register(1, ValType::I32).unwrap();
                    }
                    for _ in 0..len_groups {
                        locals.register(locals_per_group, ValType::I64).unwrap();
                    }
                    for i in 0..len_locals {
                        let ty = match i < len_single {
                            true => ValType::I32,
                            false => ValType::I64,
                        };
                        assert_eq!(locals.ty(LocalIdx(i as u32)), ty);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

use alloc::vec::Vec;
use core::{slice, str};

/// Wasm custom sections.
#[derive(Default, Debug)]
pub struct CustomSections {
    inner: CustomSectionsInner,
}

impl CustomSections {
    /// Returns an iterator over the [`CustomSection`]s stored in `self`.
    #[inline]
    pub fn iter(&self) -> CustomSectionsIter<'_> {
        self.inner.iter()
    }
}

/// A builder for [`CustomSections`].
#[derive(Default, Debug)]
pub struct CustomSectionsBuilder {
    inner: CustomSectionsInner,
}

impl CustomSectionsBuilder {
    /// Pushes a new custom section segment to the [`CustomSectionsBuilder`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn push(&mut self, name: &str, data: &[u8]) {
        self.inner.push(name, data);
    }

    /// Finalize construction of the [`CustomSections`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn finish(self) -> CustomSections {
        CustomSections { inner: self.inner }
    }
}

/// Internal representation of [`CustomSections`].
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub struct CustomSectionsInner {
    /// The name and data lengths of each Wasm custom section.
    items: Vec<CustomSectionInner>,
    /// The combined name and data of all Wasm custom sections.
    names_and_data: Vec<u8>,
}

/// Internal representation of a Wasm [`CustomSection`].
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
pub struct CustomSectionInner {
    /// The length in bytes of the Wasm custom section name.
    len_name: usize,
    /// The length in bytes of the Wasm custom section data.
    len_data: usize,
}

impl CustomSectionsInner {
    /// Pushes a new custom section segment to the [`CustomSectionsBuilder`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn push(&mut self, name: &str, data: &[u8]) {
        let name_bytes = name.as_bytes();
        self.names_and_data.extend_from_slice(name_bytes);
        self.names_and_data.extend_from_slice(data);
        self.items.push(CustomSectionInner {
            len_name: name_bytes.len(),
            len_data: data.len(),
        })
    }

    /// Returns an iterator over the [`CustomSection`]s stored in `self`.
    #[inline]
    pub fn iter(&self) -> CustomSectionsIter<'_> {
        CustomSectionsIter {
            items: self.items.iter(),
            names_and_data: &self.names_and_data[..],
        }
    }
}

/// A Wasm custom section.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct CustomSection<'a> {
    /// The name of the custom section.
    name: &'a str,
    /// The undecoded data of the custom section.
    data: &'a [u8],
}

impl<'a> CustomSection<'a> {
    /// Returns the name or identifier of the [`CustomSection`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn name(&self) -> &'a str {
        self.name
    }

    /// Returns a shared reference to the data of the [`CustomSection`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn data(&self) -> &'a [u8] {
        self.data
    }
}

/// An iterator over the custom sections of a Wasm module.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct CustomSectionsIter<'a> {
    items: slice::Iter<'a, CustomSectionInner>,
    names_and_data: &'a [u8],
}

impl<'a> Iterator for CustomSectionsIter<'a> {
    type Item = CustomSection<'a>;

    #[inline]
    fn size_hint(&self) -> (usize, Option<usize>) {
        self.items.size_hint()
    }

    #[inline]
    fn next(&mut self) -> Option<Self::Item> {
        let item = self.items.next()?;
        let names_and_data = self.names_and_data;

use crate::{IndexType, MemoryError};

/// Internal memory type data and details.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct MemoryTypeInner {
    /// The initial or minimum amount of pages.
    minimum: u64,
    /// The optional maximum amount of pages.
    maximum: Option<u64>,
    /// The size of a page log2.
    page_size_log2: u8,
    /// The index type used to address a linear memory.
    index_type: IndexType,
}

/// A type to indicate that a size calculation has overflown.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
pub struct SizeOverflow;

impl MemoryTypeInner {
    /// Returns the minimum size, in bytes, that the linear memory must have.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the calculation of the minimum size overflows the maximum size.
    /// This means that the linear memory can't be allocated.
    /// The caller is responsible to deal with that situation.
    fn minimum_byte_size(&self) -> Result<u128, SizeOverflow> {
        let min = u128::from(self.minimum);
        if min > self.absolute_max() {
            return Err(SizeOverflow);
        }
        Ok(min << self.page_size_log2)
    }

    /// Returns the maximum size, in bytes, that the linear memory must have.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// If the maximum size of a memory type is not specified a concrete
    /// maximum value is returned dependent on the index type of the memory type.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the calculation of the maximum size overflows the index type.
    /// This means that the linear memory can't be allocated.
    /// The caller is responsible to deal with that situation.
    fn maximum_byte_size(&self) -> Result<u128, SizeOverflow> {
        match self.maximum {
            Some(max) => {
                let max = u128::from(max);
                if max > self.absolute_max() {
                    return Err(SizeOverflow);
                }
                Ok(max << self.page_size_log2)
            }
            None => Ok(self.max_size_based_on_index_type()),
        }
    }

    /// Returns the size of the linear memory pages in bytes.
    fn page_size(&self) -> u32 {
        debug_assert!(
            self.page_size_log2 == 16 || self.page_size_log2 == 0,
            "invalid `page_size_log2`: {}; must be 16 or 0",
            self.page_size_log2
        );
        1 << self.page_size_log2
    }

    /// Returns the maximum size in bytes allowed by the `index_type` of this memory type.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// - This does _not_ take into account the page size.
    /// - This is based _only_ on the index type used by the memory type.
    fn max_size_based_on_index_type(&self) -> u128 {
        self.index_type.max_size()
    }

    /// Returns the absolute maximum size in pages that a linear memory is allowed to have.
    fn absolute_max(&self) -> u128 {
        self.max_size_based_on_index_type() >> self.page_size_log2
    }
}

/// The memory type of a linear memory.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct MemoryType {
    inner: MemoryTypeInner,
}

/// A builder for [`MemoryType`]s.
///
/// Constructed via [`MemoryType::builder`] or via [`MemoryTypeBuilder::default`].
/// Allows to incrementally build-up a [`MemoryType`]. When done, finalize creation
/// via a call to [`MemoryTypeBuilder::build`].
pub struct MemoryTypeBuilder {
    inner: MemoryTypeInner,
}

impl Default for MemoryTypeBuilder {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            inner: MemoryTypeInner {
                minimum: 0,
                maximum: None,
                page_size_log2: MemoryType::DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE_LOG2,
                index_type: IndexType::I32,
            },
        }
    }
}

impl MemoryTypeBuilder {
    /// Set whether this is a 64-bit memory type or not.
    ///
    /// By default a memory is a 32-bit, a.k.a. `false`.
    ///
    /// 64-bit memories are part of the [Wasm `memory64` proposal].
    ///
    /// [Wasm `memory64` proposal]: https://github.com/WebAssembly/memory64
    pub fn memory64(&mut self, memory64: bool) -> &mut Self {
        self.inner.index_type = match memory64 {
            true => IndexType::I64,
            false => IndexType::I32,
        };
        self
    }

    /// Sets the minimum number of pages the built [`MemoryType`] supports.
    ///
    /// The default minimum is `0`.
    pub fn min(&mut self, minimum: u64) -> &mut Self {
        self.inner.minimum = minimum;
        self
    }

    /// Sets the optional maximum number of pages the built [`MemoryType`] supports.
    ///
    /// A value of `None` means that there is no maximum number of pages.
    ///
    /// The default maximum is `None`.
    pub fn max(&mut self, maximum: Option<u64>) -> &mut Self {
        self.inner.maximum = maximum;
        self
    }

    /// Sets the log2 page size in bytes, for the built [`MemoryType`].
    ///
    /// The default value is 16, which results in the default Wasm page size of 64KiB (aka 2^16 or 65536).
    ///
    /// Currently, the only allowed values are 0 (page size of 1) or 16 (the default).
    /// Future Wasm proposal extensions might change this limitation.
    ///
    /// Non-default page sizes are part of the [`custom-page-sizes proposal`]
    /// for WebAssembly which is not fully standardized yet.
    ///
    /// [`custom-page-sizes proposal`]: https://github.com/WebAssembly/custom-page-sizes
    pub fn page_size_log2(&mut self, page_size_log2: u8) -> &mut Self {
        self.inner.page_size_log2 = page_size_log2;
        self
    }

    /// Finalize the construction of the [`MemoryType`].
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the chosen configuration for the constructed [`MemoryType`] is invalid.
    pub fn build(self) -> Result<MemoryType, MemoryError> {
        self.validate()?;
        Ok(MemoryType { inner: self.inner })
    }

    /// Validates the configured [`MemoryType`] of the [`MemoryTypeBuilder`].
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the chosen configuration for the constructed [`MemoryType`] is invalid.
    fn validate(&self) -> Result<(), MemoryError> {
        match self.inner.page_size_log2 {
            0 | MemoryType::DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE_LOG2 => {}
            _ => {
                // Case: currently, pages sizes log2 can only be 0 or 16.
                // Note: Future Wasm extensions might allow more values.
                return Err(MemoryError::InvalidMemoryType);
            }
        }
        if self.inner.minimum_byte_size().is_err() {
            // Case: the minimum size overflows a `absolute_max`
            return Err(MemoryError::InvalidMemoryType);
        }
        if let Some(max) = self.inner.maximum {
            if self.inner.maximum_byte_size().is_err() {
                // Case: the maximum size overflows a `absolute_max`
                return Err(MemoryError::InvalidMemoryType);
            }
            if self.inner.minimum > max {
                // Case: maximum size must be at least as large as minimum size
                return Err(MemoryError::InvalidMemoryType);
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

impl MemoryType {
    /// The default memory page size in KiB.
    const DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE_LOG2: u8 = 16; // 2^16 KiB = 64 KiB

    /// Returns a [`MemoryTypeBuilder`] to incrementally construct a [`MemoryType`].
    pub fn builder() -> MemoryTypeBuilder {
        MemoryTypeBuilder::default()
    }

    /// Returns `true` if this is a 64-bit [`MemoryType`].
    ///
    /// 64-bit memories are part of the Wasm `memory64` proposal.
    pub fn is_64(&self) -> bool {
        self.index_ty().is_64()
    }

    /// Returns the [`IndexType`] used by the [`MemoryType`].
    pub fn index_ty(&self) -> IndexType {
        self.inner.index_type
    }

    /// Returns the minimum pages of the memory type.
    pub fn minimum(self) -> u64 {
        self.inner.minimum
    }

    /// Returns the maximum pages of the memory type.
    ///
    /// Returns `None` if there is no limit set.
    pub fn maximum(self) -> Option<u64> {
        self.inner.maximum
    }

    /// Returns the page size of the [`MemoryType`] in bytes.
    pub fn page_size(self) -> u32 {
        self.inner.page_size()
    }

    /// Returns the page size of the [`MemoryType`] in log2(bytes).
    pub fn page_size_log2(self) -> u8 {
        self.inner.page_size_log2
    }

    /// Returns the minimum size, in bytes, that the linear memory must have.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the calculation of the minimum size overflows the maximum size.
    /// This means that the linear memory can't be allocated.
    /// The caller is responsible to deal with that situation.
    pub(crate) fn minimum_byte_size(self) -> Result<u128, SizeOverflow> {
        self.inner.minimum_byte_size()
    }

    /// Returns the absolute maximum size in pages that a linear memory is allowed to have.
    pub(crate) fn absolute_max(&self) -> u128 {
        self.inner.absolute_max()
    }

    /// Returns `true` if the [`MemoryType`] is a subtype of the `other` [`MemoryType`].
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// This implements the [subtyping rules] according to the WebAssembly spec.
    ///
    /// [import subtyping]:
    /// https://webassembly.github.io/spec/core/valid/types.html#import-subtyping
    pub fn is_subtype_of(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
        if self.is_64() != other.is_64() {
            return false;
        }
        if self.page_size() != other.page_size() {
            return false;
        }
        if self.minimum() < other.minimum() {
            return false;
        }
        match (self.maximum(), other.maximum()) {
            (_, None) => true,
            (Some(max), Some(other_max)) => max <= other_max,
            _ => false,
        }
    }
}

use crate::{RawVal, TypedRawVal, ValType};
use core::{error::Error, fmt, fmt::Display, ptr::NonNull};

/// An error that may occur upon operating on global variables.
#[derive(Debug)]
#[non_exhaustive]
pub enum GlobalError {
    /// Occurs when trying to write to an immutable global variable.
    ImmutableWrite,
    /// Occurs when trying writing a value with mismatching type to a global variable.
    TypeMismatch,
}

impl Error for GlobalError {}

impl Display for GlobalError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter) -> fmt::Result {
        let message = match self {
            Self::ImmutableWrite => "tried to write to immutable global variable",
            Self::TypeMismatch => "tried to write value of non-matching type to global variable",
        };
        write!(f, "{message}")
    }
}

/// The mutability of a global variable.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Mutability {
    /// The value of the global variable is a constant.
    Const,
    /// The value of the global variable is mutable.
    Var,
}

impl Mutability {
    /// Returns `true` if this mutability is [`Mutability::Const`].
    pub fn is_const(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Const)
    }

    /// Returns `true` if this mutability is [`Mutability::Var`].
    pub fn is_mut(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Var)
    }
}

/// The type of a global variable.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct GlobalType {
    /// The value type of the global variable.
    content: ValType,
    /// The mutability of the global variable.
    mutability: Mutability,
}

impl GlobalType {
    /// Creates a new [`GlobalType`] from the given [`ValType`] and [`Mutability`].
    pub fn new(content: ValType, mutability: Mutability) -> Self {
        Self {
            content,
            mutability,
        }
    }

    /// Returns the [`ValType`] of the global variable.
    pub fn content(&self) -> ValType {
        self.content
    }

    /// Returns the [`Mutability`] of the global variable.
    pub fn mutability(&self) -> Mutability {
        self.mutability
    }
}

/// A global variable entity.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Global {
    /// The current value of the global variable.
    value: RawVal,
    /// The type of the global variable.
    ty: GlobalType,
}

impl Global {
    /// Creates a new global variable with the given initial `value` and type `ty`.
    pub fn new(value: RawVal, ty: GlobalType) -> Self {
        Self { value, ty }
    }

    /// Returns the [`GlobalType`] of the global variable.
    pub fn ty(&self) -> GlobalType {
        self.ty
    }

    /// Sets a new value to the global variable.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// - If the [`Global`] is immutable.
    /// - If `new_value` does not match the type of the [`Global`].
    pub fn set(&mut self, new_value: TypedRawVal) -> Result<(), GlobalError> {
        if !self.ty().mutability().is_mut() {
            return Err(GlobalError::ImmutableWrite);
        }
        if self.ty().content() != new_value.ty() {
            return Err(GlobalError::TypeMismatch);
        }
        self.value = new_value.into();
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Returns the current [`TypedRawVal`] of the [`Global`].
    pub fn get(&self) -> TypedRawVal {
        TypedRawVal::new(self.ty().content(), self.value)
    }

    /// Returns the current [`RawVal`] of the [`Global`].
    pub fn get_raw(&self) -> &RawVal {
        &self.value
    }

    /// Returns a pointer to the [`RawVal`] of the [`Global`].
    ///
    /// # Panics (Debug)
    ///
    /// If the underlying global value is immutable.
    pub fn get_raw_ptr(&mut self) -> NonNull<RawVal> {
        debug_assert!(matches!(self.ty.mutability(), Mutability::Var));
        NonNull::from(&mut self.value)
    }
}

    14	/// Runtime representation of a Wasm value.
    15	///
    16	/// Wasm code manipulate values of the four basic value types:
    17	/// integers and floating-point (IEEE 754-2008) data of 32 or 64 bit width each, respectively.
    18	///
    19	/// There is no distinction between signed and unsigned integer types. Instead, integers are
    20	/// interpreted by respective operations as either unsigned or signed in two’s complement representation.
    21	#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
    22	pub enum Val {
    23	    /// Value of 32-bit signed or unsigned integer.
    24	    I32(i32),
    25	    /// Value of 64-bit signed or unsigned integer.
    26	    I64(i64),
    27	    /// Value of 32-bit IEEE 754-2008 floating point number.
    28	    F32(F32),
    29	    /// Value of 64-bit IEEE 754-2008 floating point number.
    30	    F64(F64),
    31	    /// 128-bit Wasm `simd` proposal vector.
    32	    V128(V128),
    33	    /// A nullable [`Func`] reference.
    34	    FuncRef(Nullable<Func>),
    35	    /// A nullable [`ExternRef`] reference.
    36	    ExternRef(Nullable<ExternRef>),
    37	}

    40	    /// Create a [`Val`] from its raw parts.
    41	    pub(crate) fn from_raw_parts(val: RawVal, ty: ValType, store: impl AsStoreId) -> Self {
    42	        match ty {
    43	            ValType::I32 => Self::I32(val.into()),
    44	            ValType::I64 => Self::I64(val.into()),
    45	            ValType::F32 => Self::F32(val.into()),
    46	            ValType::F64 => Self::F64(val.into()),
    47	            #[cfg(feature = "simd")]
    48	            ValType::V128 => Self::V128(val.into()),
    49	            #[cfg(not(feature = "simd"))]
    50	            ValType::V128 => {
    51	                panic!("Val::from_raw_parts does not work for `v128` values without `simd`")
    52	            }
    53	            ValType::FuncRef => Self::FuncRef(<Nullable<Func>>::from_raw_parts(val.into(), store)),
    54	            ValType::ExternRef => {
    55	                Self::ExternRef(<Nullable<ExternRef>>::from_raw_parts(val.into(), store))
    56	            }
    57	        }
    58	    }
    59	
    60	    /// Unwraps [`Val`] into its underlying [`RawVal`].
    61	    ///
    62	    /// Returns `None` if `self` does not originate from `store`.
    63	    pub(crate) fn unwrap_raw_val(&self, store: impl AsStoreId) -> Option<RawVal> {
    64	        let value = match *self {
    65	            Self::I32(value) => value.into(),
    66	            Self::I64(value) => value.into(),
    67	            Self::F32(value) => value.into(),
    68	            Self::F64(value) => value.into(),
    69	            #[cfg(feature = "simd")]
    70	            Self::V128(value) => value.into(),
    71	            #[cfg(not(feature = "simd"))]
    72	            Self::V128(_) => {
    73	                unimplemented!("must enable `simd` crate feature to use `V128` values")
    74	            }
    75	            Self::FuncRef(value) => <Nullable<Func>>::unwrap_raw(&value, store)?.into(),
    76	            Self::ExternRef(value) => <Nullable<ExternRef>>::unwrap_raw(&value, store)?.into(),
    77	        };
    78	        Some(value)
    79	    }
    80	
    81	    /// Unwraps [`Val`] into its underlying [`RawRef`] if possible.
    82	    ///
    83	    /// # Note
    84	    ///
    85	    /// - Returns `None` if `self` is not a reference value.
    86	    /// - Returns `None` if `self` does not originate from `store`.
    87	    pub(crate) fn unwrap_raw_ref(&self, store: impl AsStoreId) -> Option<RawRef> {
    88	        let value = match *self {
    89	            Self::FuncRef(value) => <Nullable<Func>>::unwrap_raw(&value, store)?,
    90	            Self::ExternRef(value) => <Nullable<ExternRef>>::unwrap_raw(&value, store)?,
    91	            _ => return None,
    92	        };
    93	        Some(value)
    94	    }
    95	
    96	    /// Returns the underlying [`RawVal`] of `self` or `None`.
    97	    ///
    98	    /// Returns `None` if `self` is a non-null reference value.
    99	    pub(crate) fn as_raw_or_none(&self) -> Option<RawVal> {
   100	        let raw = match *self {
   101	            Self::I32(value) => value.into(),
   102	            Self::I64(value) => value.into(),
   103	            Self::F32(value) => value.into(),
   104	            Self::F64(value) => value.into(),
   105	            #[cfg(feature = "simd")]
   106	            Self::V128(value) => value.into(),
   107	            Self::FuncRef(Nullable::Null) => RawRef::null().into(),
   108	            Self::ExternRef(Nullable::Null) => RawRef::null().into(),
   109	            _ => return None,
   110	        };
   111	        Some(raw)
   112	    }
   113	
   114	    /// Creates new default value of given type.
   115	    #[inline]
   116	    #[deprecated(note = "use `Val::default_for_ty` instead")]
   117	    pub fn default(ty: ValType) -> Self {
   118	        Self::default_for_ty(ty)
   119	    }
   120	
   121	    /// Creates new default value of given type.
   122	    #[inline]
   123	    pub fn default_for_ty(ty: ValType) -> Self {
   124	        match ty {
   125	            ValType::I32 => Self::I32(0),
   126	            ValType::I64 => Self::I64(0),
   127	            ValType::F32 => Self::F32(0f32.into()),
   128	            ValType::F64 => Self::F64(0f64.into()),
   129	            ValType::V128 => Self::V128(V128::from(0_u128)),
   130	            ValType::FuncRef => Self::from(<Nullable<Func>>::Null),
   131	            ValType::ExternRef => Self::from(<Nullable<ExternRef>>::Null),
   132	        }
   133	    }
   134	
   135	    /// Get variable type for this value.
   136	    #[inline]
   137	    pub fn ty(&self) -> ValType {
   138	        match *self {
   139	            Self::I32(_) => ValType::I32,
   140	            Self::I64(_) => ValType::I64,
   141	            Self::F32(_) => ValType::F32,
   142	            Self::F64(_) => ValType::F64,
   143	            Self::V128(_) => ValType::V128,
   144	            Self::FuncRef(_) => ValType::FuncRef,
   145	            Self::ExternRef(_) => ValType::ExternRef,
   146	        }
   147	    }
   148	
   149	    /// Returns the underlying `i32` if the type matches otherwise returns `None`.
   150	    pub fn i32(&self) -> Option<i32> {
   151	        match self {
   152	            Self::I32(value) => Some(*value),
   153	            _ => None,
   154	        }
   155	    }
   156	
   157	    /// Returns the underlying `i64` if the type matches otherwise returns `None`.
   158	    pub fn i64(&self) -> Option<i64> {
   159	        match self {
   160	            Self::I64(value) => Some(*value),
   161	            _ => None,
   162	        }
   163	    }
   164	
   165	    /// Returns the underlying `f32` if the type matches otherwise returns `None`.
   166	    pub fn f32(&self) -> Option<F32> {
   167	        match self {
   168	            Self::F32(value) => Some(*value),
   169	            _ => None,
   170	        }
   171	    }
   172	
   173	    /// Returns the underlying `f64` if the type matches otherwise returns `None`.
   174	    pub fn f64(&self) -> Option<F64> {
   175	        match self {
   176	            Self::F64(value) => Some(*value),
   177	            _ => None,
   178	        }
   179	    }

     5	/// See [`Val`] for details.

     7	/// [`Val`]: enum.Value.html

     9	pub enum ValType {
    10	    /// 32-bit signed or unsigned integer.
    11	    I32,
    12	    /// 64-bit signed or unsigned integer.

    14	    /// 32-bit IEEE 754-2008 floating point number.

    24	}

    29	    /// This is `true` for [`ValType::I32`], [`ValType::I64`],
    30	    /// [`ValType::F32`] and [`ValType::F64`].
    31	    pub fn is_num(&self) -> bool {
    32	        matches!(self, Self::I32 | Self::I64 | Self::F32 | Self::F64)
    33	    }

    37	    /// This is `true` for [`ValType::FuncRef`] and [`ValType::ExternRef`].
    38	    pub fn is_ref(&self) -> bool {
    39	        matches!(self, Self::ExternRef | Self::FuncRef)
    40	    }

[OUTPUT_HEAD]
   740	    }
   741	
   742	    /// Grows the number of cells to `new_len` if the current number is less than `new_len`.
   743	    ///
   744	    /// Does nothing if the number of cells is already at least `new_len`.
   745	    ///
   746	    /// # Errors
   747	    ///
   748	    /// - Returns [`TrapCode::OutOfSystemMemory`] if the machine ran out of memory.
   749	    /// - Returns [`TrapCode::StackOverflow`] if this exceeds the stack's predefined limits.
   750	    fn grow_if_needed(&mut self, new_len: SpOffset) -> Result<(), TrapCode> {
   751	        let new_len = new_len.into_inner();
   752	        if new_len > self.max_height {
   753	            return Err(TrapCode::StackOverflow);
   754	        }
   755	        let capacity = self.cells.capacity();
   756	        let len = self.cells.len();
   757	        if new_len > capacity {
   758	            debug_assert!(
   759	                self.cells.len() <= self.cells.capacity(),
   760	                "capacity must always be larger or equal to the actual number of the cells"
   761	            );
   762	            let additional = new_len - len;
   763	            self.cells
   764	                .try_reserve(additional)
   765	                .map_err(|_| TrapCode::OutOfSystemMemory)?;
   766	            debug_assert!(
   767	                self.cells.capacity() >= new_len,
   768	                "capacity must now be at least as large as `new_len` ({new_len}) but found {}",
   769	                self.cells.capacity()
   770	            );
   771	        }
   772	        let max_len = cmp::max(new_len, len);
   773	        // Safety: there is no need to initialize the cells since we are operating
   774	        //         on `RawVal` which only has valid bit patterns.
   775	        // Note: non-security related initialization of function parameters
   776	        //       and zero-initialization of function locals happens elsewhere.
   777	        unsafe { self.cells.set_len(max_len) };
   778	        Ok(())
   779	    }
   780	
   781	    /// Prepares `self` for a host function tail call.
   782	    ///
   783	    /// # Note
   784	    ///
   785	    /// In the following code, `callee` represents the called host function frame
   786	    /// and `caller` represents the caller of the caller of the host function, a.k.a.
   787	    /// the caller's caller.
   788	    fn return_prepare_host_frame<'a>(
   789	        &'a mut self,
   790	        caller: Option<(Ip, SpOffset, Inst)>,
   791	        callee_start: SpOffset,
   792	        callee_params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   793	        results_len: u16,
   794	    ) -> Result<(ReturnCallHost, InOutParams<'a>), TrapCode> {
   795	        let caller_start = caller.map(|(_, start, _)| start).unwrap_or_default();
   796	        let params_offset = usize::from(u16::from(callee_params.span().head()));
   797	        let params_len = usize::from(callee_params.len());
   798	        let results_len = usize::from(results_len);
   799	        let callee_size = params_len.max(results_len);
   800	        if callee_size == 0 {
   801	            let sp = match caller {
   802	                Some(_) if caller_start != callee_start => self.sp(caller_start),
   803	                _ => Sp::dangling(),
   804	            };
   805	            let inout = InOutParams::new(&mut [], 0, 0).unwrap();
   806	            let control = match caller {
   807	                Some((ip, _, instance)) => ReturnCallHost::Continue((ip, sp, instance)),
   808	                None => ReturnCallHost::Break(sp),
   809	            };
   810	            return Ok((control, inout));
   811	        }
   812	        let params_start = callee_start.add(params_offset)?;
   813	        let params_end = params_start.add(params_len)?;
   814	        self.cells_copy_within(params_start..params_end, callee_start);
   815	        let callee_end = callee_start.add(callee_size)?;
   816	        self.grow_if_needed(callee_end)?;
   817	        let caller_sp = self.sp(caller_start);
   818	        let Some(cells) = self.cells_from_to(callee_start, callee_end) else {
   819	            unsafe { unreachable_unchecked!("must fit slice after `grow_if_needed` operation") }
   820	        };
   821	        let Ok(inout) = InOutParams::new(cells, params_len, results_len) else {
   822	            panic!("todo")
   823	        };
   824	        let control = match caller {
   825	            Some((ip, _, instance)) => ReturnCallHost::Continue((ip, caller_sp, instance)),
   826	            None => ReturnCallHost::Break(caller_sp),
   827	        };
   828	        Ok((control, inout))
   829	    }

   832	    fn prepare_host_frame<'a>(
   833	        &'a mut self,
   834	        caller_start: SpOffset,
   835	        callee_params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   836	        results_len: u16,
   837	    ) -> Result<(Sp, InOutParams<'a>), TrapCode> {
   838	        let params_offset = usize::from(u16::from(
[OUTPUT_TAIL]
n,
   878	    ) -> Result<Sp, TrapCode> {
   879	        let params_len = usize::from(callee_params.len());
   880	        let params_start = usize::from(u16::from(callee_params.span().head()));
   881	        let params_end = params_start.wrapping_add(params_len);
   882	        if callee_size == 0 {
   883	            return Ok(Sp::dangling());
   884	        }
   885	        let callee_end = callee_start.add(callee_size)?;
   886	        self.grow_if_needed(callee_end)?;
   887	        let Some(callee_cells) = self.cells_from(callee_start) else {
   888	            unsafe { unreachable_unchecked!("ValueStack::replace: out of bounds callee cells") }
   889	        };
   890	        callee_cells.copy_within(params_start..params_end, 0);
   891	        callee_cells[params_len..callee_size].fill_with(Cell::default);
   892	        let sp = self.sp(callee_start);
   893	        Ok(sp)
   894	    }

   898	        let start = start.into_inner();

   904	        let start = start.into_inner();
   905	        let end = end.into_inner();
   906	        self.cells.get_mut(start..end)
   907	    }

   909	    /// Copies cells from one part of the slice to another part of itself, using a `memmove`.
   910	    ///
   911	    /// # Panics
   912	    ///
   913	    /// If either `range` exceeds the end of the slice, or if the end of src is before the start.
   914	    fn cells_copy_within(&mut self, range: ops::Range<SpOffset>, dest: SpOffset) {
   915	        let start = range.start.into_inner();
   916	        let end = range.end.into_inner();
   917	        let dest = dest.into_inner();
   918	        self.cells.copy_within(start..end, dest);
   919	    }

   922	/// The Wasmi call stack.
   923	///
   924	/// This holds all the information about function frames that are on the call stack.
   925	/// Additionally it keeps track of the [`Inst`] that is currently in use.
   926	///
   927	/// # Note
   928	///
   929	/// - A [`CallStack`] has a maximum height which it cannot exceed.
   930	#[derive(Debug)]
   931	pub struct CallStack {
   932	    /// The stack of function frames.
   933	    frames: Vec<Frame>,
   934	    /// The currently used [`Inst`] if any.
   935	    ///
   936	    /// This may be `None`, for example if the [`CallStack`] is empty.
   937	    instance: Option<Inst>,
   938	    /// The maximum height of the call stack.
   939	    max_height: usize,
   940	}
   941	
   942	impl CallStack {
   943	    /// Creates a new [`CallStack`] with the given maximum height.
   944	    fn new(max_height: usize) -> Self {
   945	        Self {
   946	            frames: Vec::new(),
   947	            instance: None,
   948	            max_height,
   949	        }
   950	    }
   951	
   952	    /// Returns the number of heap allocated bytes of `self`.
   953	    ///
   954	    /// # Note
   955	    ///
   956	    /// This is mostly used to separate instances with and without heap allocations for caching.
   957	    fn bytes_allocated(&self) -> usize {
   958	        let bytes_per_frame = mem::size_of::<Frame>();
   959	        self.frames.capacity() * bytes_per_frame
   960	    }
   961	
   962	    /// Creates an empty [`CallStack`] which uses no heap allocations.
   963	    fn empty() -> Self {
   964	        Self::new(0)
   965	    }
   966	
   967	    /// Resets `self` for reuse.
   968	    fn reset(&mut self) {
   969	        self.frames.clear();
   970	        self.instance = None;
   971	    }
   972	
   973	    /// Returns the `start` index of the top-most function frame.
   974	    ///
   975	    /// Returns 0 if `self` is empty.
   976	    fn top_start(&self) -> SpOffset {
   977	        let Some(top) = self.top() else {
   978	            return SpOffset::default();
   979	        };
   980	        top.start
   981	    }
   982	
   983	    /// Returns a shared reference to the top-most function frame if any.
   984	    ///
   985	    /// Returns `None` if `self` is empty.
   986	    fn top(&self) -> Option<&Frame> {
   987	        self.frames.last()
   988	    }
   989	
   990	    /// Synchronizes the [`Ip`] of the top-most function frame.
   991	    ///
   992	    /// # Note
   993	    ///
   994	    /// - Usually the current [`Ip`] is stored outside of the [`CallStack`].
   995	    /// - Synchronization is required when calling another function or when
   996	    ///   finishing a resumable call in order to be able to resume execution
   997	    ///   at that point later.

    12	#[inline]

    14	    match ty {

    21	/// Returns the number of Wasmi engine cell slots required to represent a slice of [`ValType`] `tys`.
    22	#[inline]
    23	pub fn required_cells_for_tys(tys: &[ValType]) -> Result<u16, Error> {
    24	    let len_cells: usize = tys

    89	    /// Slot `amount` local variables of common type `ty`.
    90	    ///
    91	    /// # Errors
    92	    ///
    93	    /// If too many local variables are being registered.
    94	    pub fn register_locals(&mut self, amount: usize, ty: ValType) -> Result<(), Error> {
    95	        let cells_per_local = required_cells_for_ty(ty);
    96	        let err_too_many_slots = || Error::from(TranslationError::AllocatedTooManySlots);
    97	        let delta_offset = amount
    98	            .checked_mul(usize::from(cells_per_local))
    99	            .ok_or_else(err_too_many_slots)?;
   100	        usize::from(self.min_temp_offset)
   101	            .checked_add(delta_offset)
   102	            .filter(|&new_max| new_max <= usize::from(u16::MAX))
   103	            .ok_or_else(err_too_many_slots)?;
   104	        for _ in 0..amount {
   105	            self.local_offsets.push(self.min_temp_offset);
   106	            self.min_temp_offset += cells_per_local;
   107	        }
   108	        Ok(())
   109	    }

   234	    pub fn push_local(
   235	        &mut self,
   236	        local_index: LocalIdx,
   237	        ty: ValType,
   238	    ) -> Result<LocalOperand, Error> {
   239	        let stack_pos = self.next_stack_pos();
   240	        let next_local = self.local_heads.replace_first(local_index, Some(stack_pos));
   241	        if let Some(next_local) = next_local {
   242	            self.update_prev_local(next_local, Some(stack_pos));
   243	        }
   244	        let temp_slots = self.push_temp_offset(required_cells_for_ty(ty))?;
   245	        self.operands.push(StackOperand::Local {
   246	            temp_slots,
   247	            ty,
   248	            local_index,
   249	            prev_local: None,
   250	            next_local,
   251	        });
   252	        self.len_locals += 1;
   253	        Ok(LocalOperand::new(temp_slots, ty, local_index))
   254	    }

   287	        });
   288	        Ok(ImmediateOperand::new(temp_slots, ty, val))
   289	    }
   290	
   291	    /// Returns an iterator that yields the last `n` [`Operand`]s.
   292	    ///
   293	    /// # Panics
   294	    ///
   295	    /// If `n` is out of bounds for `self`.
   296	    pub fn peek(&self, n: usize) -> PeekedOperands<'_> {
   297	        let len_operands = self.operands.len();
   298	        let first_index = len_operands - n;
   299	        let Some(operands) = self.operands.get(first_index..) else {
   300	            return PeekedOperands::empty();
   301	        };
   302	        PeekedOperands {
   303	            stack_pos: first_index,
   304	            operands: operands.iter(),
   305	        }
   306	    }
   307	
   308	    /// Pops the top-most [`StackOperand`] from `self` if any.
   309	    ///
   310	    /// # Panics
   311	    ///
   312	    /// If `self` is empty.
   313	    #[inline]
   314	    pub fn pop(&mut self) -> Operand {
   315	        let Some(operand) = self.operands.pop() else {
   316	            panic!("tried to pop operand from empty stack");
   317	        };
   318	        self.pop_temp_offset(usize::from(required_cells_for_ty(operand.ty())))
   319	            .unwrap_or_else(|error| panic!("failed to pop temporary offset: {error}"));
   320	        let stack_pos = self.next_stack_pos();
   321	        self.try_unlink_local(operand);
   322	        Operand::new(stack_pos, operand)
   323	    }
   324	
   325	    /// Returns the [`Operand`] at `depth`.
   326	    ///
   327	    /// # Panics
   328	    ///
   329	    /// If `depth` is out of bounds for `self`.
   330	    #[inline]
   331	    pub fn get(&self, depth: usize) -> Operand {
   332	        let stack_pos = self.depth_to_stack_pos(depth);
   333	        let operand = self.get_at(stack_pos);
   334	        Operand::new(stack_pos, operand)
   335	    }
   336	
   337	    /// Returns the [`StackOperand`] at `index`.
   338	    ///
   339	    /// # Panics
   340	    ///

END STEP

    90	/// The Wasm operand (or value) stack.
    91	#[derive(Debug, Default)]
    92	pub struct OperandStack {
    93	    /// The current set of operands on the [`OperandStack`].
    94	    operands: Vec<StackOperand>,
    95	    /// Stores the first occurrences of every local variable on the [`OperandStack`] if any.
    96	    local_heads: LocalsHead,
    97	    /// The current number of local operands on the `operands` stack.
    98	    ///
    99	    /// This field is required to optimize [`OperandStack::preserve_all_locals`].
   100	    len_locals: usize,
   101	    /// The current top-most temporary stack offset.
   102	    ///
   103	    /// # Note
   104	    ///
   105	    /// - This is used and advanced for the next operand pushed to the stack.
   106	    /// - Upon popping an operand this offset is decreased.
   107	    temp_offset: u16,
   108	    /// The maximum recorded temporary stack offset.
   109	    max_offset: u16,
   110	}
   111	
   112	impl Reset for OperandStack {
   113	    fn reset(&mut self) {
   114	        self.operands.clear();
   115	        self.local_heads.reset();
   116	        self.len_locals = 0;
   117	        self.temp_offset = 0;
   118	        self.max_offset = 0;
   119	    }
   120	}
   121	
   122	impl OperandStack {
   123	    /// Slot `amount` local variables.
   124	    ///
   125	    /// # Errors
   126	    ///
   127	    /// If too many local variables are being registered.
   128	    pub fn register_locals(&mut self, amount: usize, ty: ValType) -> Result<(), Error> {
   129	        self.local_heads.register(amount)?;
   130	        let cells_per_item = required_cells_for_ty(ty);
   131	        let Ok(amount) = u16::try_from(amount) else {
   132	            return Err(Error::from(TranslationError::TooManyLocalVariables));
   133	        };
   134	        let required_cells = amount
   135	            .checked_mul(cells_per_item)
   136	            .ok_or_else(|| Error::from(TranslationError::AllocatedTooManySlots))?;
   137	        self.push_temp_offset(required_cells)?;
   138	        Ok(())
   139	    }
   140	
   141	    /// Pushes the offset for temporary operands by `delta`.
   142	    ///
   143	    /// Returns the temporary offset before this operation.
   144	    ///
   145	    /// # Errors
   146	    ///
   147	    /// Returns an error if the new temporary offset is out of bounds.
   148	    fn push_temp_offset(&mut self, delta: u16) -> Result<SlotSpan, Error> {
   149	        let old_offset = self.temp_offset;
   150	        self.temp_offset = old_offset
   151	            .checked_add(delta)
   152	            .ok_or_else(|| Error::from(TranslationError::AllocatedTooManySlots))?;
   153	        self.max_offset = self.max_offset.max(self.temp_offset);
   154	        Ok(SlotSpan::new(Slot::from(old_offset)))
   155	    }
   156	
   157	    /// Pops the offset for temporary operands by `delta`.
   158	    ///
   159	    /// # Panics
   160	    ///
   161	    /// If the temporary offset would drop below zero.
   162	    fn pop_temp_offset(&mut self, delta: usize) -> Result<(), Error> {
   163	        let Ok(delta) = u16::try_from(delta) else {
   164	            return Err(Error::from(TranslationError::AllocatedTooManySlots));
   165	        };
   166	        self.temp_offset = self.temp_offset.checked_sub(delta).unwrap_or_else(|| {
   167	            panic!(
   168	                "underflow in `pop_temp_offset`: temp_offset = {}, delta = {delta}",
   169	                self.temp_offset
   170	            )
   171	        });
   172	        Ok(())
   173	    }
   174	
   175	    /// Returns the current height of `self`
   176	    ///
   177	    /// # Note
   178	    ///
   179	    /// The height is equal to the number of [`StackOperand`]s on `self`.
   180	    pub fn height(&self) -> usize {
   181	        self.operands.len()
   182	    }
   183	
   184	    /// Returns the temporary [`Slot`] allocated for the next pushed operand.
   185	    pub fn next_temp_slots(&self) -> SlotSpan {

END STEP

use super::{CallHooks, StoreInner, typeid};
use crate::{
