/// A wrapper used to restore a [`PrunedStore`].
///
/// This wrapper exists to provide a `Debug` impl so that `#[derive(Debug)]`
/// works for [`Store`].
#[allow(clippy::type_complexity)]
#[derive(Copy, Clone)]
pub struct PrunedStoreVTable {
    /// Calls the host function with the `params` and `results` on `instance`.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the called host function returned an error.
    call_host_func: fn(
        store: &mut PrunedStore,
        trampoline: Trampoline,
        instance: Option<Inst>,
        inout: InOutParams,
        call_hooks: CallHooks,
    ) -> Result<(), StoreError<Error>>,
    /// Grows `memory` by `delta` pages.
    grow_memory:
        fn(&mut PrunedStore, memory: &Memory, delta: u64) -> Result<u64, StoreError<MemoryError>>,
    /// Grows `table` by `delta` items filling with `init`.
    grow_table: fn(
        &mut PrunedStore,
        table: &Table,
        delta: u64,
        init: RawRef,
    ) -> Result<u64, StoreError<TableError>>,
}
impl PrunedStoreVTable {
    pub fn new<T>() -> Self {
        Self {
            call_host_func: |pruned: &mut PrunedStore,
                             trampoline: Trampoline,
                             instance: Option<Inst>,
                             inout: InOutParams,
                             call_hooks: CallHooks|
             -> Result<(), StoreError<Error>> {
                let store: &mut Store<T> = pruned.restore()?;
                if matches!(call_hooks, CallHooks::Call) {
                    store
                        .invoke_call_hook(CallHook::CallingHost)
                        .map_err(StoreError::external)?;
                }
                store.call_host_func(trampoline, instance, inout)?;
                if matches!(call_hooks, CallHooks::Call) {
                    store
                        .invoke_call_hook(CallHook::ReturningFromHost)
                        .map_err(StoreError::external)?;
                }
                Ok(())
            },
            grow_memory: |pruned: &mut PrunedStore,
                          memory: &Memory,
                          delta: u64|
             -> Result<u64, StoreError<MemoryError>> {
                let store: &mut Store<T> = pruned.restore()?;
                let (store, mut resource_limiter) = store.store_inner_and_resource_limiter_ref();
                let (memory, fuel) = store.try_resolve_memory_and_fuel_mut(memory)?;
                memory
                    .grow(delta, Some(fuel), &mut resource_limiter)
                    .map_err(StoreError::external)
            },
            grow_table: |pruned: &mut PrunedStore,
                         table: &Table,
                         delta: u64,
                         init: RawRef|
             -> Result<u64, StoreError<TableError>> {
                let store: &mut Store<T> = pruned.restore()?;
                let (store, mut resource_limiter) = store.store_inner_and_resource_limiter_ref();
                let (table, fuel) = store.try_resolve_table_and_fuel_mut(table)?;
                table
                    .grow_raw(delta, init, Some(fuel), &mut resource_limiter)
                    .map_err(StoreError::external)
            },
        }
    }
}
impl PrunedStoreVTable {
    #[inline]
    fn call_host_func(
        &self,
        pruned: &mut PrunedStore,
        trampoline: Trampoline,
        instance: Option<Inst>,
        inout: InOutParams,
        call_hooks: CallHooks,
    ) -> Result<(), StoreError<Error>> {
        (self.call_host_func)(pruned, trampoline, instance, inout, call_hooks)
    }

    #[inline]
    fn grow_memory(
        &self,
        pruned: &mut PrunedStore,
        memory: &Memory,
        delta: u64,
    ) -> Result<u64, StoreError<MemoryError>> {
        (self.grow_memory)(pruned, memory, delta)
    }

    #[inline]
    fn grow_table(
        &self,
        pruned: &mut PrunedStore,
        table: &Table,
        delta: u64,
        init: RawRef,
    ) -> Result<u64, StoreError<TableError>> {
        (self.grow_table)(pruned, table, delta, init)
    }
}
impl Debug for PrunedStoreVTable {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "RestorePrunedWrapper")
    }
}

/// A [`Store`] with a pruned `T`.
#[derive(Debug)]
#[repr(transparent)]
pub struct PrunedStore {
    /// The underlying [`Store`] with pruned type signature.
    pruned: Store<Pruned>,
}

/// Placeholder type of `T` for a pruned `Store<T>`.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Pruned;

impl<'a, T> From<&'a mut Store<T>> for &'a mut PrunedStore {
    #[inline]
    fn from(store: &'a mut Store<T>) -> Self {
        // Safety: the generic `Store<T>` has its `T` pruned here.
        //
        // - This is safe because we are operating on a `&mut Store<T>` thus it is just
        //   a reference and since `Store<T>` and `Store<Pruned>` have the same size and API.
        // - We make sure in `PrunedStore` to never access the typed parts of the original
        //   `Store<T>` and check in the restoration process the type-ID of the target `T`.
        // - `Store<T>` has the same size and alignment for all `T`.
        unsafe { mem::transmute::<&'a mut Store<T>, &'a mut PrunedStore>(store) }
    }
}

impl<T> Store<T> {
    /// Prune the [`Store`] from `T` returning a [`PrunedStore`].
    #[inline]
    pub(crate) fn prune(&mut self) -> &mut PrunedStore {
        self.into()
    }
}

impl PrunedStore {
    /// Returns a shared reference to the underlying [`StoreInner`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn inner(&self) -> &StoreInner {
        &self.pruned.inner
    }

    /// Returns an exclusive reference to the underlying [`StoreInner`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn inner_mut(&mut self) -> &mut StoreInner {
        &mut self.pruned.inner
    }

    /// Calls a host `func` at `instance` with `params_results` buffer.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the host function returns an error.
    #[inline]
    pub fn call_host_func(
        &mut self,
        trampoline: Trampoline,
        instance: Option<Inst>,
        inout: InOutParams,
        call_hooks: CallHooks,
    ) -> Result<(), StoreError<Error>> {
        self.pruned
            .restore_pruned
            .clone()
            .call_host_func(self, trampoline, instance, inout, call_hooks)
    }

    /// Grows the [`Memory`] by `delta` pages and returns the result.
    #[inline]
    pub fn grow_memory(
        &mut self,
        memory: &Memory,
        delta: u64,
    ) -> Result<u64, StoreError<MemoryError>> {
        self.pruned
            .restore_pruned
            .clone()
            .grow_memory(self, memory, delta)
    }

    /// Grows the [`Table`] by `delta` items and returns the result.
    #[inline]
    pub fn grow_table(
        &mut self,
        table: &Table,
        delta: u64,
        init: RawRef,
    ) -> Result<u64, StoreError<TableError>> {
        self.pruned
            .restore_pruned
            .clone()
            .grow_table(self, table, delta, init)
    }

    /// Restores `self` to a proper [`Store<T>`] if possible.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// If the `T` of the resulting [`Store<T>`] does not match the given `T`.
    #[inline]
    fn restore<T>(&mut self) -> Result<&mut Store<T>, InternalStoreError> {
        if hint::unlikely(typeid::of::<T>() != self.pruned.id) {
            return Err(InternalStoreError::restore_type_mismatch::<T>());
        }
        let store = {
            // Safety: we restore the original `Store<T>` from the pruned `Store<Pruned>`.
            //
            // This is safe because we have already checked above that the `TypedId` of `T`
            // matches the `id` of the original `Store<T>` and thus the `T`'s are identical.
            //
            // Furthermore, we are only operating on `&mut` pointers and not values.
            // Finally, `Store<T>` has the same size and alignment for all `T`.
            unsafe { mem::transmute::<&mut PrunedStore, &mut Store<T>>(self) }
        };
        Ok(store)
    }
}

#[test]
fn pruning_works() {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let pruned = store.prune();
    assert!(pruned.restore::<()>().is_ok());
}

#[test]
fn pruning_errors() {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let pruned = store.prune();
    assert!(pruned.restore::<i32>().is_err());
}

#[test]
fn pruned_store_deref() {
    let mut config = crate::Config::default();
    config.consume_fuel(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let fuel_amount = 100;
    store.set_fuel(fuel_amount).unwrap();
    let pruned = store.prune();
    assert_eq!(
        PrunedStore::inner(pruned).fuel.get_fuel().unwrap(),
        fuel_amount
    );
    PrunedStore::inner_mut(pruned)
        .fuel
        .set_fuel(fuel_amount * 2)
        .unwrap();
    assert_eq!(
        PrunedStore::inner(pruned).fuel.get_fuel().unwrap(),
        fuel_amount * 2
    );
}

#[test]
fn equal_size() {
    use super::TypedStoreInner;
    type SmallType = ();
    type BigType = [i64; 16];
    // Note: `TypedStore<T>` must be of the same size for all `T` so that
    //       `PrunedStore` works and is a safe abstraction.
    use core::mem::size_of;
    assert_eq!(size_of::<Store<SmallType>>(), size_of::<Store<BigType>>(),);
    assert_eq!(
        size_of::<TypedStoreInner<SmallType>>(),
        size_of::<TypedStoreInner<BigType>>(),
    );
}

END STEP

   655	macro_rules! define_panicking_getters {
   656	    (
   657	        $(
   658	            pub fn $getter:ident($receiver:ty, $( $param_name:ident: $param_ty:ty ),* $(,)? ) -> $ret_ty:ty = $try_getter:expr
   659	        );*
   660	        $(;)?
   661	    ) => {
   662	        $(
   663	            #[doc = ::core::concat!(
   664	                "Resolves `",
   665	                ::core::stringify!($ret_ty),
   666	                "` via [`",
   667	                ::core::stringify!($try_getter),
   668	                "`] panicking upon error."
   669	            )]
   670	            pub fn $getter(self: $receiver, $( $param_name: $param_ty ),*) -> $ret_ty {
   671	                match $try_getter(self, $($param_name),*) {
   672	                    ::core::result::Result::Ok(value) => value,
   673	                    ::core::result::Result::Err(error) => ::core::panic!(
   674	                        ::core::concat!(
   675	                            "failed to resolve stored",
   676	                            $( " ", ::core::stringify!($param_name), )*
   677	                            ": {}"
   678	                        ),
   679	                        error,
   680	                    )
   681	                }
   682	            }
   683	        )*
   684	    };
   685	}
   686	impl StoreInner {

//! Data structures specialized for usage in the Wasmi interpreter.
//!
//! All data structures provide an API that can be backed by both [`HashMap`] and [`BTreeMap`].
//! Users can choose which kind of backend to operate on via the `no-hash-maps` crate feature.
//!
//! # Provided Data Structures
//!
//! - [`Arena`]: typed arena for fast allocations and accesses
//! - [`DedupArena`]: typed arena that also deduplicates, based on either [`HashMap`] or [`BTreeMap`]
//! - [`ComponentVec`]: useful to add properties to entities stored in an [`Arena`] or [`DedupArena`]
//! - [`Map`]: generic set of values, based on either [`HashMap`] or [`BTreeMap`]
//! - [`Set`]: generic key-value mapping, based on either [`HashSet`] or [`BTreeSet`]
//! - [`StringInterner`]: stores and deduplicates strings efficiently, based on either [`HashSet`] or [`BTreeSet`]
//!
//! [`HashSet`]: https://docs.rs/hashbrown/0.15.0/hashbrown/struct.HashSet.html
//! [`HashMap`]: https://docs.rs/hashbrown/0.15.0/hashbrown/struct.HashMap.html
//! [`BTreeSet`]: std::collections::BTreeSet
//! [`BTreeMap`]: std::collections::BTreeMap

#![no_std]
#![warn(
    clippy::cast_lossless,
    clippy::missing_errors_doc,
    clippy::used_underscore_binding,
    clippy::redundant_closure_for_method_calls,
    clippy::type_repetition_in_bounds,
    clippy::inconsistent_struct_constructor,
    clippy::default_trait_access,
    clippy::map_unwrap_or,
    clippy::items_after_statements
)]

pub mod hash;

pub mod map;

};
//! Type definitions for a default map.

use core::{borrow::Borrow, fmt::Debug, hash::Hash, iter::FusedIterator, ops::Index};

#[cfg(all(
    feature = "hash-collections",
    not(feature = "prefer-btree-collections")
))]
mod detail {
    use crate::hash;
    use hashbrown::hash_map;

    pub type MapImpl<K, V> = hash_map::HashMap<K, V, hash::RandomState>;
    pub type EntryImpl<'a, K, V> = hash_map::Entry<'a, K, V, hash::RandomState>;
    pub type OccupiedEntryImpl<'a, K, V> = hash_map::OccupiedEntry<'a, K, V, hash::RandomState>;
    pub type VacantEntryImpl<'a, K, V> = hash_map::VacantEntry<'a, K, V, hash::RandomState>;
    pub type IterImpl<'a, K, V> = hash_map::Iter<'a, K, V>;
    pub type IterMutImpl<'a, K, V> = hash_map::IterMut<'a, K, V>;
    pub type IntoIterImpl<K, V> = hash_map::IntoIter<K, V>;
    pub type KeysImpl<'a, K, V> = hash_map::Keys<'a, K, V>;
    pub type ValuesImpl<'a, K, V> = hash_map::Values<'a, K, V>;
    pub type ValuesMutImpl<'a, K, V> = hash_map::ValuesMut<'a, K, V>;
    pub type IntoKeysImpl<K, V> = hash_map::IntoKeys<K, V>;
    pub type IntoValuesImpl<K, V> = hash_map::IntoValues<K, V>;
}

#[cfg(any(
    not(feature = "hash-collections"),
    feature = "prefer-btree-collections"
))]
mod detail {
    use alloc::collections::btree_map;

    pub type MapImpl<K, V> = btree_map::BTreeMap<K, V>;
    pub type EntryImpl<'a, K, V> = btree_map::Entry<'a, K, V>;
    pub type OccupiedEntryImpl<'a, K, V> = btree_map::OccupiedEntry<'a, K, V>;
    pub type VacantEntryImpl<'a, K, V> = btree_map::VacantEntry<'a, K, V>;
    pub type IterImpl<'a, K, V> = btree_map::Iter<'a, K, V>;
    pub type IterMutImpl<'a, K, V> = btree_map::IterMut<'a, K, V>;
    pub type IntoIterImpl<K, V> = btree_map::IntoIter<K, V>;
    pub type KeysImpl<'a, K, V> = btree_map::Keys<'a, K, V>;
    pub type ValuesImpl<'a, K, V> = btree_map::Values<'a, K, V>;
    pub type ValuesMutImpl<'a, K, V> = btree_map::ValuesMut<'a, K, V>;
    pub type IntoKeysImpl<K, V> = btree_map::IntoKeys<K, V>;
    pub type IntoValuesImpl<K, V> = btree_map::IntoValues<K, V>;
}

    87	        /// A static store index counter.
    88	        static CURRENT_STORE_IDX: AtomicU32 = AtomicU32::new(0);

   102	    ///
   103	    /// Otherwise returns `None`.
   104	    pub fn unwrap<T>(self, owned: EngineOwned<T>) -> Option<T> {
   105	        if self != owned.engine {
   106	            return None;
   107	        }
   108	        Some(owned.value)

//! The Wasmi virtual machine definitions.
//!
//! These closely mirror the WebAssembly specification definitions.
//! The overall structure is heavily inspired by the `wasmtime` virtual
//! machine architecture.
//!
//! # Example
//!
//! The following example shows a "Hello, World!"-like example of creating
//! a Wasm module from some initial `.wat` contents, defining a simple host
//! function and calling the exported Wasm function.
//!
//! The example was inspired by
//! [Wasmtime's API example](https://docs.rs/wasmtime/0.39.1/wasmtime/).
//!
//! ```
//! use wasmi::*;
//!
//! // In this simple example we are going to compile the below Wasm source,
//! // instantiate a Wasm module from it and call its exported "hello" function.
//! fn main() -> Result<(), wasmi::Error> {
//!     let wasm = r#"
//!         (module
//!             (import "host" "hello" (func $host_hello (param i32)))
//!             (func (export "hello")
//!                 (call $host_hello (i32.const 3))
//!             )
//!         )
//!     "#;
//!     // First step is to create the Wasm execution engine with some config.

integration
mod.rs
use crate::{Config, Engine, Error, Linker, Module, Store, TrapCode, WasmParams, WasmResults};
use core::{fmt::Debug, mem};

/// Wasmi execution test runner.
pub struct ExecutionTest<T> {
    /// The underlying engine that is tested.
    engine: Engine,
    /// The store data which is used for the calls.
    data: T,
    /// The compiled Wasm module that is used for the calls.
    module: Option<Module>,
}

impl Default for ExecutionTest<()> {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            engine: Engine::default(),
            data: (),
            module: None,
        }
    }
}

impl<T> ExecutionTest<T>
where
    T: Default + PartialEq + Eq + 'static,
{
    /// Creates a new [`ExecutionTest`] with default initialized data.
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            engine: Engine::default(),
            data: T::default(),
            module: None,
        }
    }

    /// Creates a new [`ExecutionTest`] with the given `config` and default initialized data.
    pub fn with_config(config: Config) -> Self {
        let engine = Engine::new(&config);
        let data = <T as Default>::default();
        Self {
            engine,
            data,
            module: None,
        }
    }

    /// Sets the Wasm source that this [`ExecutionTest`] uses for its calls.
    pub fn wasm(&mut self, wasm: &str) -> &mut Self {
        assert!(self.module.is_none(), "already provided Wasm input");
        let module = Module::new(&self.engine, wasm).unwrap();
        self.module = Some(module);
        self
    }

    /// Calls the function named `func_name` with `args` and returns the result.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If no Wasm source was set before.
    ///
    /// # Errors
    ///
    /// - If instantiation fails.
    /// - If starting the instance fails.
    /// - If there is no function named `func_name`.
    /// - If the call fails or traps.
    pub fn call<Args, Results>(&mut self, func_name: &str, args: Args) -> Result<Results, Error>
    where
        Args: WasmParams,
        Results: WasmResults,
    {
        let Some(module) = &self.module else {
            panic!("need Wasm before calling")
        };
        let data = mem::take(&mut self.data);
        let mut store = <Store<T>>::new(&self.engine, data);
        let linker = Linker::new(&self.engine);
        let results = linker
            .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, module)?
            .get_typed_func::<Args, Results>(&store, func_name)?
            .call(&mut store, args)?;
        _ = mem::replace(&mut self.data, store.into_data());
        Ok(results)
    }

    /// Sets the store data to `data`.
    pub fn data(&mut self, data: T) -> &mut Self {
        self.data = data;
        self
    }

    /// Asserts that the store data is as `expected`.
    pub fn assert_data(&mut self, expected: impl AsRef<T>) -> &mut Self {
        assert!(&self.data == expected.as_ref());
        self
    }
}

/// Convenience trait to assert that some call results are as expected.
pub trait AssertResults {
    /// The expected call result type.
    type Val;

    /// Panics if the `Result<Args, _>::Ok` value is not `expected`.
    fn assert_results(&self, expected: Self::Val);
}

impl<T, E> AssertResults for Result<T, E>
where
    T: PartialEq + Debug,
    Self: Debug,
{
    type Val = T;

    fn assert_results(&self, expected: T) {
        let results = match self {
            Ok(results) => results,
            Err(_) => panic!("must have Ok value but found: {self:?}"),
        };

END STEP

/// Number of times different callback events have fired.
#[derive(Default)]
struct CallHookTestState {
    calling_wasm: u32,
    returning_from_wasm: u32,
    calling_host: u32,
    returning_from_host: u32,
    erroneous_callback_invocation: bool,
}

fn test_setup() -> (Store<CallHookTestState>, Linker<CallHookTestState>) {
    let store = Store::default();
    let linker = <Linker<CallHookTestState>>::new(store.engine());
    (store, linker)
}

/// Prepares the test WAT and executes it. The wat defines two functions,
/// `wasm_fn_a` and `wasm_fn_b` and two imports, `host_fn_a` and `host_fn_b`.
/// `wasm_fn_a` calls `host_fn_a`, and `wasm_fn_b` calls `host_fn_b`.
/// None of the functions accept any arguments or return any value.
fn execute_wasm_fn_a(
    mut store: &mut Store<CallHookTestState>,
    linker: &mut Linker<CallHookTestState>,
) -> Result<(), Error> {
    let wasm = r#"
    (module
        (import "env" "host_fn_a" (func $host_fn_a))
        (import "env" "host_fn_b" (func $host_fn_b))
        (func (export "wasm_fn_a")
            (call $host_fn_a)
        )
        (func (export "wasm_fn_b")
            (call $host_fn_b)
        )
    )
    "#;
    let module = Module::new(store.engine(), wasm).unwrap();
    let instance = linker.instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module).unwrap();
    let wasm_fn = instance
        .get_export(store.as_context(), "wasm_fn_a")
        .and_then(Extern::into_func)
        .unwrap()
        .typed::<(), ()>(&store)
        .unwrap();

    wasm_fn.call(store.as_context_mut(), ())
}

#[test]
fn call_hooks_get_called() {
    let (mut store, mut linker) = test_setup();

    store.call_hook(
        |data: &mut CallHookTestState, hook_type: CallHook| -> Result<(), Error> {
            match hook_type {
                CallHook::CallingWasm => data.calling_wasm += 1,
                CallHook::ReturningFromWasm => data.returning_from_wasm += 1,
                CallHook::CallingHost => data.calling_host += 1,
                CallHook::ReturningFromHost => data.returning_from_host += 1,
            };

            Ok(())
        },
    );

returncode=0
//! Test to assert that host functions that call back into
//! Wasm works correctly.

use wasmi::{Caller, Engine, Extern, Func, Linker, Module, Store};

fn test_setup() -> (Store<()>, Linker<()>) {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let linker = <Linker<()>>::new(&engine);
    (store, linker)
}

#[test]
fn host_calls_wasm() {
    let (mut store, mut linker) = test_setup();
    let host_fn = Func::wrap(&mut store, |mut caller: Caller<()>, input: i32| -> i32 {
        let wasm_fn = caller
            .get_export("square")
            .and_then(Extern::into_func)
            .unwrap()
            .typed::<i32, i32>(&caller)
            .unwrap();
        wasm_fn.call(&mut caller, input + input).unwrap()
    });
    linker.define("env", "host_fn", host_fn).unwrap();
    let wasm = r#"
        (module
            (import "env" "host_fn" (func $host_fn (param i32) (result i32)))
            (func (export "wasm_fn") (param i32) (result i32)
                (call $host_fn (local.get 0))
            )
            (func (export "square") (param i32) (result i32)
                (i32.mul
                    (local.get 0)
                    (local.get 0)
                )
            )
        )
        "#;
    let module = Module::new(store.engine(), wasm).unwrap();
    let instance = linker.instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module).unwrap();
    let wasm_fn = instance
        .get_export(&store, "wasm_fn")
        .and_then(Extern::into_func)
        .unwrap()
        .typed::<i32, i32>(&store)
        .unwrap();
    let input = 5;
    let expected = (input + input) * (input + input);
    let result = wasm_fn.call(&mut store, input).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(result, expected);
}

     5	/// Configuration for an [`Engine`].
     6	///
     7	/// [`Engine`]: [`crate::Engine`]
     8	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
     9	pub struct Config {
    10	    /// The limits set on the value stack and call stack.
    11	    pub(crate) stack: StackConfig,
    12	    /// The Wasm features used when validating or translating functions.
    13	    features: WasmFeatures,
    14	    /// Is `true` if Wasmi executions shall consume fuel.
    15	    consume_fuel: bool,
    16	    /// Is `true` if Wasmi shall ignore Wasm custom sections when parsing Wasm modules.
    17	    ignore_custom_sections: bool,
    18	    /// The configured fuel costs of all Wasmi bytecode instructions.
    19	    fuel_costs: FuelCostsProvider,
    20	    /// The mode of Wasm to Wasmi bytecode compilation.
    21	    compilation_mode: CompilationMode,
    22	    /// Enforced limits for Wasm module parsing and compilation.
    23	    limits: EnforcedLimits,
    24	}
    25	
    26	/// The chosen mode of Wasm to Wasmi bytecode compilation.
    27	#[derive(Debug, Default, Copy, Clone)]
    28	pub enum CompilationMode {
    29	    /// The Wasm code is compiled eagerly to Wasmi bytecode.
    30	    Eager,
    31	    /// The Wasm code is validated eagerly and translated lazily on first use.
    32	    #[default]
    33	    LazyTranslation,
    34	    /// The Wasm code is validated and translated lazily on first use.
    35	    ///
    36	    /// # Note
    37	    ///
    38	    /// This mode must not be used if the result of Wasm execution
    39	    /// must be deterministic amongst multiple Wasm implementations.
    40	    Lazy,
    41	}
    42	
    43	impl Default for Config {
    44	    fn default() -> Self {
    45	        Self {
    46	            stack: StackConfig::default(),
    47	            features: Self::default_features(),
    48	            consume_fuel: false,
    49	            ignore_custom_sections: false,
    50	            fuel_costs: FuelCostsProvider::default(),
    51	            compilation_mode: CompilationMode::default(),
    52	            limits: EnforcedLimits::default(),
    53	        }
    54	    }
    55	}
    56	
    57	impl Config {
    58	    /// Returns the default [`WasmFeatures`].
    59	    fn default_features() -> WasmFeatures {
    60	        let mut features = WasmFeatures::empty();
    61	        features.set(WasmFeatures::MUTABLE_GLOBAL, true);
    62	        features.set(WasmFeatures::MULTI_VALUE, true);
    63	        features.set(WasmFeatures::MULTI_MEMORY, true);
    64	        features.set(WasmFeatures::SATURATING_FLOAT_TO_INT, true);
    65	        features.set(WasmFeatures::SIGN_EXTENSION, true);
    66	        features.set(WasmFeatures::BULK_MEMORY, true);
    67	        features.set(WasmFeatures::REFERENCE_TYPES, true);
    68	        features.set(WasmFeatures::GC_TYPES, true); // required by reference-types
    69	        features.set(WasmFeatures::TAIL_CALL, true);
    70	        features.set(WasmFeatures::EXTENDED_CONST, true);
    71	        features.set(WasmFeatures::FLOATS, true);
    72	        features.set(WasmFeatures::CUSTOM_PAGE_SIZES, false);
    73	        features.set(WasmFeatures::MEMORY64, true);
    74	        features.set(WasmFeatures::WIDE_ARITHMETIC, false);
    75	        features.set(WasmFeatures::SIMD, cfg!(feature = "simd"));
    76	        features.set(WasmFeatures::RELAXED_SIMD, cfg!(feature = "simd"));
    77	        features
    78	    }
    79

    81	    ///

    88	        self.stack.set_max_recursion_depth(value);

   322	    /// so that it consumes fuel as it executes. Once an execution runs out of fuel
   323	    /// a [`TrapCode::OutOfFuel`](crate::TrapCode::OutOfFuel) trap is raised.
   324	    /// This way users can deterministically halt or yield the execution of WebAssembly code.

   793	/// Meta information about a [`EngineFunc`].
   794	#[derive(Debug)]
   795	pub struct CompiledFuncEntity {
   796	    /// The sequence of [`Op`] of the [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
   797	    ops: Pin<Box<[u8]>>,
   798	    /// The number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`] in total.
   799	    ///
   800	    /// # Note
   801	    ///
   802	    /// This includes stack slots to store the function local constant values,
   803	    /// function parameters, function locals and dynamically used stack slots.
   804	    len_stack_slots: u16,
   805	}
   806	
   807	impl CompiledFuncEntity {
   808	    /// Create a new initialized [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
   809	    ///
   810	    /// # Panics
   811	    ///
   812	    /// - If `ops` is empty.
   813	    /// - If `ops` contains more than `i32::MAX` encoded bytes.
   814	    pub fn new(len_stack_slots: u16, ops: &[u8]) -> Self {
   815	        let ops: Pin<Box<[u8]>> = Pin::new(ops.into());
   816	        assert!(
   817	            !ops.is_empty(),
   818	            "compiled functions must have at least one instruction"
   819	        );
   820	        assert!(
   821	            // Generally, Wasmi has no issues with more than `i32::MAX` instructions.
   822	            // However, Wasmi's branch instructions can jump across at most `i32::MAX`
   823	            // forwards or `i32::MIN` instructions backwards and thus having more than
   824	            // `i32::MAX` instructions might introduce problems.
   825	            ops.len() <= i32::MAX as usize,
   826	            "compiled function has too many instructions: {}",
   827	            ops.len(),
   828	        );
   829	        Self {
   830	            ops,
   831	            len_stack_slots,
   832	        }
   833	    }
   834	}
   835	
   836	/// A shared reference to the data of a [`EngineFunc`].
   837	#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]
   838	pub struct CompiledFuncRef<'a> {
   839	    /// The sequence of encoded [`Op`]s of the [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
   840	    ops: Pin<&'a [u8]>,
   841	    /// The number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`] in total.
   842	    len_stack_slots: u16,
   843	}
   844	
   845	impl<'a> From<&'a CompiledFuncEntity> for CompiledFuncRef<'a> {
   846	    #[inline]
   847	    fn from(func: &'a CompiledFuncEntity) -> Self {
   848	        Self {
   849	            ops: func.ops.as_ref(),
   850	            len_stack_slots: func.len_stack_slots,
   851	        }
   852	    }
   853	}
   854	
   855	impl<'a> CompiledFuncRef<'a> {
   856	    /// Returns the sequence of encoded [`Op`]s of the [`EngineFunc`].
   857	    #[inline]
   858	    pub fn ops(&self) -> &'a [u8] {
   859	        self.ops.get_ref()
   860	    }
   861	
   862	    /// Returns the number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`].
   863	    #[inline]
   864	    pub fn len_stack_slots(&self) -> u16 {
   865	        self.len_stack_slots
   866	    }
   867	}

    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// This is used for coredump generation to map instruction pointers
    /// of call frames back to their compiled Wasm functions.
    pub fn find_func_by_ip<'a>(&'a self, ip: Ip) -> Option<(EngineFunc, CompiledFuncRef<'a>)> {
        let funcs = self.funcs.lock();
        for (func, entity) in funcs.iter() {
            let Some(cref) = entity.get_compiled() else {
                continue;
            };
            let start = cref.ops().as_ptr() as usize;
            let end = start + cref.ops().len();
            let ip_addr = ip.as_ptr() as usize;
            if ip_addr >= start && ip_addr < end {
                return Some((func, cref));
            }
        }
        None
    }

'''
s = s[:insert_at] + method + s[insert_at:]
p.write_text(s)
print("done")

   513	pub fn return_call_host(
   514	    state: &mut VmState,
   515	    func: Func,
   516	    host_func: HostFuncEntity,
   517	    params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   518	    instance: Inst,
   519	) -> Control<(Ip, Sp, Inst), Break> {
   520	    debug_assert_eq!(params.len(), host_func.len_param_cells());
   521	    let trampoline = *host_func.trampoline();
   522	    let (control, inout) = state
   523	        .stack
   524	        .return_prepare_host_frame(params, host_func.len_result_cells(), instance)
   525	        .into_control()?;
   526	    match state
   527	        .store
   528	        .call_host_func(trampoline, Some(instance), inout, CallHooks::Call)
   529	    {
   530	        Ok(()) => {}
   531	        Err(StoreError::External(error)) => {
   532	            // Note: we won't allow resumption in case the execution would
   533	            //       have returned with this the host function tail call.
   534	            let reason = match control {
   535	                Control::Continue(_) => DoneReason::host_error(error, func, params.span()),
   536	                Control::Break(_) => DoneReason::error(error),
   537	            };

   539	        }
   540	        Err(StoreError::Internal(error)) => unsafe {
   541	            unreachable_unchecked!(
   542	                "internal interpreter error while executing host function: {error}"
   543	            )
   544	        },
   545	    }
   546	    match control {
   547	        Control::Continue((ip, sp, instance)) => Control::Continue((ip, sp, instance)),
   548	        Control::Break(sp) => done!(state, DoneReason::Return(sp)),
   549	    }
   550	}

   552	pub fn call_wasm_or_host(
   553	    state: &mut VmState,
   554	    caller_ip: Ip,
   555	    func: Func,
   556	    params: BoundedSlotSpan,
   557	    mem0: Mem0Ptr,
   558	    mem0_len: Mem0Len,
   559	    instance: Inst,
   560	) -> Control<(Ip, Sp, Mem0Ptr, Mem0Len, Inst), Break> {
   561	    let func_entity = resolve_func(state.store, &func);
   562	    let next_state = match func_entity {
   563	        FuncEntity::Wasm(wasm_func) => {
   564	            let func = wasm_func.func_body();
   565	            let callee_instance = *wasm_func.instance();
   566	            let callee_instance: Inst = resolve_instance(state.store, &callee_instance).into();
   567	            let (callee_ip, callee_sp) =
   568	                call_wasm(state, caller_ip, params, func, Some(callee_instance))?;
   569	            let (instance, mem0, mem0_len) =
   570	                update_instance(state.store, instance, callee_instance, mem0, mem0_len);
   571	            (callee_ip, callee_sp, mem0, mem0_len, instance)
   572	        }
   573	        FuncEntity::Host(host_func) => {
   574	            let host_func = *host_func;
   575	            let sp = call_host(
   576	                state,
   577	                func,
   578	                Some(caller_ip),
   579	                host_func,
   580	                params,
   581	                Some(instance),
   582	                CallHooks::Call,
   583	            )?;
   584	            (caller_ip, sp, mem0, mem0_len, instance)
   585	        }
   586	    };
   587	    Control::Continue(next_state)
   588	}

//! Coredump generation for Wasmi.
//!
//! When coredump generation is enabled via
//! [`Config::generate_coredump`](crate::Config::generate_coredump) and a Wasm
//! trap occurs, the returned [`Error`](crate::Error) carries a serialized
//! coredump that post-mortem debugging tools can load.
//!
//! The coredump is a valid Wasm binary with the following custom sections:
//!
//! - `"core"`: executable name.
//! - `"coremodules"`: module names.
//! - `"coreinstances"`: instances with their memories and globals.
//! - `"corestack"`: stack frames with locals and operand stacks.
//!
//! The binary also contains regular Wasm memory, global and data sections
//! capturing the state of linear memories and globals at trap time.

use super::{
    CodeMap,
    executor::{Inst, Stack},
};
use crate::{
    GlobalType,
    ValType,
    collections::Map,
    core::{CoreMemoryType, RawVal, ReadAs},
    store::PrunedStore,
};
use alloc::{string::String, vec, vec::Vec};

/// A value captured for coredump generation.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub(crate) enum CoredumpValue {
    /// A 32-bit integer value.
    I32(i32),
    /// A 64-bit integer value.
    I64(i64),
    /// A 32-bit float value stored as its raw IEEE 754 bits.
    F32(u32),
    /// A 64-bit float value stored as its raw IEEE 754 bits.
    F64(u64),
    /// A value that could not be recovered.
    Unknown,
}

/// A module instance captured for coredump generation.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub(crate) struct CoredumpInstance {
    /// The raw pointer identity of the Wasm instance used for deduplication.
    key: usize,
    /// The index of the module within the `"coremodules"` section.
    module_index: u32,
    /// Indices into the coredump's memory index space.
    memory_indices: Vec<u32>,
    /// Indices into the coredump's global index space.
    global_indices: Vec<u32>,
}

/// A linear memory captured for coredump generation.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub(crate) struct CoredumpMemory {
    /// The raw pointer identity of the memory used for deduplication.
    key: usize,
    /// The type of the linear memory.
    ty: CoreMemoryType,
    /// The bytes of the linear memory at trap time.
    data: Vec<u8>,
}

/// A global variable captured for coredump generation.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub(crate) struct CoredumpGlobal {
    /// The raw pointer identity of the global used for deduplication.
    key: usize,
    /// The type of the global variable.
    ty: GlobalType,
    /// The value of the global variable at trap time.
    value: RawVal,
}

/// A stack frame captured for coredump generation.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub(crate) struct CoredumpFrame {
    /// The index of the instance within the `"coreinstances"` section.
    instance_index: u32,
    /// The Wasm function index of the function within its module.
    func_index: u32,
    /// The code offset into the function or `0` if not available.
    code_offset: u32,
    /// The values of the function parameters and local variables.
    locals: Vec<CoredumpValue>,
    /// The values on the operand stack of the function.
    operands: Vec<CoredumpValue>,
}

/// A Wasm coredump.
///
/// # Note
///
/// This is the structured representation used while constructing a coredump.
/// The final serialized bytes are stored in [`Coredump::bytes`].
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub(crate) struct Coredump {
    /// The executable name of the coredump.
    executable_name: String,
    /// The names of all modules in the coredump.
    modules: Vec<String>,
    /// All instances in the coredump.
    instances: Vec<CoredumpInstance>,
    /// All stack frames in the coredump ordered oldest to newest.
    frames: Vec<CoredumpFrame>,
    /// All linear memories in the coredump.
    memories: Vec<CoredumpMemory>,
    /// All global variables in the coredump.
    globals: Vec<CoredumpGlobal>,
    /// The serialized coredump bytes.
    bytes: Vec<u8>,
    /// Maps instance raw pointers to indices in [`Coredump::instances`].
    instance_map: Map<usize, u32>,
    /// Maps memory raw pointers to indices in [`Coredump::memories`].
    memory_map: Map<usize, u32>,
    /// Maps global raw pointers to indices in [`Coredump::globals`].
    global_map: Map<usize, u32>,
}

impl Coredump {
    /// Creates a new empty [`Coredump`] with the given `executable_name`.
    pub(crate) fn new(executable_name: &str) -> Self {
        Self {
            executable_name: executable_name.into(),
            ..Self::default()
        }
    }

    /// Captures the current state of the given `stack` into a new [`Coredump`].
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// - The captured frames are ordered youngest to oldest after serialization.
    /// - The `executable_name` is taken from the engine configuration.
    pub(crate) fn capture(
        store: &PrunedStore,
        stack: &Stack,
        code: &CodeMap,
        executable_name: &str,
    ) -> Coredump {
        let mut dump = Coredump::new(executable_name);
        dump.capture_frames(store, stack, code);
        dump.serialize();
        dump
    }

    /// Extends `self` with the frames of the given `stack`.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// This is used when a host function re-enters Wasm and the inner execution
    /// traps: the coredump generated by the inner invocation is extended with
    /// the frames of the outer Wasm execution levels.
    pub(crate) fn extend_with_stack(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, stack: &Stack, code: &CodeMap) {
        let mut outer = Coredump::new("");
        outer.capture_frames(store, stack, code);
        self.merge_outer(&outer);
        self.serialize();
    }

    /// Returns the serialized coredump bytes.
    pub(crate) fn bytes(&self) -> &[u8] {
        &self.bytes
    }

    /// Captures the frames of the given `stack` into `self`.
    fn capture_frames(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, stack: &Stack, code: &CodeMap) {
        let frames = stack.frames();
        let cells = stack.cells();
        let len_frames = frames.len();
        if len_frames == 0 {
            return;
        }
        // Reconstruct the actual instance of every frame.
        let mut actual_instances = vec![None; len_frames];
        let mut current = stack.current_instance();
        for index in (0..len_frames).rev() {
            actual_instances[index] = current;
            if let Some(prev) = frames[index].instance() {
                current = Some(prev);
            }
        }
        for (index, frame) in frames.iter().enumerate() {
            let Some(instance) = actual_instances[index] else {
                continue;
            };
            let instance_index = self.get_or_add_instance(store, instance);
            let Some((_func, cref)) = code.find_func_by_ip(frame.ip()) else {
                continue;
            };
            let ops = cref.ops();
            let ip_addr = frame.ip().as_ptr() as usize;
            let code_offset = ip_addr.saturating_sub(ops.as_ptr() as usize) as u32;
            let frame_start = frame.start().into_inner();
            let local_tys = cref.locals();
            // Compute the values of all function parameters and declared locals.
            let mut locals = Vec::with_capacity(local_tys.len());
            let mut cell_offset = frame_start;
            for ty in local_tys {
                locals.push(read_cell(cells, cell_offset, *ty));
                cell_offset += cells_for_valtype(*ty);
            }
            // Compute the values on the operand stack.
            let operand_start = cell_offset;
            let operand_end = match frames.get(index + 1) {
                Some(next) => next.start().into_inner(),
                None => {
                    let frame_end = frame_start.saturating_add(usize::from(cref.len_stack_slots()));
                    frame_end.min(cells.len())
                }
            };
            let operands = cells[operand_start..operand_end]
                .iter()
                .map(|_| CoredumpValue::Unknown)
                .collect();
            self.frames.push(CoredumpFrame {
                instance_index,
                func_index: cref.func_index().into_u32(),
                code_offset,
                locals,
                operands,
            });
        }
    }

    /// Merges the frames of an outer execution into `self`.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// The frames of the outer execution are older than the frames already
    /// contained in `self` and are therefore prepended to the frame list.
    fn merge_outer(&mut self, outer: &Coredump) {
        let mut instance_remap = vec![0; outer.instances.len()];
        for (index, instance) in outer.instances.iter().enumerate() {
            let new_index = if let Some(index) = self.instance_map.get(&instance.key) {
                *index
            } else {
                let module_index = self.modules.len() as u32;
                self.modules
                    .push(outer.modules[instance.module_index as usize].clone());
                let memory_indices = instance
                    .memory_indices
                    .iter()
                    .map(|&index| self.merge_memory(&outer.memories[index as usize]))
                    .collect();
                let global_indices = instance
                    .global_indices
                    .iter()
                    .map(|&index| self.merge_global(&outer.globals[index as usize]))
                    .collect();
                let new_index = self.instances.len() as u32;
                self.instances.push(CoredumpInstance {
                    key: instance.key,
                    module_index,
                    memory_indices,
                    global_indices,
                });
                self.instance_map.insert(instance.key, new_index);
                new_index
            };
            instance_remap[index] = new_index;
        }
        let mut new_frames = Vec::with_capacity(outer.frames.len() + self.frames.len());
        for frame in &outer.frames {
            let mut frame = frame.clone();
            frame.instance_index = instance_remap[frame.instance_index as usize];
            new_frames.push(frame);
        }
        new_frames.extend(self.frames.drain(..));
        self.frames = new_frames;
    }

    /// Merges a memory from an outer capture into `self` returning its new index.
    fn merge_memory(&mut self, memory: &CoredumpMemory) -> u32 {
        if let Some(index) = self.memory_map.get(&memory.key) {
            return *index;
        }
        let index = self.memories.len() as u32;
        self.memories.push(memory.clone());
        self.memory_map.insert(memory.key, index);
        index
    }

    /// Merges a global from an outer capture into `self` returning its new index.
    fn merge_global(&mut self, global: &CoredumpGlobal) -> u32 {
        if let Some(index) = self.global_map.get(&global.key) {
            return *index;
        }
        let index = self.globals.len() as u32;
        self.globals.push(global.clone());
        self.global_map.insert(global.key, index);
        index
    }

    /// Returns the index of the given `instance`, adding it if necessary.
    fn get_or_add_instance(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, instance: Inst) -> u32 {
        let key = unsafe { instance.as_ref() } as *const _ as usize;
        if let Some(index) = self.instance_map.get(&key) {
            return *index;
        }
        let entity = unsafe { instance.as_ref() };
        let module_index = self.modules.len() as u32;
        self.modules.push(String::new());
        let mut memory_indices = Vec::new();
        let mut memory_index = 0;
        while let Some(memory) = entity.get_memory(memory_index) {
            memory_indices.push(self.get_or_add_memory(store, &memory));
            memory_index += 1;
        }
        let mut global_indices = Vec::new();
        let mut global_index = 0;
        while let Some(global) = entity.get_global(global_index) {
            global_indices.push(self.get_or_add_global(store, &global));
            global_index += 1;
        }
        let index = self.instances.len() as u32;
        self.instances.push(CoredumpInstance {
            key,
            module_index,
            memory_indices,
            global_indices,
        });
        self.instance_map.insert(key, index);
        index
    }

    /// Returns the index of the given `memory`, adding it if necessary.
    fn get_or_add_memory(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, memory: &crate::Memory) -> u32 {
        let core_memory = store.inner().resolve_memory(memory);
        let key = core_memory as *const _ as usize;
        if let Some(index) = self.memory_map.get(&key) {
            return *index;
        }
        let index = self.memories.len() as u32;
        self.memories.push(CoredumpMemory {
            key,
            ty: core_memory.ty(),
            data: core_memory.data().to_vec(),
        });
        self.memory_map.insert(key, index);
        index
    }

    /// Returns the index of the given `global`, adding it if necessary.
    fn get_or_add_global(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, global: &crate::Global) -> u32 {
        let core_global = store.inner().resolve_global(global);
        let key = core_global as *const _ as usize;
        if let Some(index) = self.global_map.get(&key) {
            return *index;
        }
        let index = self.globals.len() as u32;
        self.globals.push(CoredumpGlobal {
            key,
            ty: core_global.ty(),
            value: *core_global.get_raw(),
        });
        self.global_map.insert(key, index);
        index
    }

    /// Serializes `self` into the Wasm binary format.
    fn serialize(&mut self) {
        let mut out = vec![0x00, 0x61, 0x73, 0x6D, 0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00];

        // Section: "core"
        let mut payload = Vec::new();
        payload.push(0x00);
        payload.encode_name(&self.executable_name);
        out.encode_custom_section("core", &payload);

        // Section: "coremodules"
        let mut payload = Vec::new();
        payload.encode_u32(self.modules.len() as u32);
        for module in &self.modules {
            payload.push(0x00);
            payload.encode_name(module);
        }
        out.encode_custom_section("coremodules", &payload);

        // Section: "coreinstances"
        let mut payload = Vec::new();
        payload.encode_u32(self.instances.len() as u32);
        for instance in &self.instances {
            payload.push(0x00);
            payload.encode_u32(instance.module_index);
            payload.encode_u32(instance.memory_indices.len() as u32);
            for &memory_index in &instance.memory_indices {
                payload.encode_u32(memory_index);
            }
            payload.encode_u32(instance.global_indices.len() as u32);
            for &global_index in &instance.global_indices {
                payload.encode_u32(global_index);
            }
        }
        out.encode_custom_section("coreinstances", &payload);

        // Section: "corestack"
        let mut payload = Vec::new();
        payload.push(0x00);
        payload.encode_name("main");
        payload.encode_u32(self.frames.len() as u32);
        for frame in self.frames.iter().rev() {
            payload.push(0x00);
            payload.encode_u32(frame.instance_index);
            payload.encode_u32(frame.func_index);
            payload.encode_u32(frame.code_offset);
            payload.encode_values(&frame.locals);
            payload.encode_values(&frame.operands);
        }
        out.encode_custom_section("corestack", &payload);

        // Section: memory (id 5)
        let mut section = Vec::new();
        section.encode_u32(self.memories.len() as u32);
        for memory in &self.memories {
            memory.encode(&mut section);
        }
        out.encode_standard_section(5, &section);

        // Section: global (id 6)
        let mut section = Vec::new();
        section.encode_u32(self.globals.len() as u32);
        for global in &self.globals {
            global.encode(&mut section);
        }
        out.encode_standard_section(6, &section);

        // Section: data (id 11)
        let mut section = Vec::new();
        let mut segments = Vec::new();
        for (index, memory) in self.memories.iter().enumerate() {
            if memory.data.is_empty() {
                continue;
            }
            segments.push((index as u32, &memory.data[..]));
        }
        section.encode_u32(segments.len() as u32);
        for (memory_index, data) in segments {
            if memory_index == 0 {
                section.push(0x00);
            } else {
                section.push(0x01);
                section.encode_u32(memory_index);
            }
            section.push(0x41);
            section.encode_signed_i32(0);
            section.push(0x0B);
            section.encode_u32(data.len() as u32);
            section.extend_from_slice(data);
        }
        out.encode_standard_section(11, &section);

        self.bytes = out;
    }
}

impl CoredumpMemory {
    /// Encodes `self` into the memory section payload.
    fn encode(&self, out: &mut Vec<u8>) {
        let mut flags = if self.ty.maximum().is_some() { 0x01 } else { 0x00 };
        if self.ty.is_64() {
            flags |= 0x04;
        }
        out.push(flags);
        if self.ty.is_64() {
            out.encode_u64(self.ty.minimum());
            if let Some(maximum) = self.ty.maximum() {
                out.encode_u64(maximum);
            }
        } else {
            out.encode_u32(self.ty.minimum() as u32);
            if let Some(maximum) = self.ty.maximum() {
                out.encode_u32(maximum as u32);
            }
        }
    }
}

impl CoredumpGlobal {
    /// Encodes `self` into the global section payload.
    fn encode(&self, out: &mut Vec<u8>) {
        out.push(valtype_byte(self.ty.content()));
        out.push(if self.ty.mutability().is_mut() { 0x01 } else { 0x00 });
        match self.ty.content() {
            ValType::I32 => {
                out.push(0x41);
                out.encode_signed_i32(self.value.read_as());
                out.push(0x0B);
            }
            ValType::I64 => {
                out.push(0x42);
                out.encode_signed_i64(self.value.read_as());
                out.push(0x0B);
            }
            ValType::F32 => {
                out.push(0x43);
                out.extend_from_slice(&self.value.read_as::<f32>().to_bits().to_le_bytes());
                out.push(0x0B);
            }
            ValType::F64 => {
                out.push(0x44);
                out.extend_from_slice(&self.value.read_as::<f64>().to_bits().to_le_bytes());
                out.push(0x0B);
            }
            _ => {
                // Reference and vector values cannot be represented in the
                // coredump global section, emit a zero i32 constant instead.
                out.push(0x41);
                out.encode_signed_i32(0);
                out.push(0x0B);
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Returns the number of cells required to represent a value of `ty`.
fn cells_for_valtype(ty: ValType) -> usize {
    match ty {
        #[cfg(feature = "simd")]
        ValType::V128 => 2,
        _ => 1,
    }
}

/// Returns the Wasm binary value type byte for `ty`.
fn valtype_byte(ty: ValType) -> u8 {
    match ty {
        ValType::I32 => 0x7F,
        ValType::I64 => 0x7E,
        ValType::F32 => 0x7D,
        ValType::F64 => 0x7C,
        ValType::V128 => 0x7B,
        ValType::FuncRef => 0x70,
        ValType::ExternRef => 0x6F,
    }
}

/// Reads a value of type `ty` from `cells` at `offset`.
fn read_cell(cells: &[crate::engine::executor::Cell], offset: usize, ty: ValType) -> CoredumpValue {
    let Some(cell) = cells.get(offset) else {
        return CoredumpValue::Unknown;
    };
    let bits = u64::from(*cell);
    match ty {
        ValType::I32 => CoredumpValue::I32(bits as i32),
        ValType::I64 => CoredumpValue::I64(bits as i64),
        ValType::F32 => CoredumpValue::F32(bits as u32),
        ValType::F64 => CoredumpValue::F64(bits),
        _ => CoredumpValue::Unknown,
    }
}

trait Encode {
    fn encode_u32(&mut self, value: u32);
    fn encode_u64(&mut self, value: u64);
    fn encode_signed_i32(&mut self, value: i32);
    fn encode_signed_i64(&mut self, value: i64);
    fn encode_name(&mut self, name: &str);
    fn encode_values(&mut self, values: &[CoredumpValue]);
    fn encode_custom_section(&mut self, name: &str, payload: &[u8]);
    fn encode_standard_section(&mut self, id: u8, payload: &[u8]);
}

impl Encode for Vec<u8> {
    fn encode_u32(&mut self, mut value: u32) {
        loop {
            let mut byte = (value & 0x7F) as u8;
            value >>= 7;
            if value != 0 {
                byte |= 0x80;
            }
            self.push(byte);
            if value == 0 {
                break;
            }
        }
    }

    fn encode_u64(&mut self, mut value: u64) {
        loop {
            let mut byte = (value & 0x7F) as u8;
            value >>= 7;
            if value != 0 {
                byte |= 0x80;
            }
            self.push(byte);
            if value == 0 {
                break;
            }
        }
    }

    fn encode_signed_i32(&mut self, value: i32) {
        self.encode_signed_i64(i64::from(value));
    }

    fn encode_signed_i64(&mut self, mut value: i64) {
        loop {
            let byte = (value & 0x7F) as u8;
            value >>= 7;
            let done = (value == 0 && byte & 0x40 == 0) || (value == -1 && byte & 0x40 != 0);
            self.push(if done { byte } else { byte | 0x80 });
            if done {
                break;
            }
        }
    }

    fn encode_name(&mut self, name: &str) {
        self.encode_u32(name.len() as u32);
        self.extend_from_slice(name.as_bytes());
    }

    fn encode_values(&mut self, values: &[CoredumpValue]) {
        self.encode_u32(values.len() as u32);
        for value in values {
            match *value {
                CoredumpValue::I32(value) => {
                    self.push(0x7F);
                    self.encode_signed_i32(value);
                }
                CoredumpValue::I64(value) => {
                    self.push(0x7E);
                    self.encode_signed_i64(value);
                }
                CoredumpValue::F32(value) => {
                    self.push(0x7D);
                    self.extend_from_slice(&value.to_le_bytes());
                }
                CoredumpValue::F64(value) => {
                    self.push(0x7C);
                    self.extend_from_slice(&value.to_le_bytes());
                }
                CoredumpValue::Unknown => {
                    self.push(0x01);
                }
            }
        }
    }

    fn encode_custom_section(&mut self, name: &str, payload: &[u8]) {
        self.push(0x00);
        let mut section = Vec::new();
        section.encode_name(name);
        section.extend_from_slice(payload);
        self.encode_u32(section.len() as u32);
        self.extend_from_slice(&section);
    }

    fn encode_standard_section(&mut self, id: u8, payload: &[u8]) {
        self.push(id);
        self.encode_u32(payload.len() as u32);
        self.extend_from_slice(payload);
    }
}

anchor = '''macro_rules! trap {'''
helper = '''/// Creates an [`Error`] for the given `trap_code`, attaching a coredump if enabled.
pub(crate) fn error_with_coredump(state: &mut VmState, trap_code: TrapCode) -> Error {
    let mut error = Error::from(trap_code);
    let config = state.store.inner().engine().config();
    if config.get_generate_coredump() {
        let coredump = Coredump::capture(
            &*state.store,
            &*state.stack,
            state.code,
            config.get_coredump_executable_name(),
        );
        error.set_coredump(coredump);
    }
    error
}

/// Extends the coredump of `error` with the frames of the current `state` if any.
pub(crate) fn extend_error_with_coredump(error: &mut Error, state: &mut VmState) {
    if let Some(coredump) = error.coredump_mut() {
        coredump.extend_with_stack(&*state.store, &*state.stack, state.code);
    }
}

'''
idx = s.find(anchor)
s = s[:idx] + helper + s[idx:]
p.write_text(s)
print("done")

//! Tests for opt-in coredump generation.

use wasmi::{
    Caller,

/// Returns the names of all custom sections in the given Wasm binary.
fn custom_section_names(wasm: &[u8]) -> Vec<String> {
    // This is a tiny Wasm parser sufficient for the coredump tests.
    let mut names = Vec::new();
    let mut pos = 8; // skip magic and version
    while pos < wasm.len() {
        let id = wasm[pos];
        pos += 1;
        // parse u32 LEB
        let (size, next) = read_uleb32(wasm, pos);
        let section_end = next + size as usize;
        if id == 0 {
            let (len, next) = read_uleb32(wasm, next);
            let name = core::str::from_utf8(&wasm[next..next + len as usize]).unwrap();
            names.push(name.to_string());
        }
        pos = section_end;
    }
    names
}

fn read_uleb32(wasm: &[u8], mut pos: usize) -> (u32, usize) {
    let mut result = 0u32;
    let mut shift = 0;
    loop {
        let byte = wasm[pos];
        pos += 1;
        result |= u32::from(byte & 0x7f) << shift;
        if byte & 0x80 == 0 {
            break;
        }
        shift += 7;
    }
    (result, pos)
}

#[test]
fn coredump_generated_on_trap() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config
        .generate_coredump(true)
        .coredump_executable_name("myexe");
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let module = Module::new(&engine, r#"(module (func (export "boom") unreachable))"#).unwrap();
    let instance = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "boom").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(
        error.as_trap_code(),
        Some(TrapCode::UnreachableCodeReached)
    );
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("must have coredump");
    assert_eq!(&coredump[..4], &[0x00, 0x61, 0x73, 0x6d]);
    assert_eq!(&coredump[4..8], &[0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00]);
    let names = custom_section_names(coredump);
    assert!(names.contains(&"core".to_string()));
    assert!(names.contains(&"coremodules".to_string()));
    assert!(names.contains(&"coreinstances".to_string()));
    assert!(names.contains(&"corestack".to_string()));
}

#[test]
fn no_coredump_when_disabled() {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let module = Module::new(&engine, r#"(module (func (export "boom") unreachable))"#).unwrap();
    let instance = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "boom").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap_err();
    assert!(error.coredump().is_none());
}

#[test]
fn coredump_has_frames_across_reenrant_host_call() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let host_fn = Func::wrap(&mut store, |mut caller: Caller<()>, _: i32| -> i32 {
        let wasm_fn = caller
            .get_export("inner")
            .and_then(wasmi::Extern::into_func)
            .unwrap()
            .typed::<i32, i32>(&caller)
            .unwrap();
        wasm_fn.call(&mut caller, 42).unwrap()
    });
    let linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    linker.define("env", "host_fn", host_fn).unwrap();
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (import "env" "host_fn" (func $host_fn (param i32) (result i32)))
            (func (export "outer") (param i32) (result i32)
                (call $host_fn (local.get 0))
            )
            (func (export "inner") (param i32) (result i32)
                unreachable
            )
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let instance = linker
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<i32, i32>(&store, "outer").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, 1).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("must have coredump");
    // The corestack should contain two frames: inner (trap site) and outer.
    let names = custom_section_names(coredump);
    assert!(names.contains(&"corestack".to_string()));
    // Check that the coredump is non-trivial.
    assert!(coredump.len() > 50);
}

            .unwrap()
            .typed::<i32, i32>(&caller)
            .unwrap();
        wasm_fn.call(&mut caller, 42).unwrap()

cd /app && cat > crates/wasmi/tests/integration/coredump.rs <<'EOF'
//! Tests for opt-in coredump generation.

use wasmi::{
    Caller,
    Config,
    Engine,
    Error,
    Func,
    Linker,
    Module,
    Store,
    TrapCode,
};

/// A minimal Wasm binary reader sufficient for the coredump tests.
struct Reader<'a> {
    bytes: &'a [u8],
    pos: usize,
}

impl<'a> Reader<'a> {
    fn new(bytes: &'a [u8]) -> Self {
        Self { bytes, pos: 8 } // skip magic and version
    }

    fn u8(&mut self) -> u8 {
        let value = self.bytes[self.pos];
        self.pos += 1;
        value
    }

    fn u32(&mut self) -> u32 {
        let mut result = 0u32;
        let mut shift = 0;
        loop {
            let byte = self.u8();
            result |= u32::from(byte & 0x7f) << shift;
            if byte & 0x80 == 0 {
                return result;
            }
            shift += 7;
        }
    }

    fn bytes(&mut self, len: usize) -> &'a [u8] {
        let value = &self.bytes[self.pos..self.pos + len];
        self.pos += len;
        value
    }

    fn name(&mut self) -> &'a str {
        let len = self.u32() as usize;
        core::str::from_utf8(self.bytes(len)).unwrap()
    }

    fn custom_section(&mut self) -> (&'a str, &'a [u8]) {
        assert_eq!(self.u8(), 0);
        let size = self.u32() as usize;
        let start = self.pos;
        let name = self.name();
        let payload = self.bytes(size - (self.pos - start));
        (name, payload)
    }

    fn skip_section(&mut self) {
        let _id = self.u8();
        let size = self.u32() as usize;
        self.pos += size;
    }
}

/// Returns `(name, payload)` pairs of all custom sections.
fn custom_sections(wasm: &[u8]) -> Vec<(String, Vec<u8>)> {
    let mut reader = Reader::new(wasm);
    let mut sections = Vec::new();
    while reader.pos < wasm.len() {
        let id = wasm[reader.pos];
        if id == 0 {
            let (name, payload) = reader.custom_section();
            sections.push((name.to_string(), payload.to_vec()));
        } else {
            reader.skip_section();
        }
    }
    sections
}

/// Parses the number of frames in the `"corestack"` section.
fn corestack_frame_count(wasm: &[u8]) -> usize {
    let sections = custom_sections(wasm);
    let (_, payload) = sections
        .iter()
        .find(|(name, _)| name == "corestack")
        .expect("must have corestack section");
    let mut reader = Reader::new(payload);
    assert_eq!(reader.u8(), 0x00);
    let _thread_name = reader.name();
    let count = reader.u32() as usize;
    for _ in 0..count {
        assert_eq!(reader.u8(), 0x00);
        let _instance_index = reader.u32();
        let _func_index = reader.u32();
        let _code_offset = reader.u32();
        // locals
        let locals = reader.u32() as usize;
        for _ in 0..locals {
            let tag = reader.u8();
            match tag {
                0x7F => {
                    let _ = reader.u32(); // i32 LEB (u32 parse works for small negatives)
                }
                0x7E => {
                    let _ = reader.u32();
                }
                0x7D => {
                    let _ = reader.bytes(4);
                }
                0x7C => {
                    let _ = reader.bytes(8);
                }
                0x01 => {}
                _ => panic!("unexpected value tag: {tag:#x}"),
            }
        }
        // operands
        let operands = reader.u32() as usize;
        for _ in 0..operands {
            let tag = reader.u8();
            match tag {
                0x7F => {
                    let _ = reader.u32();
                }
                0x7E => {
                    let _ = reader.u32();
                }
                0x7D => {
                    let _ = reader.bytes(4);
                }
                0x7C => {
                    let _ = reader.bytes(8);
                }
                0x01 => {}
                _ => panic!("unexpected value tag: {tag:#x}"),
            }
        }
    }
    count
}

/// Parses the executable name from the `"core"` section.
fn core_executable_name(wasm: &[u8]) -> String {
    let sections = custom_sections(wasm);
    let (_, payload) = sections
        .iter()
        .find(|(name, _)| name == "core")
        .expect("must have core section");
    let mut reader = Reader::new(payload);
    assert_eq!(reader.u8(), 0x00);
    reader.name().to_string()
}

#[test]
fn coredump_generated_on_trap() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config
        .generate_coredump(true)
        .coredump_executable_name("myexe");
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let module = Module::new(&engine, r#"(module (func (export "boom") unreachable))"#).unwrap();
    let instance = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "boom").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(error.as_trap_code(), Some(TrapCode::UnreachableCodeReached));
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("must have coredump");
    assert_eq!(&coredump[..4], &[0x00, 0x61, 0x73, 0x6d]);
    assert_eq!(&coredump[4..8], &[0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00]);
    let names: Vec<String> = custom_sections(coredump)
        .into_iter()
        .map(|(name, _)| name)
        .collect();
    assert!(names.contains(&"core".to_string()));
    assert!(names.contains(&"coremodules".to_string()));
    assert!(names.contains(&"coreinstances".to_string()));
    assert!(names.contains(&"corestack".to_string()));
    assert_eq!(core_executable_name(coredump), "myexe");
    assert_eq!(corestack_frame_count(coredump), 1);
}

#[test]
fn no_coredump_when_disabled() {
    let engine = Engine::default();
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let module = Module::new(&engine, r#"(module (func (export "boom") unreachable))"#).unwrap();
    let instance = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "boom").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap_err();
    assert!(error.coredump().is_none());
}

#[test]
fn coredump_has_frames_across_reenrant_host_call() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let host_fn = Func::wrap(&mut store, |mut caller: Caller<()>, _: i32| -> Result<i32, Error> {
        let wasm_fn = caller
            .get_export("inner")
            .and_then(wasmi::Extern::into_func)
            .unwrap()
            .typed::<i32, i32>(&caller)
            .unwrap();
        wasm_fn.call(&mut caller, 42)
    });
    let mut linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    linker.define("env", "host_fn", host_fn).unwrap();
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (import "env" "host_fn" (func $host_fn (param i32) (result i32)))
            (func (export "outer") (param i32) (result i32)
                (call $host_fn (local.get 0))
            )
            (func (export "inner") (param i32) (result i32)
                unreachable
            )
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let instance = linker
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<i32, i32>(&store, "outer").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, 1).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("must have coredump");
    // The corestack must contain both the inner (trap site) and outer frames.
    assert_eq!(corestack_frame_count(coredump), 2);
}

    91	    /// - The Wasm module header is the set of all sections that appear before
    92	    ///   the Wasm code section.
    93	    /// - We separate parsing of the Wasm module header since the information of
    94	    ///   the Wasm module header is required for translating the Wasm code section.
    95	    ///
    96	    /// # Errors
    97	    ///
    98	    /// If the Wasm bytecode stream fails to parse or validate.
    99	    fn parse_buffered_header(
   100	        &mut self,
   101	        buffer: &mut &[u8],
   102	        custom_sections: &mut CustomSectionsBuilder,
   103	    ) -> Result<ModuleHeader, Error> {
   104	        let mut header = ModuleHeaderBuilder::new(&self.engine);
   105	        loop {
   106	            let (consumed, payload) = self.next_payload(buffer)?;
   107	            match payload {
   108	                Payload::Version {
   109	                    num,
   110	                    encoding,
   111	                    range,
   112	                } => self.process_version(num, encoding, range),
   113	                Payload::TypeSection(section) => self.process_types(section, &mut header),
   114	                Payload::ImportSection(section) => self.process_imports(section, &mut header),
   115	                Payload::FunctionSection(section) => self.process_functions(section, &mut header),
   116	                Payload::TableSection(section) => self.process_tables(section, &mut header),
   117	                Payload::MemorySection(section) => self.process_memories(section, &mut header),
   118	                Payload::GlobalSection(section) => self.process_globals(section, &mut header),
   119	                Payload::ExportSection(section) => self.process_exports(section, &mut header),
   120	                Payload::StartSection { func, range } => {
   121	                    self.process_start(func, range, &mut header)
   122	                }
   123	                Payload::ElementSection(section) => self.process_element(section, &mut header),
   124	                Payload::DataCountSection { count, range } => self.process_data_count(count, range),
   125	                Payload::CodeSectionStart { count, range, size } => {
   126	                    self.process_code_start(count, range, size)?;
   127	                    Self::consume_buffer(consumed, buffer);
   128	                    break;
   129	                }
   130	                Payload::DataSection(_) => break,
   131	                Payload::End(_) => break,
   132	                Payload::CustomSection(reader) => {
   133	                    self.process_custom_section(custom_sections, reader)
   134	                }
   135	                unexpected => self.process_invalid_payload(unexpected),
   136	            }?;
   137	            Self::consume_buffer(consumed, buffer);
   138	        }
   139	        Ok(header.finish())
   140	    }

#[test]
fn coredump_includes_memory_global_and_locals() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (memory (export "memory") 1)
            (global (export "g") (mut i32) (i32.const 7))
            (func (export "boom") (param i32 i64)
                (local i32)
                i32.const 5
                local.set 2
                local.get 0
                local.get 1
                local.get 2
                unreachable
            )
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let instance = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<(i32, i64), ()>(&store, "boom").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, (3, 4)).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("must have coredump");

    // The coredump must be parseable as a Wasm binary.
    let mut parser = wasmparser::Parser::new(0);
    let mut saw_memory = false;
    let mut saw_global = false;
    let mut saw_data = false;
    for payload in parser.parse_all(coredump) {
        let payload = payload.unwrap();
        match payload {
            wasmparser::Payload::CustomSection(section) => {
                let name = section.name();
                assert!(
                    ["core", "coremodules", "coreinstances", "corestack"].contains(&name),
                    "unexpected custom section {name}"
                );
            }
            wasmparser::Payload::MemorySection(_) => saw_memory = true,
            wasmparser::Payload::GlobalSection(_) => saw_global = true,
            wasmparser::Payload::DataSection(_) => saw_data = true,
            _ => {}
        }
    }
    assert!(saw_memory, "must have memory section");
    assert!(saw_global, "must have global section");
    assert!(saw_data, "must have data section");
    assert_eq!(corestack_frame_count(coredump), 1);
}

from pathlib import Path

# Add a helper to parse frame locals. Insert before #[test] coredump_includes_memory_global_and_locals
helper = '''
/// Parses the locals of the first frame in the `"corestack"` section.
fn corestack_first_frame_locals(wasm: &[u8]) -> Vec<i64> {
    let sections = custom_sections(wasm);
    let (_, payload) = sections
        .iter()
        .find(|(name, _)| name == "corestack")
        .expect("must have corestack section");
    let mut reader = Reader::new(payload);
    assert_eq!(reader.u8(), 0x00);
    let _thread_name = reader.name();
    let count = reader.u32() as usize;
    assert!(count >= 1);
    assert_eq!(reader.u8(), 0x00);
    let _instance_index = reader.u32();
    let _func_index = reader.u32();
    let _code_offset = reader.u32();
    let locals = reader.u32() as usize;
    let mut values = Vec::new();
    for _ in 0..locals {
        let tag = reader.u8();
        match tag {
            0x7F => values.push(i64::from(reader.u32() as i32)),
            0x7E => values.push(reader.u32() as i64),
            0x7D => {
                let _ = reader.bytes(4);
                values.push(0);
            }
            0x7C => {
                let _ = reader.bytes(8);
                values.push(0);
            }
            0x01 => values.push(i64::MIN),
            _ => panic!("unexpected value tag: {tag:#x}"),
        }
    }
    values
}

                assert_eq!(memory.maximum, None);

                }
