#[test]
fn coredump_from_root_host_calling_wasm() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let module = Module::new(&engine, r#"(module (func (export "inner") unreachable))"#).unwrap();
    let instance = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let inner = instance
        .get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "inner")
        .unwrap();
    let host_fn = Func::wrap(&mut store, move |mut caller: Caller<()>| -> Result<(), Error> {
        inner.call(&mut caller, ())
    });
    let error = host_fn.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(error.coredump().expect("must have coredump").len() > 0, true);
    assert_eq!(corestack_frame_count(error.coredump().unwrap()), 1);
}

#[test]
fn coredump_has_frames_from_all_nested_levels() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    // innermost traps
    let module_inner = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"(module (func (export "innermost") unreachable))"#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let instance_inner = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module_inner)
        .unwrap();
    let innermost = instance_inner
        .get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "innermost")
        .unwrap();
    // level 1: Wasm calls host1; host1 calls Wasm (level 2); level 2 calls host2; host2 calls innermost Wasm
    let host2 = Func::wrap(&mut store, move |mut caller: Caller<()>| -> Result<(), Error> {
        innermost.call(&mut caller, ())
    });
    let mut linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    linker.define("env", "host2", host2).unwrap();
    let module_mid = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (import "env" "host2" (func $host2))
            (func (export "middle") (call $host2))
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let instance_mid = linker
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module_mid)
        .unwrap();
    let middle = instance_mid.get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "middle").unwrap();
    let host1 = Func::wrap(&mut store, move |mut caller: Caller<()>| -> Result<(), Error> {
        middle.call(&mut caller, ())
    });
    let mut linker = Linker::new(&engine);
    linker.define("env", "host1", host1).unwrap();
    let module_outer = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (import "env" "host1" (func $host1))
            (func (export "outer") (call $host1))
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let instance_outer = linker
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module_outer)
        .unwrap();
    let outer = instance_outer
        .get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "outer")
        .unwrap();
    let error = outer.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("must have coredump");
    // Three Wasm execution levels: innermost, middle, outer.
    assert_eq!(corestack_frame_count(coredump), 3);
}
EOF

from pathlib import Path
p = Path('crates/wasmi/tests/integration/coredump.rs')
s = p.read_text()
# Add a helper to parse func indices in corestack order
helper = '''
/// Parses the function indices of the frames in the `"corestack"` section in order.
fn corestack_func_indices(wasm: &[u8]) -> Vec<u32> {
    let sections = custom_sections(wasm);
    let (_, payload) = sections
        .iter()
        .find(|(name, _)| name == "corestack")
        .expect("must have corestack section");
    let mut reader = Reader::new(payload);
    assert_eq!(reader.u8(), 0x00);
    let _thread_name = reader.name();
    let count = reader.u32() as usize;
    let mut funcs = Vec::new();
    for _ in 0..count {
        assert_eq!(reader.u8(), 0x00);
        let _instance_index = reader.u32();
        funcs.push(reader.u32());
        let _code_offset = reader.u32();
        let locals = reader.u32() as usize;
        for _ in 0..locals {
            let tag = reader.u8();
            match tag {
                0x7F => {
                    let _ = reader.u32();
                }
                0x7E => {
                    let _ = reader.u32();
                }
                0x7D => {
                    let _ = reader.bytes(4);
                }
                0x7C => {
                    let _ = reader.bytes(8);
                }
                0x01 => {}
                _ => panic!("unexpected value tag: {tag:#x}"),
            }
        }
        let operands = reader.u32() as usize;
        for _ in 0..operands {
            let tag = reader.u8();
            match tag {
                0x7F => {
                    let _ = reader.u32();
                }
                0x7E => {
                    let _ = reader.u32();
                }
                0x7D => {
                    let _ = reader.bytes(4);
                }
                0x7C => {
                    let _ = reader.bytes(8);
                }
                0x01 => {}
                _ => panic!("unexpected value tag: {tag:#x}"),
            }
        }
    }
    funcs
}

'''

   196	        self.items

returncode=0
use crate::{
    StoreContext,
    store::{PrunedStore, Store, StoreInner},
};

/// A unique store identifier.
///
/// # Note
///
/// Used to differentiate different store instances.
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct StoreId(u32);

impl StoreId {
    /// Returns a new unique [`StoreId`].
    pub(super) fn new() -> Self {
        use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
        /// An atomic, static store identifier counter.
        static STORE_ID: AtomicU32 = AtomicU32::new(0);
        let next = STORE_ID.fetch_add(1, Ordering::AcqRel);
        Self(next)
    }
}

/// A value associated to a [`Store`].
#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Stored<T> {
    /// The identifier of the associated store.
    store: StoreId,
    /// The stored value.
    value: T,
}

/// Trait implemented by utilities that can wrap or unwrap [`Stored`] values.
pub trait AsStoreId: Copy {
    /// Wraps the given `value` as [`Stored<T>`] associating it with `self`.
    fn wrap<T>(self, value: T) -> Stored<T>;

    /// Unwraps the given [`Stored<T>`] reference and returns the `T`.
    ///
    /// Returns `None` if `value` does not originate from `self`.
    fn unwrap<T>(self, value: &Stored<T>) -> Option<&T>;
}

impl AsStoreId for StoreId {
    #[inline]
    fn wrap<T>(self, value: T) -> Stored<T> {
        Stored { store: self, value }
    }

    #[inline]
    fn unwrap<T>(self, value: &Stored<T>) -> Option<&T> {
        if value.store != self {
            return None;
        }
        Some(&value.value)
    }
}

impl AsStoreId for &'_ StoreId {
    #[inline]
    fn wrap<T>(self, value: T) -> Stored<T> {
        <StoreId as AsStoreId>::wrap(*self, value)
    }

    #[inline]
    fn unwrap<T>(self, value: &Stored<T>) -> Option<&T> {
        <StoreId as AsStoreId>::unwrap(*self, value)
    }
}

impl AsStoreId for &'_ StoreInner {
    #[inline]
    fn wrap<T>(self, value: T) -> Stored<T> {
        self.id().wrap(value)
    }

    #[inline]
    fn unwrap<T>(self, value: &Stored<T>) -> Option<&T> {
        self.id().unwrap(value)
    }
}

impl<D> AsStoreId for &'_ Store<D> {
    #[inline]
    fn wrap<T>(self, value: T) -> Stored<T> {
        self.inner.wrap(value)
    }

    #[inline]
    fn unwrap<T>(self, value: &Stored<T>) -> Option<&T> {
        self.inner.unwrap(value)
    }
}

impl AsStoreId for &'_ PrunedStore {
    #[inline]
    fn wrap<T>(self, value: T) -> Stored<T> {
        self.inner().wrap(value)
    }

    #[inline]
    fn unwrap<T>(self, value: &Stored<T>) -> Option<&T> {
        self.inner().unwrap(value)
    }
}

impl<S> AsStoreId for StoreContext<'_, S> {
    #[inline]
    fn wrap<T>(self, value: T) -> Stored<T> {
        self.store.wrap(value)
    }

    #[inline]
    fn unwrap<T>(self, value: &Stored<T>) -> Option<&T> {
        self.store.unwrap(value)
    }
}

use crate::collections::arena::ArenaKey;
use core::{fmt, marker::PhantomData};

/// A handle for a stored owned entity.
pub trait Handle: Copy {
    /// The raw representation of the handle.
    type Raw: ArenaKey;
    /// The store owned entity type of the handle.
    type Entity;
    /// The owner associating reference.
    type Owned<T>;

    /// Returns a shared reference to the raw handle to the store owned entity.
    fn as_raw(&self) -> &Self::Owned<RawHandle<Self>>;

    /// Creates `Self` from its raw representation.
    fn from_raw(raw: Self::Owned<RawHandle<Self>>) -> Self;
}

/// A raw handle with an associated handle type.
pub struct RawHandle<T: Handle> {
    /// The raw underlying handle.
    raw: <T as Handle>::Raw,
    /// Marker to signal the associated handle type.
    marker: PhantomData<T>,
}

impl<T: Handle> RawHandle<T> {
    /// Creates a new [`RawHandle`] from the underlying raw representation.]
    // TODO: this is pub for `Nullable<ExternRef,Func>::from_raw_parts`, not sure if really necessary
    #[inline]
    pub(crate) fn new(raw: <T as Handle>::Raw) -> Self {
        Self {
            raw,
            marker: PhantomData,
        }
    }

    /// Returns the raw underlying handle value.
    #[inline]
    pub(crate) fn raw(self) -> <T as Handle>::Raw {
        self.raw
    }
}

impl<T: Handle> ArenaKey for RawHandle<T> {
    #[inline]
    fn into_usize(self) -> usize {
        self.raw.into_usize()
    }

    #[inline]
    fn from_usize(value: usize) -> Option<Self> {
        <<T as Handle>::Raw as ArenaKey>::from_usize(value).map(Self::new)
    }
}

impl<T: Handle> Clone for RawHandle<T> {
    #[inline]
    fn clone(&self) -> Self {
        *self
    }
}
impl<T: Handle> Copy for RawHandle<T> {}

impl<T: Handle> PartialEq for RawHandle<T>
where
    T::Raw: PartialEq,
{
    #[inline]
    fn eq(&self, other: &Self) -> bool {
        self.raw == other.raw
    }
}

impl<T: Handle> Eq for RawHandle<T> where T::Raw: Eq {}

impl<T: Handle> PartialOrd for RawHandle<T>
where
    T::Raw: PartialOrd,

/// Parses the number of instances in the `"coreinstances"` section.
fn coreinstances_count(wasm: &[u8]) -> usize {
    let sections = custom_sections(wasm);
    let (_, payload) = sections
        .iter()
        .find(|(name, _)| name == "coreinstances")
        .expect("must have coreinstances section");
    let mut reader = Reader::new(payload);
    let count = reader.u32() as usize;
    for _ in 0..count {
        assert_eq!(reader.u8(), 0x00);
        let _module_index = reader.u32();
        let memories = reader.u32() as usize;
        for _ in 0..memories {
            let _ = reader.u32();
        }
        let globals = reader.u32() as usize;
        for _ in 0..globals {
            let _ = reader.u32();
        }
    }
    count
}

'''

                Some(next) => next.start().into_inner(),
                None => {

                Some(next) => next.start().into_inner(),
                None => {
                    let frame_end = frame_start.saturating_add(usize::from(cref.len_stack_slots()));
                    frame_end.min(cells.len())
                }
            };
            // Guard against frames that overlap or where the callee starts at or
            // before the caller's operand area.
            let operand_end = operand_end.max(operand_start);
            let operands = cells[operand_start..operand_end]
                .iter()

                .collect();'''

cd /app && cat >> crates/wasmi/tests/integration/coredump.rs <<'EOF'

#[test]
fn coredump_internal_call_chain_no_args() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (func $inner unreachable)
            (func (export "outer") (call $inner))
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let instance = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let func = instance.get_typed_func::<(), ()>(&store, "outer").unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, ()).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("must have coredump");
    assert_eq!(corestack_frame_count(coredump), 2);
    // module funcs: inner=0, outer=1. Youngest->oldest = [0,1].
    assert_eq!(corestack_func_indices(coredump), vec![0, 1]);
}
EOF

#[test]
fn coredump_from_start_function_trap() {

    config.generate_coredump(true);

+/// Creates an [`Error`] for the given `trap_code`, attaching a coredump if enabled.
+pub(crate) fn error_with_coredump(state: &mut VmState, trap_code: TrapCode) -> Error {
+    let mut error = Error::from(trap_code);
+    let config = state.store.inner().engine().config();
+    if config.get_generate_coredump() {
+        let coredump = Coredump::capture(
+            &*state.store,
+            &*state.stack,
+            state.code,
+            config.get_coredump_executable_name(),
+        );
+        error.set_coredump(coredump);
+    }
+    error
+}
+
+/// Extends the coredump of `error` with the frames of the current `state` if any.
+pub(crate) fn extend_error_with_coredump(error: &mut Error, state: &mut VmState) {
+    if let Some(coredump) = error.coredump_mut() {
+        coredump.extend_with_stack(&*state.store, &*state.stack, state.code);
+    }
+}

         }

+    /// Returns the compiled function whose instruction sequence contains the given `ip`, if any.
+    ///
+    /// # Note
+    ///
+    /// This is used for coredump generation to map instruction pointers
+    /// of call frames back to their compiled Wasm functions.
+    pub fn find_func_by_ip<'a>(&'a self, ip: Ip) -> Option<(EngineFunc, CompiledFuncRef<'a>)> {
+        let funcs = self.funcs.lock();
+        for (func, entity) in funcs.iter() {
+            let Some(cref) = entity.get_compiled() else {
+                continue;
+            };
+            let start = cref.ops().as_ptr() as usize;
+            let end = start + cref.ops().len();
+            let ip_addr = ip.as_ptr() as usize;
+            if ip_addr >= start && ip_addr < end {
+                return Some((func, self.adjust_cref_lifetime(cref)));
+            }
+        }
+        None
+    }
+

from pathlib import Path

# ---------- code_map.rs ----------
p = Path('crates/wasmi/src/engine/code_map.rs')
s = p.read_text()
s = s.replace('''use crate::{
    Config,
    Error,
    TrapCode,''', '''use crate::{
    Config,
    ValType,
    Error,
    TrapCode,''')
s = s.replace('''    engine::{ResumableOutOfFuelError, utils::unreachable_unchecked},
    errors::FuelError,''', '''    engine::{ResumableOutOfFuelError, executor::Ip, utils::unreachable_unchecked},
    errors::FuelError,''')
s = s.replace('use alloc::boxed::Box;\n', 'use alloc::{boxed::Box, vec::Vec};\n')

# Add find_func_by_ip after get_compiled (before "Compile `func` or wait")
anchor = "    /// Compile `func` or wait for result if another process already started compilation."
method = '''    /// Returns the compiled function whose instruction sequence contains the given `ip`, if any.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// This is used for coredump generation to map instruction pointers
    /// of call frames back to their compiled Wasm functions.
    pub fn find_func_by_ip<'a>(&'a self, ip: Ip) -> Option<(EngineFunc, CompiledFuncRef<'a>)> {
        let funcs = self.funcs.lock();
        for (func, entity) in funcs.iter() {
            let Some(cref) = entity.get_compiled() else {
                continue;
            };
            let start = cref.ops().as_ptr() as usize;
            let end = start + cref.ops().len();
            let ip_addr = ip.as_ptr() as usize;
            if ip_addr >= start && ip_addr < end {
                return Some((func, self.adjust_cref_lifetime(cref)));
            }
        }
        None
    }

'''
idx = s.find(anchor)
s = s[:idx] + method + s[idx:]

# CompiledFuncEntity fields + new signature
s = s.replace('''    /// This includes stack slots to store the function local constant values,
    /// function parameters, function locals and dynamically used stack slots.
    len_stack_slots: u16,
}''', '''    /// This includes stack slots to store the function local constant values,
    /// function parameters, function locals and dynamically used stack slots.
    len_stack_slots: u16,
    /// The Wasm function index of the compiled function.
    func_index: FuncIdx,
    /// The types of the function parameters and declared local variables.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// This information is required for coredump generation.
    locals: Vec<ValType>,
}''')
s = s.replace('''    /// - If `ops` contains more than `i32::MAX` encoded bytes.
    pub fn new(len_stack_slots: u16, ops: &[u8]) -> Self {''', '''    /// - If `ops` contains more than `i32::MAX` encoded bytes.
    pub fn new(len_stack_slots: u16, ops: &[u8], func_index: FuncIdx, locals: Vec<ValType>) -> Self {''')
s = s.replace('''        Self {
            ops,
            len_stack_slots,
        }
    }
}''', '''        Self {
            ops,
            len_stack_slots,
            func_index,
            locals,
        }
    }
}''')

# CompiledFuncRef fields
s = s.replace('''pub struct CompiledFuncRef<'a> {
    /// The sequence of encoded [`Op`]s of the [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
    ops: Pin<&'a [u8]>,
    /// The number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`] in total.
    len_stack_slots: u16,
}''', '''pub struct CompiledFuncRef<'a> {
    /// The sequence of encoded [`Op`]s of the [`CompiledFuncEntity`].
    ops: Pin<&'a [u8]>,
    /// The number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`] in total.
    len_stack_slots: u16,
    /// The Wasm function index of the compiled function.
    func_index: FuncIdx,
    /// The types of the function parameters and declared local variables.
    locals: &'a [ValType],
}''')
s = s.replace('''        Self {
            ops: func.ops.as_ref(),
            len_stack_slots: func.len_stack_slots,
        }''', '''        Self {
            ops: func.ops.as_ref(),
            len_stack_slots: func.len_stack_slots,
            func_index: func.func_index,
            locals: &func.locals[..],
        }''')
s = s.replace('''    /// Returns the number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn len_stack_slots(&self) -> u16 {
        self.len_stack_slots
    }
}''', '''    /// Returns the number of stack slots used by the [`EngineFunc`].
    #[inline]
    pub fn len_stack_slots(&self) -> u16 {
        self.len_stack_slots
    }

    /// Returns the Wasm function index of the compiled function.
    #[inline]
    pub fn func_index(&self) -> FuncIdx {
        self.func_index
    }

    /// Returns the types of the function parameters and declared local variables.
    #[inline]
    pub fn locals(&self) -> &'a [ValType] {
        self.locals
    }
}''')
p.write_text(s)
print("code_map.rs done")

# ---------- translator/func/locals.rs ----------
p = Path('crates/wasmi/src/engine/translator/func/locals.rs')
s = p.read_text()
s = s.replace('''    /// Returns the type of the local variable at `index` if any.
    ///
    /// # Panics
    ///
    /// If `index` is out of bounds and does not refer to a local in `self`.
    pub fn ty(&self, index: LocalIdx) -> ValType {''', '''    /// Returns the types of all local variables in `self`.
    pub fn tys(&self) -> Vec<ValType> {
        (0..self.len())
            .map(|index| self.ty(LocalIdx::from(index as u32)))
            .collect()
    }

        coredump.extend_with_stack(&*state.store, &*state.stack, state.code);

returncode=0
//! Coredump generation for Wasmi.
//!
//! When coredump generation is enabled via
//! [`Config::generate_coredump`](crate::Config::generate_coredump) and a Wasm
//! trap occurs, the returned [`Error`](crate::Error) carries a serialized
//! coredump that post-mortem debugging tools can load.
//!
//! The coredump is a valid Wasm binary with the following custom sections:
//!
//! - `"core"`: executable name.
//! - `"coremodules"`: module names.
//! - `"coreinstances"`: instances with their memories and globals.
//! - `"corestack"`: stack frames with locals and operand stacks.
//!
//! The binary also contains regular Wasm memory, global and data sections
//! capturing the state of linear memories and globals at trap time.

use super::{
    CodeMap,
    executor::{Inst, Stack},
};
use crate::{
    GlobalType, ValType,
    collections::Map,
    core::{CoreMemoryType, RawVal, ReadAs},
    store::PrunedStore,
};
use alloc::{string::String, vec, vec::Vec};
use core::fmt;

/// A value captured for coredump generation.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub(crate) enum CoredumpValue {
    /// A 32-bit integer value.
    I32(i32),
    /// A 64-bit integer value.
    I64(i64),
    /// A 32-bit float value stored as its raw IEEE 754 bits.
    F32(u32),
    /// A 64-bit float value stored as its raw IEEE 754 bits.
    F64(u64),
    /// A value that could not be recovered.
    Unknown,
}

/// A module instance captured for coredump generation.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub(crate) struct CoredumpInstance {
    /// The raw pointer identity of the Wasm instance used for deduplication.
    key: usize,
    /// The index of the module within the `"coremodules"` section.
    module_index: u32,
    /// Indices into the coredump's memory index space.
    memory_indices: Vec<u32>,
    /// Indices into the coredump's global index space.
    global_indices: Vec<u32>,
}

/// A linear memory captured for coredump generation.
#[derive(Clone)]
pub(crate) struct CoredumpMemory {
    /// The raw pointer identity of the memory used for deduplication.
    key: usize,
    /// The type of the linear memory.
    ty: CoreMemoryType,
    /// The bytes of the linear memory at trap time.
    data: Vec<u8>,
}

impl fmt::Debug for CoredumpMemory {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter) -> fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("CoredumpMemory")
            .field("key", &self.key)
            .field("ty", &self.ty)
            .field("data_len", &self.data.len())
            .finish()
    }
}

/// A global variable captured for coredump generation.
...
165:    pub(crate) fn capture(
184:    pub(crate) fn extend_with_stack(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, stack: &Stack, code: &CodeMap) {
197:    fn capture_frames(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, stack: &Stack, code: &CodeMap) {
265:    fn merge_outer(&mut self, outer: &Coredump) {

[OUTPUT_HEAD]
impl Coredump {
    /// Creates a new empty [`Coredump`] with the given `executable_name`.
    pub(crate) fn new(executable_name: &str) -> Self {
        Self {
            executable_name: executable_name.into(),
            ..Self::default()
        }
    }

    /// Captures the current state of the given `stack` into a new [`Coredump`].
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// - The captured frames are ordered youngest to oldest after serialization.
    /// - The `executable_name` is taken from the engine configuration.
    pub(crate) fn capture(
        store: &PrunedStore,
        stack: &Stack,
        code: &CodeMap,
        executable_name: &str,
    ) -> Coredump {
        let mut dump = Coredump::new(executable_name);
        dump.capture_frames(store, stack, code);
        dump.serialize();
        dump
    }

    /// Extends `self` with the frames of the given `stack`.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// This is used when a host function re-enters Wasm and the inner execution
    /// traps: the coredump generated by the inner invocation is extended with
    /// the frames of the outer Wasm execution levels.
    pub(crate) fn extend_with_stack(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, stack: &Stack, code: &CodeMap) {
        let mut outer = Coredump::new("");
        outer.capture_frames(store, stack, code);
        self.merge_outer(&outer);
        self.serialize();
    }

    /// Returns the serialized coredump bytes.
    pub(crate) fn bytes(&self) -> &[u8] {
        &self.bytes
    }

    /// Captures the frames of the given `stack` into `self`.
    fn capture_frames(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, stack: &Stack, code: &CodeMap) {
        let frames = stack.frames();
        let cells = stack.cells();
        let len_frames = frames.len();
        if len_frames == 0 {
            return;
        }
        // Reconstruct the actual instance of every frame.
        let mut actual_instances = vec![None; len_frames];
        let mut current = stack.current_instance();
        for index in (0..len_frames).rev() {
            actual_instances[index] = current;
            if let Some(prev) = frames[index].instance() {
                current = Some(prev);
            }
        }
        for (index, frame) in frames.iter().enumerate() {
            let Some(instance) = actual_instances[index] else {
                continue;
            };
            let instance_index = self.get_or_add_instance(store, instance);
            let Some((_func, cref)) = code.find_func_by_ip(frame.ip()) else {
                continue;
            };
            let ops = cref.ops();
            let ip_addr = frame.ip().as_ptr() as usize;
            let code_offset = ip_addr.saturating_sub(ops.as_ptr() as usize) as u32;
            let frame_start = frame.start().into_inner();
            let local_tys = cref.locals();
            // Compute the values of all function parameters and declared locals.
            let mut locals = Vec::with_capacity(local_tys.len());
            let mut cell_offset = frame_start;
            for ty in local_tys {
                locals.push(read_cell(cells, cell_offset, *ty));
                cell_offset += cells_for_valtype(*ty);
            }
            // Compute the values on the operand stack.
            let operand_start = cell_offset;
            let operand_end = match frames.get(index + 1) {
                Some(next) => next.start().into_inner(),
                None => {
                    let frame_end = frame_start.saturating_add(usize::from(cref.len_stack_slots()));
                    frame_end.min(cells.len())
                }
            };
            // Guard against frames that overlap or where the callee starts at or
            // before the caller's operand area.
            let operand_end = operand_end.max(operand_start);
            let operands = cells[operand_start..operand_end]
                .iter()
                .map(|_| CoredumpValue::Unknown)
                .collect();
            self.frames.push(CoredumpFrame {
                instance_index,
                func_index: cref.func_index().into_u32(),
                code_offset,
                locals,
                operands,
            });
        }
    }

    /// Merges the frames of an outer execution into `self`.
    ///
    /// # Note
    ///
    /// The frames of the outer execution are older than the frames already
    /// contained in `self` and are therefore prepended to the frame list.
    fn merge_outer(&mut self, outer: &Coredump) {
        let mut instance_remap = vec![0; outer.instances.len()];
        for (index, instance) in outer.instances.iter().enumerate() {
            let new_index = if let Some(index) = self.instance_map.get(&instance.key) {
                *index
            } else {
                let module_index = self.modules.len() as u32;
                self.modules
                    .push(outer.modules[instance.module_index as usize].clone());
                let memory_
[OUTPUT_TAIL]
          });
                self.instance_map.insert(instance.key, new_index);
                new_index
            };
            instance_remap[index] = new_index;
        }
        let mut new_frames = Vec::with_capacity(outer.frames.len() + self.frames.len());
        for frame in &outer.frames {
            let mut frame = frame.clone();
            frame.instance_index = instance_remap[frame.instance_index as usize];
            new_frames.push(frame);
        }
        new_frames.append(&mut self.frames);
        self.frames = new_frames;
    }

    /// Merges a memory from an outer capture into `self` returning its new index.
    fn merge_memory(&mut self, memory: &CoredumpMemory) -> u32 {
        if let Some(index) = self.memory_map.get(&memory.key) {
            return *index;
        }
        let index = self.memories.len() as u32;
        self.memories.push(memory.clone());
        self.memory_map.insert(memory.key, index);
        index
    }

    /// Merges a global from an outer capture into `self` returning its new index.
    fn merge_global(&mut self, global: &CoredumpGlobal) -> u32 {
        if let Some(index) = self.global_map.get(&global.key) {
            return *index;
        }
        let index = self.globals.len() as u32;
        self.globals.push(global.clone());
        self.global_map.insert(global.key, index);
        index
    }

    /// Returns the index of the given `instance`, adding it if necessary.
    fn get_or_add_instance(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, instance: Inst) -> u32 {
        let entity = unsafe { instance.as_ref() };
        let key = store.inner().resolve_instance_index(entity);
        if let Some(index) = self.instance_map.get(&key) {
            return *index;
        }
        let module_index = self.modules.len() as u32;
        self.modules.push(String::new());
        let mut memory_indices = Vec::new();
        let mut memory_index = 0;
        while let Some(memory) = entity.get_memory(memory_index) {
            memory_indices.push(self.get_or_add_memory(store, &memory));
            memory_index += 1;
        }
        let mut global_indices = Vec::new();
        let mut global_index = 0;
        while let Some(global) = entity.get_global(global_index) {
            global_indices.push(self.get_or_add_global(store, &global));
            global_index += 1;
        }
        let index = self.instances.len() as u32;
        self.instances.push(CoredumpInstance {
            key,
            module_index,
            memory_indices,
            global_indices,
        });
        self.instance_map.insert(key, index);
        index
    }

    /// Returns the index of the given `memory`, adding it if necessary.
    fn get_or_add_memory(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, memory: &crate::Memory) -> u32 {
        let core_memory = store.inner().resolve_memory(memory);
        let key = store.inner().resolve_memory_index(memory);
        if let Some(index) = self.memory_map.get(&key) {
            return *index;
        }
        let index = self.memories.len() as u32;
        self.memories.push(CoredumpMemory {
            key,
            ty: core_memory.ty(),
            data: core_memory.data().to_vec(),
        });
        self.memory_map.insert(key, index);
        index
    }

    /// Returns the index of the given `global`, adding it if necessary.
    fn get_or_add_global(&mut self, store: &PrunedStore, global: &crate::Global) -> u32 {
        let core_global = store.inner().resolve_global(global);
        let key = store.inner().resolve_global_index(global);
        if let Some(index) = self.global_map.get(&key) {
            return *index;
        }
        let index = self.globals.len() as u32;
        self.globals.push(CoredumpGlobal {
            key,
            ty: core_global.ty(),
            value: *core_global.get_raw(),
        });
        self.global_map.insert(key, index);
        index
    }

    /// Serializes `self` into the Wasm binary format.
    fn serialize(&mut self) {
        let mut out = vec![0x00, 0x61, 0x73, 0x6D, 0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00];

        // Section: "core"
        let mut payload = Vec::new();
        payload.push(0x00);
        payload.encode_name(&self.executable_name);
        out.encode_custom_section("core", &payload);

        // Section: "coremodules"
        let mut payload = Vec::new();
        payload.encode_u32(self.modules.len() as u32);
        for module in &self.modules {
            payload.push(0x00);
            payload.encode_name(module);
        }
        out.encode_custom_section("coremodules", &payload);

        // Section: "coreinstances"
        let mut payload = Vec::new();
        payload.encode_u32(self.instances.len() as u32);
        for instance in &self.instances {
            payload.push(0x00);
            payload.encode_u32(instance.module_index);
            payload.encode_u32(instance.memory_indices.len() as u32);
            for &memory_index in &instance.memory_indices {

[ARCHIVE ELIDED 695 CHARS]

        for memory in &self.memories {

        }
        out.encode_standard_section(5, &section);

        // Section: global (id 6)
        let mut section = Vec::new();
        section.encode_u32(self.globals.len() as u32);
        for global in &self.globals {
            global.encode(&mut section);
        }
        out.encode_standard_section(6, &section);

        // Section: data (id 11)
        let mut section = Vec::new();
        let mut segments = Vec::new();
        for (index, memory) in self.memories.iter().enumerate() {
            if memory.data.is_empty() {
                continue;
            }
            segments.push((index as u32, &memory.data[..]));
        }
        section.encode_u32(segments.len() as u32);
        for (memory_index, data) in segments {
            if memory_index == 0 {
                section.push(0x00);
            } else {
                section.push(0x01);
                section.encode_u32(memory_index);
            }
            section.push(0x41);
            section.encode_signed_i32(0);
            section.push(0x0B);
            section.encode_u32(data.len() as u32);
            section.extend_from_slice(data);
        }
        out.encode_standard_section(11, &section);

        self.bytes = out;
    }
}

impl CoredumpMemory {
    /// Encodes `self` into the memory section payload.
    fn encode(&self, out: &mut Vec<u8>) {
        let mut flags = if self.ty.maximum().is_some() {
            0x01
        } else {
            0x00
        };
        if self.ty.is_64() {
            flags |= 0x04;
        }
        out.push(flags);
        if self.ty.is_64() {
            out.encode_u64(self.ty.minimum());
            if let Some(maximum) = self.ty.maximum() {
                out.encode_u64(maximum);
            }
        } else {
            out.encode_u32(self.ty.minimum() as u32);
            if let Some(maximum) = self.ty.maximum() {
                out.encode_u32(maximum as u32);
            }
        }
    }
}

impl CoredumpGlobal {
    /// Encodes `self` into the global section payload.
    fn encode(&self, out: &mut Vec<u8>) {
        out.push(valtype_byte(self.ty.content()));
        out.push(if self.ty.mutability().is_mut() {
            0x01
        } else {
            0x00
        });
        match self.ty.content() {
            ValType::I32 => {
                let value: i32 = self.value.read_as();
                out.push(0x41);
                out.encode_signed_i32(value);
                out.push(0x0B);
            }
            ValType::I64 => {
                let value: i64 = self.value.read_as();
                out.push(0x42);
                out.encode_signed_i64(value);
                out.push(0x0B);
            }
            ValType::F32 => {
                let value: f32 = self.value.read_as();
                out.push(0x43);
                out.extend_from_slice(&value.to_bits().to_le_bytes());
                out.push(0x0B);
            }
            ValType::F64 => {
                let value: f64 = self.value.read_as();
                out.push(0x44);
                out.extend_from_slice(&value.to_bits().to_le_bytes());
                out.push(0x0B);
            }
            _ => {
                // Reference and vector values cannot be represented in the
                // coredump global section, emit a zero i32 constant instead.
                out.push(0x41);
                out.encode_signed_i32(0);
                out.push(0x0B);
            }
        }
    }
}

from pathlib import Path
p = Path('crates/wasmi/tests/integration/coredump.rs')
s = p.read_text()

# Add signed LEB reader and a richer locals parser before the last tests
helper = '''
/// Reads a signed LEB128 encoded `i64` from the reader.
fn read_signed_i64(reader: &mut Reader) -> i64 {
    let mut result: i64 = 0;
    let mut shift = 0;
    let mut byte;
    loop {
        byte = reader.u8();
        result |= i64::from(byte & 0x7f) << shift;
        shift += 7;
        if byte & 0x80 == 0 {
            break;
        }
    }
    if shift < 64 && byte & 0x40 != 0 {
        result |= -1i64 << shift;
    }
    result
}

/// Parses the locals of the first frame as raw encoded values.
fn corestack_first_frame_local_values(wasm: &[u8]) -> Vec<(u8, Vec<u8>)> {
    let sections = custom_sections(wasm);
    let (_, payload) = sections
        .iter()
        .find(|(name, _)| name == "corestack")
        .expect("must have corestack section");
    let mut reader = Reader::new(payload);
    assert_eq!(reader.u8(), 0x00);
    let _thread_name = reader.name();
    let count = reader.u32() as usize;
    assert!(count >= 1);
    assert_eq!(reader.u8(), 0x00);
    let _instance_index = reader.u32();
    let _func_index = reader.u32();
    let _code_offset = reader.u32();
    let locals = reader.u32() as usize;
    let mut values = Vec::new();
    for _ in 0..locals {
        let tag = reader.u8();
        match tag {
            0x7F => {
                let _ = read_signed_i64(&mut reader);
                values.push((tag, Vec::new()));
            }
            0x7E => {
                let _ = read_signed_i64(&mut reader);
                values.push((tag, Vec::new()));
            }
            0x7D => {
                let bytes = reader.bytes(4).to_vec();
                values.push((tag, bytes));
            }
            0x7C => {
                let bytes = reader.bytes(8).to_vec();
                values.push((tag, bytes));
            }
            0x01 => values.push((tag, Vec::new())),
            _ => panic!("unexpected value tag: {tag:#x}"),
        }
    }
    values
}

#[test]
fn coredump_encodes_negative_and_float_locals() {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config.generate_coredump(true);
    let engine = Engine::new(&config);
    let mut store = Store::new(&engine, ());
    let module = Module::new(
        &engine,
        r#"
        (module
            (func (export "boom") (param i32 i64 f32 f64)
                unreachable
            )
        )
        "#,
    )
    .unwrap();
    let instance = Linker::new(&engine)
        .instantiate_and_start(&mut store, &module)
        .unwrap();
    let func = instance
        .get_typed_func::<(i32, i64, f32, f64), ()>(&store, "boom")
        .unwrap();
    let error = func.call(&mut store, (-5, -6, 1.5f32.into(), 2.5f64.into())).unwrap_err();
    let coredump = error.coredump().expect("must have coredump");
    let values = corestack_first_frame_local_values(coredump);
    assert_eq!(values.len(), 4);
    // i32, i64, f32, f64 tags
    assert_eq!(values[0].0, 0x7F);
    assert_eq!(values[1].0, 0x7E);
    assert_eq!(values[2].0, 0x7D);
    assert_eq!(values[3].0, 0x7C);
    // f32 1.5 -> bytes 00 00 c0 3f
    assert_eq!(values[2].1, 1.5f32.to_bits().to_le_bytes());
    // f64 2.5 -> bytes 00 00 00 00 00 00 04 40
    assert_eq!(values[3].1, 2.5f64.to_bits().to_le_bytes());
}

STEP 193 TOOL 1
COMMAND:
cd /app && git commit -m "Add opt-in coredump generation to Wasmi

Add support for generating coredumps upon Wasm traps when enabled via
`Config::generate_coredump`. The configured executable name is set via
`Config::coredump_executable_name` and defaults to an empty string.

When a Wasm trap occurs, the returned `Error` carries the coredump bytes
which can be retrieved via `Error::coredump`. The coredump is a valid Wasm
binary containing the `core`, `coremodules`, `coreinstances` and
`corestack` custom sections as well as memory, global and data sections
capturing the runtime state at trap time.

When a host function re-enters Wasm and an inner execution traps, the
coredump of the inner invocation is extended with the frames of all outer
Wasm execution levels, including executions on separate stacks.

This also records the Wasm function index and local variable types on
compiled functions and adds code map lookups by instruction pointer to
support frame reconstruction." && git log --oneline -3

use wasmi::{CompilationMode, Config};
use wasmi_wast::WastRunner;

/// Runs the Wasm test spec identified by the given name.
fn process_wast(path: &'static str, wast: &'static str, config: Config) {
    let mut runner = WastRunner::new(&config);
    if let Err(error) = runner.register_spectest() {
        panic!("{path}: failed to setup Wasm spectest module: {error}");
    }
    if let Err(error) = runner.register_wasmitest() {
        panic!("{path}: failed to setup Wasmi test module: {error:?}");
    }
    if let Err(error) = runner.process_directives(path, wast) {
        panic!("{error:#}")
    }
}

macro_rules! define_test {
    (
        config: $get_config:expr;

        $( $(#[$attr:meta])* fn $test_name:ident($file_name:literal); )*
    ) => {
        $(
            #[test]
            $( #[$attr] )*
            fn $test_name() {
                let name: &'static ::core::primitive::str = ::core::concat!($file_name);
                let file: &'static ::core::primitive::str = self::blobs::$test_name();
                process_wast(name, file, $get_config)
            }
        )*
    };
}

/// Create a [`Config`] for the Wasm MVP feature set.
fn mvp_config() -> Config {
    let mut config = Config::default();
    config
        .compilation_mode(CompilationMode::Eager)
        .wasm_mutable_global(false)
        .wasm_saturating_float_to_int(false)
        .wasm_sign_extension(false)
        .wasm_multi_value(false)
        .wasm_multi_memory(false)
        .wasm_simd(false)
        .wasm_memory64(false);
    config
}

/// If Wasmi's fuel metering is enabled or disabled.
pub enum FuelMetering {
    /// Fuel metering is disabled.
    Disabled,
    /// Fuel metering is enabled.
    Enabled,
}

/// Returns [`Config`] with `parsing_mode` and apply `adjust_config` to its [`Config`].
fn runner_config(adjust_config: impl Fn(&mut Config)) -> Config {
